The standard construction for cocycle twisted and braided tensor product W ∗ -algebras
K. De Commer and J. Krajczok

Abstract
. Given a locally compact quantum group 𝔾 \mathbb{G} and a (generalized) dual unitary 2 2 -cocycle Ω ^ \hat{\Omega} , any W ∗ -algebra A A with a 𝔾 \mathbb{G} -action can be twisted into a new W ∗ -algebra A Ω ^ A_{\hat{\Omega}} with an action by the cocycle twist 𝔾 Ω ^ \mathbb{G}_{\hat{\Omega}} of 𝔾 \mathbb{G} . We show how, in general, the standard space L 2 ​ ( A Ω ^ ) L^{2}(A_{\hat{\Omega}}) , with its standard 𝔾 Ω ^ \mathbb{G}_{\hat{\Omega}} -representation, can be seen as a twist of L 2 ​ ( A ) L^{2}(A) with its standard 𝔾 \mathbb{G} -representation. We then apply this general result in the special case of (generalized) Drinfeld doubles.

Introduction

A
locally compact quantum group

\mathbb{G}=(M,\Delta)

[
KV00
,
KV03
]
consists of a W
∗
-algebra
M
, together with a faithful, normal, unital
*
-homomorphism
\Delta\colon M\rightarrow M\bar{\otimes}M
, the
coproduct
, satisfying
coassociativity
,

(\Delta\otimes\mathrm{id})\Delta=(\mathrm{id}\otimes\Delta)\Delta,

(0.1)

and admitting normal, semi-finite, faithful (nsf) weights
\varphi,\psi\colon M^{+}\rightarrow[0,\infty]
satisfying the left, resp. right invariance condition

\varphi((\omega\otimes\mathrm{id})\Delta(x))=\varphi(x),\quad\psi((\mathrm{id}\otimes\omega)\Delta(x))=\psi(x),\qquad\forall x\in M^{+},\,\textrm{normal states }\omega\textrm{ on }M.

We write
M=L^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})
to guide intuition, and abbreviate LCQG = locally compact quantum group.

Such a LCQG
\mathbb{G}
admits a W
∗
-category
\mathrm{Rep}(\mathbb{G})
of
unitary (left)
\mathbb{G}
-representations
, i.e. couples
(\mathcal{H},U)
with

\mathcal{H}\textrm{ Hilbert space},\quad U\in M\bar{\otimes}\mathcal{B}(\mathcal{H})\textrm{ unitary \emph{corepresentation}:}\qquad(\Delta\otimes\mathrm{id})U=U_{[13]}U_{[23]},

(0.2)

where we use the leg numbering notation
U_{[23]}=1\otimes U
etc. (the brackets are used to shield off the leg numbering from other indices). Morphisms are given by bounded intertwiners,

\mathrm{Mor}((\mathcal{H},U),(\mathcal{H}^{\prime},U^{\prime}))=\{T\in\mathcal{B}(\mathcal{H},\mathcal{H}^{\prime})\mid(1\otimes T)U=U^{\prime}(1\otimes T)\}.

Alternatively,
\mathbb{G}
-representations may be viewed as non-degenerate contractive Hilbert space representations of the universal Banach algebra envelope
\hat{M}_{u}
of the predual
M_{*}
(see
[
Kus01
]
), where
M_{*}
is endowed with the convolution product
\omega*\chi=(\omega\otimes\chi)\Delta
and the smallest norm making

\hat{\pi}_{U}(\omega):=(\omega\otimes\mathrm{id})(U)

(0.3)

contractive for all unitary
\mathbb{G}
-representations. One shows that
\hat{M}_{u}
is in fact a C
∗
-algebra (recall that a Banach algebra can have at most one C
∗
-algebra structure), and the unique bounded extension of (
0.3
) to
\hat{M}_{u}
gives a (functorial) one-to-one correspondence
(\mathcal{H},U)\mapsto(\mathcal{H},\hat{\pi}_{U})
with non-degenerate representations of
\hat{M}_{u}
on
\mathcal{H}
(recall that non-degenerate contractive Hilbert space representations of a C
∗
-algebra are automatically
*
-preserving). This (alternative) construction gives the same C
∗
-algebra
\hat{M}_{u}
as in
[
Kus01
]
. Again, we write
\hat{M}_{u}=C^{*}(\mathbb{G})
(‘universal group C
∗
-algebra’) when this is useful for intuition.

One has that
\mathrm{Rep}(\mathbb{G})
is a tensor W
∗
-category by the tensor structure

(\mathcal{H},U)\otimes_{\mathbb{G}}(\mathcal{H}^{\prime},U^{\prime}):=(\mathcal{H}\otimes\mathcal{H}^{\prime},U\otop U^{\prime}),\qquad U\otop U^{\prime}:=U_{[12]}U_{[13]}^{\prime}.

(0.4)

Alternatively, this tensor product is implemented by the coproduct
\hat{\Delta}_{u}\colon\hat{M}_{u}\rightarrow\operatorname{M}(\hat{M}_{u}\otimes\hat{M}_{u})
into the multiplier C
∗
-algebra of the minimal tensor product, which is uniquely determined by the fact that

\hat{\pi}_{U\otop U^{\prime}}=(\hat{\pi}_{U}\otimes\hat{\pi}_{U^{\prime}})\hat{\Delta}_{u}^{\mathrm{op}}=:(\hat{\pi}_{U}*\hat{\pi}_{U^{\prime}}),\qquad\forall(\mathcal{H},U),(\mathcal{H}^{\prime},U^{\prime})\in\mathrm{Rep}(\mathbb{G}),

where
\hat{\Delta}_{u}^{\mathrm{op}}
is the coproduct with the legs flipped.
1
1

1

The definitions above depend on conventions, which are unfortunately not uniform throughout the literature. We follow here the conventions as in
[
DCKr24
]
for consistency.

There is also a natural notion of a
(left)
\mathbb{G}
-action
on a W
∗
-algebra
A
, defined as a (left) coaction by
(M,\Delta)
: i.e. a faithful, unital, normal
*
-homomorphism
\gamma\colon A\rightarrow M\bar{\otimes}A
satisfying the coaction property

(\Delta\otimes\mathrm{id})\gamma=(\mathrm{id}\otimes\gamma)\gamma.

We refer to
(A,\gamma)
as a
\mathbb{G}
-W
∗
-algebra
. By
[
Vae01
]
*Definition 3.5, any such
\mathbb{G}
-W
∗
-algebra
(A,\gamma)
admits a canonical unitary representation
U_{\gamma}
on its standard Hilbert space
L^{2}(A)
implementing the
\mathbb{G}
-action,

\gamma(a)=U_{\gamma}^{*}(1\otimes a)U_{\gamma},\qquad\forall a\in A.

In this paper, we prove a structural result for standard implementations in the presence of a
quasi-triangular structure
on
\mathbb{G}
. This consists of a unitary
\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{u}\in\operatorname{M}(\hat{M}_{u}\otimes\hat{M}_{u})
which is a
skew bicharacter
, i.e.

(\hat{\Delta}_{u}\otimes\mathrm{id})\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{u}=\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{u[13]}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{u[23]},\quad(\mathrm{id}\otimes\hat{\Delta}_{u})\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{u}=\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{u[13]}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{u[12]},

(0.5)

which moreover satisfies the compatibility property

\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{u}\hat{\Delta}_{u}(x)\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{u}^{*}=\hat{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op}}_{u}(x),\qquad\forall x\in\hat{M}_{u}.

(0.6)

For example, the Drinfeld double of a locally compact quantum group
[
BV05
]
has a natural quasi-triangular structure. A quasi-triangular structure turns
\mathrm{Rep}(\mathbb{G})
into a
unitary braided
tensor W
∗
-category, with braiding

{}^{\mathcal{H}}{\fdiagovrdiag}^{\mathcal{H}^{\prime}}\colon\mathcal{H}\otimes\mathcal{H}^{\prime}\rightarrow\mathcal{H}^{\prime}\otimes\mathcal{H},\qquad{}^{\mathcal{H}}{\fdiagovrdiag}^{\mathcal{H}^{\prime}}:=\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{U^{\prime},U}\circ\Sigma_{\mathcal{H},\mathcal{H}^{\prime}},\qquad\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{U^{\prime},U}:=(\hat{\pi}_{U^{\prime}}\otimes\hat{\pi}_{U})(\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{u}),

where
\Sigma_{\mathcal{H},\mathcal{H}^{\prime}}
is the usual flip map
\mathcal{H}\otimes\mathcal{H}^{\prime}\rightarrow\mathcal{H}^{\prime}\otimes\mathcal{H}
(
[
DCKr24
]
*Proposition 3.4). When it is convenient and
U,U^{\prime}
are clear from the context, we will also write
\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{\mathcal{H}^{\prime},\mathcal{H}}=\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{U^{\prime},U}
.

On the other hand, as shown in
[
DCKr24
]
, a quasi-triangular structure allows to take the
braided tensor product

(A_{1}\overline{\boxtimes}A_{2},\gamma_{1}\bowtie\gamma_{2})
of two
\mathbb{G}
-W
∗
-algebras
(A_{1},\gamma_{1})
and
(A_{2},\gamma_{2})
. This is a W
∗
-algebraic version of the C
∗
-algebraic results in
[
NV10
,
MRW14
,
MRW16
]
(see also
[
Kas15
,
BMRS19
]
, the W
∗
-algebraic results in
[
Hou07
]
for the case
\mathbb{G}=\mathbb{R}
and
[
Moa18
]
for the case
\mathbb{G}

discrete
, and e.g.
[
Maj95
]
for an exposition in the purely algebraic realm). This braided tensor product can be concretely implemented as follows: viewing
A_{i}\subseteq\mathcal{B}(L^{2}(A_{i}))
via the standard representation and putting

\begin{split}\pi_{\boxtimes,1}(a_{1})&:=({}^{L^{2}(A_{2})}{\fdiagovrdiag}^{L^{2}(A_{1})})(1\otimes a_{1})({}^{L^{2}(A_{2})}{\fdiagovrdiag}^{L^{2}(A_{1})})^{*}=\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{L^{2}(A_{1}),L^{2}(A_{2})}(a_{1}\otimes 1)\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{L^{2}(A_{1}),L^{2}(A_{2})}^{*},\\
\pi_{\boxtimes,2}(a_{2})&:=1\otimes a_{2}\end{split}

(0.7)

for
a_{i}\in A_{i}
, define
A_{1}\overline{\boxtimes}A_{2}\subseteq\mathcal{B}(L^{2}(A_{1})\otimes L^{2}(A_{2}))
via

A_{1}\overline{\boxtimes}A_{2}=\overline{\operatorname{span}}^{\sigma\textrm{-weak}}\{\pi_{\boxtimes,1}(a_{1})\pi_{\boxtimes,2}(a_{2})\mid a_{i}\in A_{i}\}.

(0.8)

Then
[
DCKr24
]
*Theorem 4.9 shows that
A_{1}\overline{\boxtimes}A_{2}
is a von Neumann algebra, and by
[
DCKr24
]
*Proposition 6.3 there is a well-defined action
\gamma_{1}\bowtie\gamma_{2}
of
\mathbb{G}
on
A_{1}\overline{\boxtimes}A_{2}
, uniquely defined via

(\gamma_{1}\bowtie\gamma_{2})(\pi_{\boxtimes,i}(a_{i}))=(\mathrm{id}\otimes\pi_{\boxtimes,i})\gamma_{i}(a_{i}),\qquad\forall a_{i}\in A_{i}.

(0.9)

The main result of this paper is the following.

Theorem
.

Let
(\mathbb{G},\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{u})
be a quasi-triangular quantum group, and let
(A_{1},\gamma_{1}),(A_{2},\gamma_{2})
be two
\mathbb{G}
-W
∗
-algebras. Then there is a canonical unitary intertwiner of unitary
\mathbb{G}
-representations

I_{\boxtimes}\colon L^{2}(A_{1}\overline{\boxtimes}A_{2})\cong L^{2}(A_{1})\otimes L^{2}(A_{2}),\qquad U_{\gamma_{1}\bowtie\gamma_{2}}\cong U_{\gamma_{1}}\otop U_{\gamma_{2}}.

This unitary intertwines the standard representation of
A_{1}\overline{\boxtimes}A_{2}
with the identity representation (
0.8
), and satisfies

I_{\boxtimes}J_{A_{1}\overline{\boxtimes}A_{2}}I^{*}_{\boxtimes}=\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{L^{2}(A_{1}),L^{2}(A_{2})}(J_{A_{1}}\otimes J_{A_{2}}),

where
J_{A}\colon L^{2}(A)\rightarrow L^{2}(A)
denotes the modular conjugation of a W
∗
-algebra
A
.

At the moment, it is not clear for us how to characterise the image of the standard positive cone in
L^{2}(A_{1}\overline{\boxtimes}A_{2})
under
I_{\boxtimes}
(for a description of the positive cone in the case of ordinary tensor product, see
[
MT84
]
).

The above main theorem will be a consequence of quite general results on
(generalized) cocycle twists
, which are dealt with in the first four sections:

•

In the
first section
, we introduce the notion of a
linking quantum groupoid
, which is a convenient technical device to deal with generalized unitary
2
-cocycles (i.e.
Galois objects
).

•

In the
second section
, we study representations and actions of linking quantum groupoids and their duals.

The details for the results in these two sections are not spelled out - many of them can be found in
[
DC09
]
(see also
[
BC17
]
), and in general the techniques required in this setting offer nothing new except for a slight adaptation of the results in e.g.
[
KV00
,
Kus01
,
KV03
,
Vae01
]
.

•

In the
third section
, we use the formalism of the first two sections to show how to induce actions on W
∗
-algebras and representations on Hilbert spaces across cocycle twists, and we then show our main abstract result which roughly states that ‘Induction commutes with the standard construction’, see Theorem
3.9
.

•

In the
fourth section
, we specialize these results to the case of (genuine)
2
-cocycle twists, and single out the case of
universally continuous
2
-cocycles
where one does not need to change Hilbert spaces when inducing.

All of the above results depend on robust techniques, with results guided by a very general abstract formalism. However:

•

In the
fifth section
we specialize even further to the setting of generalized Drinfeld double, and prove our main concrete result on the standard representation of braided tensor products, see Theorem
5.11
. Here we need some more delicate machinery to lift certain dual unitary
2
-cocycles to the appropriate universal level. In particular, we will show that the universal C
∗
-algebra of
C_{0}
-functions on a generalized Drinfeld double is given by the maximal tensor product of the universal C
∗
-algebras of
C_{0}
-functions on its components, which as far as we know is a new result – see Proposition
5.16
. We then prove the theorem on quasi-triangular quantum group actions alluded to above.

Conventions

We denote by
[S]
the norm closure of the linear span of a subset
S
of a normed space. We write
[S]^{\sigma\textrm{-weak}}
for the
\sigma
-weak closure of the linear span of
S
, if
S
is a subset of a W
∗
-algebra. The symbol
\odot
stands for the algebraic tensor product of vector spaces. We will use leg numbering notation with flips, so that e.g.
(a\otimes b\otimes c)_{[412]}=b\otimes c\otimes 1\otimes a
.

We denote by
\Sigma\colon\mathcal{H}\otimes\mathcal{G}\rightarrow\mathcal{G}\otimes\mathcal{H}
the flip map between tensor products of Hilbert spaces. We denote by
\mathcal{B}(\mathcal{H})
the W
∗
-algebra of all bounded operators on
\mathcal{H}
, and by
\mathcal{K}(\mathcal{H})
the C
∗
-algebra of all compact operators.

For dual operator spaces
X\subseteq\mathcal{B}(\mathcal{H}),Y\subseteq\mathcal{B}(\mathcal{G})
, we denote by
X\bar{\otimes}Y
the normal spatial tensor product and by
X\bar{\otimes}_{\mathcal{F}}Y
the normal Fubini tensor product
[
ER00
]
*Section 7.2. It is straightforward to check, using
[
ER00
]
*Theorem 7.2.4, that if
X\subseteq A
,
Y\subseteq B
are (possibly off-diagonal) corners in von Neumann algebras
A\subseteq\mathcal{B}(\mathcal{H}),B\subseteq\mathcal{B}(\mathcal{G})
, then
X\bar{\otimes}Y=X\bar{\otimes}_{\mathcal{F}}Y\subseteq A\bar{\otimes}B
. Furthermore,
X\bar{\otimes}Y
is a corner in
A\bar{\otimes}B
. Every dual operator space we use is of this form.

In particular, any Hilbert space equipped with the column or row operator space structure is a corner in a von Neumann algebra
[
ER00
]
*Section 3.4. By default, we equip a Hilbert space
\mathcal{H}
with its column operator space structure
\mathcal{H}=\mathcal{B}(\mathbb{C},\mathcal{H})
, and its dual
\mathcal{H}^{*}
with the row operator space structure
\mathcal{H}^{*}=\mathcal{B}(\mathcal{H},\mathbb{C})
. We also identify
\mathcal{H}^{*}
with its complex conjugate
\overline{\mathcal{H}}
via
\xi^{*}\mapsto\overline{\xi}
. For any von Neumann algebra
A
we have

A\bar{\otimes}\mathcal{H}=A\bar{\otimes}\mathcal{B}(\mathbb{C},\mathcal{H})\subseteq\mathcal{B}(L^{2}(A),L^{2}(A)\otimes\mathcal{H}),\quad A\bar{\otimes}\overline{\mathcal{H}}=A\bar{\otimes}\mathcal{B}(\mathcal{H},\mathbb{C})\subseteq\mathcal{B}(L^{2}(A)\otimes\mathcal{H},L^{2}(A))

and we identify
\xi\in A\bar{\otimes}\mathcal{H}
,
\eta\in A\bar{\otimes}\overline{\mathcal{H}}
with maps
\xi\colon L^{2}(A)\rightarrow L^{2}(A)\otimes\mathcal{H}
,
\eta\colon L^{2}(A)\otimes\mathcal{H}\rightarrow L^{2}(A)
.

If
A
is a C
∗
-algebra, we denote by
\operatorname{M}(A)
its multiplier C
∗
-algebra. If
A,B
are two C
∗
-algebras, we denote by
A\otimes B
their minimal tensor product, and by
A\otimes_{\max}B
their maximal tensor product. If
\pi_{A}
is a non-degenerate
*
-representation of
A
on
\mathcal{H}
, resp.
\pi_{B}
of
B
on
\mathcal{G}
, we write

\pi_{A}\otimes\pi_{B}\colon A\otimes B\rightarrow\mathcal{B}(\mathcal{H}\otimes\mathcal{G}),

while if
\pi_{A}
and
\pi_{B}
are non-degenerate
*
-representations of
A,B
on the
same
Hilbert space
\mathcal{H}
such that
\pi_{A}
and
\pi_{B}
elementwise commute, we put

\pi_{A}\times\pi_{B}\colon A\otimes_{\max}B\rightarrow\mathcal{B}(\mathcal{H}),\qquad a\otimes b\mapsto\pi_{A}(a)\pi_{B}(b).

When
A,B,C,D
are C
∗
-algebras equipped with non-degenerate
*
-homomorphisms
\pi_{A}\colon A\rightarrow\operatorname{M}(C),\pi_{B}\colon B\rightarrow\operatorname{M}(D)
, we put
\pi\otimes_{\max}\pi^{\prime}
for the non-degenerate
*
-homomorphism

\pi\otimes_{\max}\pi^{\prime}\colon A\otimes_{\max}B\rightarrow\operatorname{M}(C\otimes_{\max}D),\qquad a\otimes b\mapsto\pi_{A}(a)\otimes\pi_{B}(b),

the latter interpreted in the obvious way. When
\varepsilon_{A}\colon A\rightarrow\mathbb{C}
, resp.
\varepsilon_{B}\colon B\rightarrow\mathbb{C}
is a non-zero character, we usually abuse notation and write

\varepsilon_{A}\times\pi_{B}=\varepsilon_{A}\otimes_{\max}\pi_{B}\colon A\otimes_{\max}B\rightarrow\operatorname{M}(D),\qquad\pi_{A}\times\varepsilon_{B}=\pi_{A}\otimes_{\max}\varepsilon_{B}\colon A\otimes_{\max}B\rightarrow\operatorname{M}(C).

If
A
is a C
∗
-algebra, we denote by
\mathrm{Rep}(A)
the W
∗
-category of non-degenerate
*
-representations of
A
on Hilbert spaces, together with bounded intertwiners as morphisms.

We refer to
[
Tak02
,
Tak03
,
Haa75
]
for the basics of W
∗
-algebra (= von Neumann algebra) theory. If
A
is a W
∗
-algebra, we denote by
A^{+}
its positive cone, by
A_{*}
its predual and by
A_{*}^{+}
the positive cone of its predual. We write
L^{2}(A)
for its standard Hilbert space with modular conjugation
J_{A}
. We write
\pi_{A}
for the standard representation of
A
on
L^{2}(A)
, and
\rho_{A}
for the standard right representation, so

\rho_{A}(a)=J_{A}\pi_{A}(a)^{*}J_{A},\qquad\forall a\in A.

We drop the notation
\pi_{A}
when there is no ambiguity. If
\varphi
is an nsf weight for
A
, we denote by
\Lambda_{\varphi}
the associated GNS-map into
L^{2}(A)
with domain

\mathscr{N}_{\varphi}=\mathscr{D}(\Lambda_{\varphi})=\{a\in A\mid\varphi(a^{*}a)<\infty\}.

We mainly follow the conventions of
[
KV00
,
KV03
]
for locally compact quantum groups. In the following, we fix a locally compact quantum group
\mathbb{G}=(M,\Delta)
. We index notation with
M
or
\mathbb{G}
whenever this is useful. We further fix a left invariant nsf weight
\varphi
on
M
, and then normalize the right Haar weight
\psi
on
M
so that
\psi=\varphi\circ R
, where
R\colon M\rightarrow M
is the
unitary antipode
of
\mathbb{G}
. We write the standard Hilbert space of
M
as
L^{2}(\mathbb{G})=L^{2}(M)
.

The
left regular unitary
\mathbb{G}
-representation
is given by the unitary
\mathrm{W}
, uniquely determined by

(\omega\otimes\mathrm{id})(\mathrm{W}^{*})\Lambda_{\varphi}(x)=\Lambda_{\varphi}((\omega\otimes\mathrm{id})\Delta(x)),\qquad\forall x\in\mathscr{N}_{\varphi},\,\omega\in\mathcal{B}(L^{2}(\mathbb{G}))_{*}.

Whenever there is a risk of confusion, we will write
\mathrm{W}=\mathrm{W}_{\mathbb{G}}
. We write
\hat{\pi}_{\mathrm{red}}
for the associated non-degenerate representation of
\hat{M}_{u}
on
L^{2}(\mathbb{G})
(the
reducing map
) and put

\hat{M}_{u}=C^{*}(\mathbb{G})=C_{0}^{u}(\hat{\mathbb{G}}),\qquad\hat{M}_{\mathrm{red}}=C^{*}_{\mathrm{red}}(\mathbb{G})=C_{0}(\hat{\mathbb{G}}):=\hat{\pi}_{\mathrm{red}}(\hat{M}_{u}),\qquad\hat{M}=W^{*}(\mathbb{G})=L^{\infty}(\hat{\mathbb{G}})=\hat{M}_{\mathrm{red}}^{\prime\prime}.

Then
\hat{M}
defines the
Pontryagin dual
LCQG

\hat{\mathbb{G}}=(\hat{M},\hat{\Delta}),\qquad\hat{\Delta}(x)=\Sigma\mathrm{W}(x\otimes 1)\mathrm{W}^{*}\Sigma,\qquad\forall x\in\hat{M}.

Having fixed
\varphi=\varphi_{M}
, one can canonically construct a left invariant nsf weight
\hat{\varphi}=\varphi_{\hat{M}}
on
\hat{M}
, together with a GNS-map
\Lambda_{\hat{\varphi}}\colon\mathscr{N}_{\hat{\varphi}}\rightarrow L^{2}(\mathbb{G})
, see
[
KV03
]
. This construction is involutive, and we may hence without ambiguity identify
L^{2}(\mathbb{G})\cong L^{2}(\hat{\mathbb{G}})
in this way. We then have that

\mathrm{W}_{\hat{\mathbb{G}}}=\hat{\mathrm{W}}=\Sigma\mathrm{W}^{*}\Sigma,

and the unitary antipodes of
\mathbb{G}
and
\hat{\mathbb{G}}
are implemented by

R(x)=J_{\hat{M}}x^{*}J_{\hat{M}},\qquad\hat{R}(y)=J_{M}y^{*}J_{M},\qquad\forall x\in M,y\in\hat{M}.

(0.10)

We also recall the formulas

(J_{\hat{M}}\otimes J_{M})\mathrm{W}(J_{\hat{M}}\otimes J_{M})=\mathrm{W}^{*},\qquad(J_{M}\otimes J_{\hat{M}})\hat{\mathrm{W}}(J_{M}\otimes J_{\hat{M}})=\hat{\mathrm{W}}^{*}.

(0.11)

The
scaling constant

\nu_{\mathbb{G}}
is the unique positive number governing the skew-commutation between the unitary modular one-parametergroups associated to
\varphi
and
\hat{\varphi}
, i.e.

\nabla_{\hat{\varphi}}^{it}\nabla_{\varphi}^{is}=\nu_{\mathbb{G}}^{ist}\nabla_{\varphi}^{is}\nabla_{\hat{\varphi}}^{it},\qquad\forall s,t\in\mathbb{R}.

The self-adjoint unitary

u_{\mathbb{G}}=\nu_{\mathbb{G}}^{i/8}J_{M}J_{\hat{M}}=\nu_{\mathbb{G}}^{-i/8}J_{\hat{M}}J_{M}

(0.12)

is referred to as the
fundamental symmetry
.

1.
Monoidal equivalence and cocycle twists

1.1.
W
∗
-Morita equivalence of W
∗
-algebras

We briefly recall Morita equivalence theory for W
∗
-algebras. As to have our notation compatible already with structures as they will appear later on, we will use ‘dual’ notation, so e.g.
\hat{M}
will at the moment denote just an arbitrary W
∗
-algebra.

Definition 1.1

(
[
Rie74
]
)
.

Let
\hat{P},\hat{M}
be two W
∗
-algebras. We call
\hat{P}
and
\hat{M}

W
∗
-Morita equivalent
if there exists a W
∗
-algebra
\hat{Q}
with self-adjoint projection
e\in\hat{Q}
such that

\hat{P}\cong e\hat{Q}e,\qquad\hat{M}\cong e^{\perp}\hat{Q}e^{\perp},

and such that both
e
and
e^{\perp}
are
full
, i.e.
\hat{Q}e\hat{Q}
and
\hat{Q}e^{\perp}\hat{Q}
are
\sigma
-weakly dense in
\hat{Q}
.

We call
(\hat{Q},e)
a
linking W
∗
-algebra
, or more specifically a linking W
∗
-algebra between
\hat{M}
and
\hat{P}
.

We often simply identify
\hat{P}=e\hat{Q}e
and
\hat{M}=e^{\perp}\hat{Q}e^{\perp}
, and then identify
\hat{Q}
with the space of
2
-by-
2
-matrices

\hat{Q}=\begin{pmatrix}\hat{P}&\hat{N}\\
\hat{O}&\hat{M}\end{pmatrix},\qquad\hat{N}=e\hat{Q}e^{\perp},\quad\hat{O}=e^{\perp}\hat{Q}e.

The fullness condition is equivalent to the condition that

[\hat{N}\hat{O}]^{\sigma\textrm{-weak}}=\hat{P},\qquad[\hat{O}\hat{N}]^{\sigma\textrm{-weak}}=\hat{M}.

It is also convenient to label
e_{1}:=e,e_{2}:=e^{\perp}
and to put

\hat{Q}=\begin{pmatrix}\hat{Q}_{11}&\hat{Q}_{12}\\
\hat{Q}_{21}&\hat{Q}_{22}\end{pmatrix}.

Then we can identify
L^{2}(\hat{Q})=\bigoplus_{i,j=1}^{2}L^{2}(\hat{Q}_{ij})
(orthogonal direct sum), where we write

L^{2}(\hat{Q}_{ij})=\pi_{\hat{Q}}(e_{i})\rho_{\hat{Q}}(e_{j})L^{2}(\hat{Q}),

and the standard representation
\pi_{\hat{Q}}
and anti-representation
\rho_{\hat{Q}}
split as

\begin{split}\hat{\pi}_{ij}^{k}\colon\hat{Q}_{ij}\rightarrow\mathcal{B}(L^{2}(\hat{Q}_{jk}),L^{2}(\hat{Q}_{ik})),&\quad x\mapsto\pi_{\hat{Q}}(x)_{\mid L^{2}(\hat{Q}_{jk})},\\
\hat{\rho}_{ij}^{k}\colon\hat{Q}_{ij}\rightarrow\mathcal{B}(L^{2}(\hat{Q}_{ki}),L^{2}(\hat{Q}_{kj})),&\quad x\mapsto\rho_{\hat{Q}}(x)_{\mid L^{2}(\hat{Q}_{ik})}.\end{split}

We also write

\hat{J}_{ij}\colon L^{2}(\hat{Q}_{ij})\rightarrow L^{2}(\hat{Q}_{ji}),\qquad\xi\mapsto J_{\hat{Q}}\xi.

Then
(L^{2}(\hat{Q}_{ii}),\hat{\pi}_{ii}^{i},\hat{J}_{ii})
can be identified with the standard representation and modular conjugation of
\hat{Q}_{ii}
(and with positive cone the projection of the positive cone of
L^{2}(\hat{Q})
).

An alternative viewpoint for W
∗
-Morita equivalence is to have a
W
∗
-Morita correspondence
, that is, a Hilbert space
\mathcal{H}
with a faithful, normal, unital
*
-representation
\hat{\pi}
of
\hat{P}
, resp. anti-
*
-representation
\hat{\rho}
of
\hat{M}
, such that

\hat{\pi}(\hat{P})=\hat{\rho}(\hat{M})^{\prime}.

For example, if
\hat{Q}
is a linking W
∗
-algebra, then fullness of
e_{1},e_{2}
implies that
\mathcal{H}=L^{2}(\hat{Q}_{12})
is a W
∗
-Morita equivalence between
\hat{M}
and
\hat{P}
via
\hat{\pi}_{11}^{2}
and
\hat{\rho}_{22}^{1}
. Conversely, from a W
∗
-Morita correspondence
\mathcal{H}
we construct a linking W
∗
-algebra

\hat{Q}:=\begin{pmatrix}\hat{P}&\hat{N}\\
\hat{O}&\hat{M}\end{pmatrix}\subseteq\mathcal{B}\begin{pmatrix}\mathcal{H}\\
L^{2}(\hat{M})\end{pmatrix},

where we identify
\hat{P}\cong\hat{\pi}(\hat{P})
and

\hat{N}=\{x\colon L^{2}(\hat{M})\rightarrow\mathcal{H}\mid x\rho_{\hat{M}}(y)=\hat{\rho}(y)x\textrm{ for all }y\in\hat{M}\},\qquad\hat{O}=\{x^{*}\mid x\in\hat{N}\}.

Moreover, we have a convenient model for
L^{2}(\hat{Q})
through the unique unitary such that

\begin{pmatrix}L^{2}(\hat{P})&L^{2}(\hat{N})\\
L^{2}(\hat{O})&L^{2}(\hat{M})\end{pmatrix}\cong\begin{pmatrix}\hat{N}\bar{\otimes}_{\hat{M}}\overline{\mathcal{H}}&\mathcal{H}\\
\overline{\mathcal{H}}&L^{2}(\hat{M})\end{pmatrix},\qquad\begin{pmatrix}\pi_{\hat{Q}}(x)\hat{J}_{12}\pi_{\hat{Q}}(w)\xi&\pi_{\hat{Q}}(y)\eta\\
\hat{J}_{12}\pi_{\hat{Q}}(z)\zeta&\vartheta\end{pmatrix}\mapsto\begin{pmatrix}x\otimes\overline{w\xi}&y\eta\\
\overline{z\zeta}&\vartheta\end{pmatrix},

(1.1)

where
x,y,w,z\in\hat{N}
and
\xi,\eta,\zeta,\vartheta\in L^{2}(\hat{M})
, and where
\hat{N}\bar{\otimes}_{\hat{M}}\overline{\mathcal{H}}
is the separation-completion of the algebraic tensor product
\hat{N}\odot\overline{\mathcal{H}}
under the scalar product

\langle x\otimes\overline{\xi},y\otimes\overline{\eta}\rangle=\langle\eta,\hat{\rho}(x^{*}y)\xi\rangle,\qquad\forall\xi,\eta\in\mathcal{H},x,y\in\hat{N}.

In this model, the left regular representation of
\hat{Q}
and its modular conjugation are determined by

\pi_{\hat{Q}}\begin{pmatrix}a&b\\
c&d\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}x\otimes\overline{\xi}&\eta\\
\overline{\zeta}&\vartheta\end{pmatrix}=\begin{pmatrix}ax\otimes\overline{\xi}+b\otimes\overline{\zeta}&a\eta+b\vartheta\\
\overline{\hat{\rho}(cx)^{*}\xi}+\overline{\hat{\rho}(d)^{*}\zeta}&c\eta+d\vartheta\end{pmatrix}

(1.2)

when
a\in\hat{P},b,x\in\hat{N},c\in\hat{O},d\in\hat{M},\xi,\eta,\zeta\in\mathcal{H},\vartheta\in L^{2}(\hat{M})
and

J_{\hat{Q}}\begin{pmatrix}x\otimes\overline{y\xi}&\eta\\
\overline{\zeta}&\vartheta\end{pmatrix}=\begin{pmatrix}y\otimes\overline{xJ_{\hat{M}}\xi}&\zeta\\
\overline{\eta}&J_{\hat{M}}\vartheta\end{pmatrix}

(1.3)

when
x,y\in\hat{N},\eta,\zeta\in\mathcal{H},\xi,\vartheta\in L^{2}(\hat{M})
.

1.2.
W
∗
-Morita equivalence of W
∗
-bialgebras and linking quantum groupoids

A
W
∗
-bialgebra
is a W
∗
-algebra
\hat{M}
, equipped with a coassociative, faithful, unital, normal
*
-homomorphism
\hat{\Delta}\colon\hat{M}\rightarrow\hat{M}\bar{\otimes}\hat{M}
.

The following notion is introduced in
[
DC11a
]
.

Definition 1.2
.

Let
(\hat{M},\Delta_{\hat{M}})
and
(\hat{P},\Delta_{\hat{P}})
be W
∗
-bialgebras. We say that
(\hat{M},\Delta_{\hat{M}})
and
(\hat{P},\Delta_{\hat{P}})
are
comonoidally W
∗
-Morita equivalent
if there exists a linking W
∗
-algebra
(\hat{Q},e)
with

\hat{Q}=\begin{pmatrix}\hat{P}&\hat{N}\\
\hat{O}&\hat{M}\end{pmatrix}=\begin{pmatrix}\hat{Q}_{11}&\hat{Q}_{12}\\
\hat{Q}_{21}&\hat{Q}_{22}\end{pmatrix},

together with a coassociative, faithful, (non-unital) normal
*
-homomorphism
\Delta_{\hat{Q}}\colon\hat{Q}\rightarrow\hat{Q}\bar{\otimes}\hat{Q}
such that

\Delta_{\hat{Q}}(e)=e\otimes e,\quad\Delta_{\hat{Q}}(e^{\perp})=e^{\perp}\otimes e^{\perp},\qquad(\Delta_{\hat{Q}})_{\mid\hat{M}}=\Delta_{\hat{M}},\quad(\Delta_{\hat{Q}})_{\mid\hat{P}}=\Delta_{\hat{P}}.

We say that
(\hat{Q},\Delta_{\hat{Q}})
is a concrete implementation of the comonoidal W
∗
-Morita equivalence.

In the above setting,
\Delta_{\hat{Q}}
restricts to the off-diagonal corners as

\Delta_{\hat{N}}=\hat{\Delta}_{12}\colon\hat{N}\rightarrow\hat{N}\bar{\otimes}\hat{N},\qquad\Delta_{\hat{O}}=\hat{\Delta}_{21}\colon\hat{O}\rightarrow\hat{O}\bar{\otimes}\hat{O}.

As explained in the introduction, the above tensor products can be seen as appropriate off-diagonal corners of
\hat{Q}\bar{\otimes}\hat{Q}
or as abstract tensor products of dual operator spaces.

Theorem 1.3

(
[
DC11a
]
*Theorem 0.7)
.

Assume
(\hat{M},\Delta_{\hat{M}})
and
(\hat{P},\Delta_{\hat{P}})
are comonoidally W
∗
-Morita equivalent W
∗
-bialgebras. Then
(\hat{M},\Delta_{\hat{M}})
is a LCQG if and only if
(\hat{P},\Delta_{\hat{P}})
is a LCQG.

In the situation of the above theorem, we write
\hat{M}=W^{*}(\mathbb{G})
and
\hat{P}=W^{*}(\mathbb{H})
, and we then call
\mathbb{G}
and
\mathbb{H}

monoidally equivalent
(see Theorem
3.6
for the reason of this nomenclature). We then write also

\hat{Q}=W^{*}(\mathscr{G})=\begin{pmatrix}W^{*}(\mathbb{H})&W^{*}(\mathbb{X})\\
W^{*}(\mathbb{Y})&W^{*}(\mathbb{G})\end{pmatrix},\qquad\hat{\mathscr{G}}=(\hat{Q},e,\Delta_{\hat{Q}}).

Definition 1.4
.

We refer to the symbol
\mathscr{G}
as above a
linking quantum groupoid
between
\mathbb{G}
and
\mathbb{H}
.

See Section
1.3
for more information on the precise structure be attached to
\mathscr{G}
so that the notation is consistent with the one for LCQGs.

Remark 1.5
.

Let
\mathscr{G}
be a linking quantum groupoid corresponding to
\hat{\mathscr{G}}=(\hat{Q},e,\Delta_{\hat{Q}})
. Then the triple
(\hat{Q},e^{\perp},\Delta_{\hat{Q}})
(with the role of
e
and
e^{\perp}
swapped) also defines a linking quantum groupoid. It will be called the
reflection
of
\mathscr{G}
. Reflecting is a useful technical tool when we want to exchange the roles of
\mathbb{G}
and
\mathbb{H}
.

One can interpret
\mathscr{G}
as a quantum groupoid with two (classical) objects having
\mathbb{G}
and
\mathbb{H}
as their isotropy. As discussed at the end of
[
DC11a
]
*Section 5, one can view
(W^{*}(\mathscr{G}),\Delta_{\hat{Q}})
more concretely as an instance of a
measured quantum groupoid
in the sense of
[
Les07
]
. However, as they are quantum groupoids of a simple type, they can be handled in a manner similar to quantum groups themselves, with the necessary generalisation only concerning the manipulation of the algebraic structure, and less so the analytic structure.

One such change concerns the multiplicative unitaries, which will in this setting be multiplicative
partial isometries
: endowing
\hat{Q}
with the weight
2
2

2

There is in general no canonical normalisation of
\varphi_{\hat{P}}
in terms of
\varphi_{\hat{M}}
, so one simply makes a choice here.

\varphi_{\hat{Q}}=\varphi_{\hat{P}}\oplus\varphi_{\hat{M}}
, we construct the partial isometry

\mathrm{W}_{\hat{Q}}^{*}\colon L^{2}(\hat{Q})\otimes L^{2}(\hat{Q})\rightarrow L^{2}(\hat{Q})\otimes L^{2}(\hat{Q}),\quad\Lambda_{\hat{Q}}(x)\otimes\Lambda_{\hat{Q}}(y)\mapsto(\Lambda_{\hat{Q}}\otimes\Lambda_{\hat{Q}})(\Delta_{\hat{Q}}(y)(x\otimes 1)),\quad\;\forall x,y\in\mathscr{N}_{\varphi_{\hat{Q}}}.

(1.4)

We can split
L^{2}(\hat{Q})=L^{2}(\mathscr{G})=L^{2}(\mathbb{H})\oplus L^{2}(\mathbb{Y})\oplus L^{2}(\mathbb{X})\oplus L^{2}(\mathbb{G})
, where

L^{2}(\mathbb{H})=L^{2}(\hat{P}),\quad L^{2}(\mathbb{Y})=L^{2}(\hat{O}),\quad L^{2}(\mathbb{X})=L^{2}(\hat{N}),\quad L^{2}(\mathbb{G})=L^{2}(\hat{M}).

If we abbreviate

\mathbb{G}_{11}=\mathbb{H},\quad\mathbb{G}_{12}=\mathbb{X},\quad\mathbb{G}_{21}=\mathbb{Y},\quad\mathbb{G}_{22}=\mathbb{G},

then we can state that there exist elements

\hat{\mathrm{W}}_{ij}\in W^{*}(\mathbb{G}_{ji})\bar{\otimes}\mathcal{B}(L^{2}(\mathbb{G}_{ij}))

such that
\mathrm{W}_{\hat{Q}}
splits into unitaries

\hat{\mathrm{W}}_{ij}^{k}=(\hat{\pi}_{ji}^{k}\otimes\mathrm{id})\hat{W}_{ij}\colon L^{2}(\mathbb{G}_{ik})\otimes L^{2}(\mathbb{G}_{ij})\rightarrow L^{2}(\mathbb{G}_{jk})\otimes L^{2}(\mathbb{G}_{ij}),\qquad\xi\mapsto\mathrm{W}_{\hat{Q}}\xi.

(1.5)

See
[
DC09
]
*Chapter 11 for a detailed discussion, where also the contents of the next section are elaborated on.

1.3.
Dual of a linking quantum groupoid

Definition 1.6
.

Let
\mathscr{G}
be a linking quantum groupoid. Its associated
function W
∗
-algebra
is

Q=L^{\infty}(\mathscr{G}):=[(\omega\otimes\mathrm{id})\mathrm{W}_{\hat{Q}}\mid\omega\in\hat{Q}_{*}]^{\sigma\textrm{-weak}}\subseteq\mathcal{B}(L^{2}(\mathscr{G})).

One shows that
Q
is indeed a W
∗
-algebra. The decomposition of (
1.5
) then shows that
L^{\infty}(\mathscr{G})
is naturally a direct sum W
∗
-algebra

L^{\infty}(\mathscr{G})=L^{\infty}(\mathbb{H})\oplus L^{\infty}(\mathbb{Y})\oplus L^{\infty}(\mathbb{X})\oplus L^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})\subseteq\mathcal{B}(L^{2}(\mathbb{H})\oplus L^{2}(\mathbb{Y})\oplus L^{2}(\mathbb{X})\oplus L^{2}(\mathbb{G})).

If we put

L^{\infty}(\mathbb{G}_{11})=L^{\infty}(\mathbb{H}),\quad L^{\infty}(\mathbb{G}_{21})=L^{\infty}(\mathbb{Y}),\quad L^{\infty}(\mathbb{G}_{12})=L^{\infty}(\mathbb{X}),\quad L^{\infty}(\mathbb{G}_{22})=L^{\infty}(\mathbb{G}),

then
1_{ij}=\pi_{\hat{Q}}(e_{i})\rho_{\hat{Q}}(e_{j})
is the unit of
L^{\infty}(\mathbb{G}_{ij})
.

These components of
L^{\infty}(\mathscr{G})
are endowed with faithful, normal, unital
*
-homomorphisms

\Delta_{ij}^{k}\colon L^{\infty}(\mathbb{G}_{ij})\rightarrow L^{\infty}(\mathbb{G}_{ik})\bar{\otimes}L^{\infty}(\mathbb{G}_{kj})

such that

\Sigma\mathrm{W}_{\hat{Q}}(x\otimes 1)\mathrm{W}_{\hat{Q}}^{*}\Sigma=\Delta_{ij}^{1}(x)+\Delta_{ij}^{2}(x),\qquad\forall x\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{G}_{ij}).

These maps
\Delta_{ij}^{k}
satisfy the joint coassociativity condition

(\Delta_{i,k}^{\ell}\otimes\mathrm{id})\Delta_{i,j}^{k}=(\mathrm{id}\otimes\Delta^{k}_{\ell,j})\Delta_{i,j}^{\ell},\qquad\forall i,j,k,\ell\in\{1,2\}.

If we endow
Q=L^{\infty}(\mathscr{G})
with the coassociative, faithful, (non-unital) normal
*
-homomorphism

\Delta_{Q}(x)=\Delta_{ij}^{1}(x)+\Delta_{ij}^{2}(x),\qquad\forall x\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{G}_{ij}),

then

\mathscr{G}=(L^{\infty}(\mathscr{G}),\Delta_{Q})=(L^{\infty}(\mathscr{G}),\Delta_{Q},\{1_{ij}\})

(1.6)

is again a measured quantum groupoid (we add the units
\{1_{ij}\}
to the notation if we want to emphasize it as a
linking
quantum groupoid). In fact,
(L^{\infty}(\mathscr{G}),\Delta_{Q})
is the dual of
(\hat{Q},\Delta_{\hat{Q}})
in the sense of measured quantum groupoids. We also have on each of the
L^{\infty}(\mathbb{G}_{ij})
two nsf weights
\varphi_{ij}
and
\psi_{ij}
, satisfying

\begin{split}\varphi_{kj}((\omega_{ik}\otimes\mathrm{id})\Delta_{ij}^{k}(x))&=\varphi_{ij}(x),\qquad\forall x\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{G}_{ij})^{+},\,\textrm{normal states }\omega_{ik}\textrm{ on }L^{\infty}(\mathbb{G}_{ik}),\\
\psi_{ik}((\mathrm{id}\otimes\omega_{kj})\Delta_{ij}^{k}(x))&=\psi_{ij}(x),\qquad\forall x\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{G}_{ij})^{+},\,\textrm{normal states }\omega_{kj}\textrm{ on }L^{\infty}(\mathbb{G}_{kj}).\end{split}

These weights are canonically determined once
\varphi_{\hat{M}}
and
\varphi_{\hat{P}}
are fixed, and one can then identify
L^{2}(\hat{Q})\cong L^{2}(Q)
via a concrete GNS-map for the weight
\varphi_{Q}=\bigoplus_{i,j=1}^{2}\varphi_{ij}
, in a manner completely similar to the construction in
[
KV03
]
. In particular,
\varphi_{ii}
and
\psi_{ii}
can be made to coincide with the left and right invariant nsf weights on
(Q_{ii},\Delta_{ii}^{i})
, and the identification
L^{2}(\hat{Q}_{ii})\cong L^{2}(Q_{ii})
is the usual one from the theory of locally compact quantum groups.

Clearly
\hat{\mathrm{W}}^{k}_{ij}\in\mathcal{B}(L^{2}(\mathbb{G}_{ik}),L^{2}(\mathbb{G}_{jk}))\bar{\otimes}L^{\infty}(\mathbb{G}_{ij})
. Using
[
DC09
]
*Lemma 11.1.5.(3) one can check that

(\mathrm{id}\otimes\Delta^{\ell}_{ij})(\hat{\mathrm{W}}^{k}_{ij})=\hat{\mathrm{W}}^{k}_{\ell j[13]}\hat{\mathrm{W}}^{k}_{i\ell[12]}

(1.7)

for all indices
i,j,k,\ell\in\{1,2\}
.

As for locally compact quantum groups, the modular conjugation of
Q
allows to construct a
unitary antipode
for the quantum groupoid W
∗
-algebra
W^{*}(\mathscr{G})
, via

\hat{R}\colon W^{*}(\mathscr{G})\rightarrow W^{*}(\mathscr{G}),\qquad\hat{R}(x)=J_{Q}x^{*}J_{Q},\qquad\forall x\in W^{*}(\mathscr{G}).

(1.8)

With
J_{ij}
denoting the restriction of
J_{Q}
to
L^{2}(\mathbb{G}_{ij})
, we then have that
\hat{R}
restricts to anti-multiplicative
*
-compatible maps

\hat{R}_{ij}\colon W^{*}(\mathbb{G}_{ij})\rightarrow W^{*}(\mathbb{G}_{ji}),\qquad\hat{\pi}_{ji}^{k}(\hat{R}_{ij}(x))=J_{jk}\hat{\pi}_{ij}^{k}(x)^{*}J_{ik},

(1.9)

meaning that for all
i,j,k\in\{1,2\}
we have

\hat{R}_{ik}(xy)=\hat{R}_{jk}(y)\hat{R}_{ij}(x),\quad\hat{R}_{ij}(x)^{*}=\hat{R}_{ji}(x^{*}),\qquad\forall x\in W^{*}(\mathbb{G}_{ij}),y\in W^{*}(\mathbb{G}_{jk}).

(1.10)

We also have the expected anti-comultiplicativity property

\hat{\Delta}_{ji}\circ\hat{R}_{ij}=(\hat{R}_{ij}\otimes\hat{R}_{ij})\hat{\Delta}_{ij}^{\mathrm{op}}.

Finally, we also have C
∗
-algebras associated to
\mathscr{G}
, determined via

C_{0}(\mathscr{G})=[(\omega\otimes\mathrm{id})\mathrm{W}_{\hat{Q}}\mid\omega\in\hat{Q}_{*}]\subseteq L^{\infty}(\mathbb{G}),

which breaks up into components

C_{0}(\mathbb{G}_{ij})=C_{0}(\mathbb{H})\oplus C_{0}(\mathbb{Y})\oplus C_{0}(\mathbb{X})\oplus C_{0}(\mathbb{G})\subseteq\mathcal{B}(L^{2}(\mathbb{H})\oplus L^{2}(\mathbb{Y})\oplus L^{2}(\mathbb{X})\oplus L^{2}(\mathbb{G})).

1.4.
Bi-Galois objects

Let
\mathscr{G}=\begin{pmatrix}\mathbb{H}&\mathbb{X}\\
\mathbb{Y}&\mathbb{G}\end{pmatrix}
be a linking quantum groupoid. Then the particular component

(L^{\infty}(\mathbb{X}),\alpha_{\mathbb{X}}),\qquad\alpha_{\mathbb{X}}=\Delta_{12}^{2}\colon L^{\infty}(\mathbb{X})\rightarrow L^{\infty}(\mathbb{X})\bar{\otimes}L^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})

(1.11)

defines a (right) action of
\mathbb{G}
on
L^{\infty}(\mathbb{X})
. It is called the
Galois object
for
\mathbb{G}
associated to the above quantum groupoid. From
\mathbb{G}
and
\mathbb{X}
one can reconstruct the whole quantum groupoid (up to isomorphism)
[
DC11b
]
, but we will not need this result here. We only observe that
(N,\alpha_{\mathbb{X}})=(L^{\infty}(\mathbb{X}),\alpha_{\mathbb{X}})
is an
ergodic
right
\mathbb{G}
-action: with

N^{\alpha_{\mathbb{X}}}=\{x\in N\mid\alpha_{\mathbb{X}}(x)=x\otimes 1\},

we have
N^{\alpha_{\mathbb{X}}}=\mathbb{C}
. Moreover,
\alpha_{\mathbb{X}}
is
integrable
, in that we have semi-finiteness of the normal, faithful weight

\varphi_{\mathbb{X}}=\varphi_{12}=(\mathrm{id}\otimes\varphi_{M})\alpha_{\mathbb{X}}\colon N^{+}\rightarrow(N^{\alpha_{\mathbb{X}}})_{\mathrm{ext}}^{+}=[0,\infty].

If we also consider on
L^{\infty}(\mathbb{X})
its associated left
\mathbb{H}
-action

\gamma_{\mathbb{X}}=\Delta_{12}^{1}\colon L^{\infty}(\mathbb{X})\rightarrow L^{\infty}(\mathbb{H})\bar{\otimes}L^{\infty}(\mathbb{X}),

(1.12)

then
\alpha_{\mathbb{X}}
and
\gamma_{\mathbb{X}}
commute:

(\gamma_{\mathbb{X}}\otimes\mathrm{id})\alpha_{\mathbb{X}}=(\mathrm{id}\otimes\alpha_{\mathbb{X}})\gamma_{\mathbb{X}}.

We can similarly define on
L^{\infty}(\mathbb{Y})
the commuting left and right actions

\gamma_{\mathbb{Y}}=\Delta_{21}^{2}\colon L^{\infty}(\mathbb{Y})\rightarrow L^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})\bar{\otimes}L^{\infty}(\mathbb{Y}),\quad\alpha_{\mathbb{Y}}=\Delta_{21}^{1}\colon L^{\infty}(\mathbb{Y})\rightarrow L^{\infty}(\mathbb{Y})\bar{\otimes}L^{\infty}(\mathbb{H}).

Definition 1.7
.

The triple
(L^{\infty}(\mathbb{X}),\alpha_{\mathbb{X}},\gamma_{\mathbb{X}})
is referred to as the
biGalois object
implementing the monoidal equivalence between
\mathbb{G}
and
\mathbb{H}
. We call
(L^{\infty}(\mathbb{Y}),\alpha_{\mathbb{Y}},\gamma_{\mathbb{Y}})
the
opposite
biGalois object, implementing the monoidal equivalence between
\mathbb{H}
and
\mathbb{G}
.

1.5.
2
-cocycles and skew bicharacters

As a particular case, consider a W
∗
-bialgebra
(\hat{M},\Delta_{\hat{M}})
and let
\hat{\Omega}\in\hat{M}\bar{\otimes}\hat{M}
be a unitary
2
-cocycle:

\hat{\Omega}_{[12]}(\Delta_{\hat{M}}\otimes\mathrm{id})\hat{\Omega}=\hat{\Omega}_{[23]}(\mathrm{id}\otimes\Delta_{\hat{M}})\hat{\Omega}.

Then we can make on
\hat{P}:=\hat{M}
a new coassociative coproduct

\Delta_{\hat{P}}(x):=\hat{\Omega}\Delta_{\hat{M}}(x)\hat{\Omega}^{*},\qquad\forall x\in\hat{M}.

The W
∗
-bialgebras
(\hat{M},\Delta_{\hat{M}})
and
(\hat{P},\Delta_{\hat{P}})
are comonoidally W
∗
-Morita equivalent via the trivial linking W
∗
-algebra

\hat{Q}=\begin{pmatrix}\hat{M}&\hat{M}\\
\hat{M}&\hat{M}\end{pmatrix}
with coproducts given by

\hat{\Delta}_{11}=\Delta_{\hat{P}},\quad\hat{\Delta}_{22}=\Delta_{\hat{M}},\quad\hat{\Delta}_{12}=\hat{\Omega}\Delta_{\hat{M}}(\cdot),\quad\hat{\Delta}_{21}=\Delta_{\hat{M}}(\cdot)\hat{\Omega}^{*}.

(1.13)

If
\mathbb{G}=(M,\Delta_{M})
is a LCQG, we refer to
\hat{\Omega}
as a
dual unitary
2
-cocycle
for
\mathbb{G}
, and we write

\mathbb{G}_{\hat{\Omega}}=(P,\Delta_{P}).

(1.14)

Specializing further, consider W
∗
-bialgebras
(\hat{M}_{1},\hat{\Delta}_{1}),(\hat{M}_{2},\hat{\Delta}_{2})
together with a
unitary skew bicharacter

\widehat{\mathcal{X}}\in\hat{M}_{1}\bar{\otimes}\hat{M}_{2},\qquad(\hat{\Delta}_{1}\otimes\mathrm{id})\widehat{\mathcal{X}}=\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{[13]}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{[23]},\quad(\mathrm{id}\otimes\hat{\Delta}_{2})\widehat{\mathcal{X}}=\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{[13]}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{[12]}.

(1.15)

Then the tensor product W
∗
-bialgebra
(\hat{M},\Delta_{\hat{M}}):=(\hat{M}_{1}\bar{\otimes}\hat{M}_{2},\hat{\Delta}_{\otimes})
, where

\hat{\Delta}_{\otimes}(x\otimes y)=\hat{\Delta}_{1}(x)_{[13]}\hat{\Delta}_{2}(y)_{[24]},\qquad\forall x\in\hat{M}_{1},y\in\hat{M}_{2},

obtains the unitary
2
-cocycle

\hat{\Omega}=\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{[32]}\in(\hat{M}_{1}\bar{\otimes}\hat{M}_{2})\bar{\otimes}(\hat{M}_{1}\bar{\otimes}\hat{M}_{2}).

(1.16)

If
\hat{\mathbb{G}}_{i}=(\hat{M}_{i},\hat{\Delta}_{i})
for LCQG
\hat{\mathbb{G}}_{i}
, one calls

D_{\widehat{\mathcal{X}}}(\hat{\mathbb{G}}_{1},\hat{\mathbb{G}}_{2}):=(\hat{M},\hat{\Omega}\Delta_{\hat{M}}(-)\hat{\Omega}^{*})

(1.17)

a
generalized Drinfeld double

[
BV05
]
*Section 8. When
\hat{\mathbb{G}}_{1}=\mathbb{G}
and
\hat{\mathbb{G}}_{2}=\hat{\mathbb{G}}
with
\widehat{\mathcal{X}}=\mathrm{W}
,

D(\mathbb{G})=D_{\mathrm{W}}(\hat{\mathbb{G}}_{1},\hat{\mathbb{G}}_{2})

(1.18)

is the
Drinfeld double
of
\mathbb{G}
.

2.
Representations and actions of linking quantum groupoids

Many of the results on locally compact quantum groups extend to linking quantum groupoids. In this section, we give some information on how such extensions look like.

2.1.
Representations of linking quantum groupoids

We refer to
[
DC09
]
*Section 11 and Section 7.6 for details on the next results.

Definition 2.1
.

Let
\mathscr{G}=(Q,\Delta_{Q},\{1_{ij}\})
be a linking quantum groupoid. By a
unitary
\mathscr{G}
-representation
, we mean a pair of Hilbert spaces
\mathcal{H}_{1},\mathcal{H}_{2}
together with four unitaries
U_{ij}\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{G}_{ij})\bar{\otimes}\mathcal{B}(\mathcal{H}_{j},\mathcal{H}_{i})

such that

(\Delta_{ij}^{k}\otimes\mathrm{id})U_{ij}=U_{ik[13]}U_{kj[23]},\qquad\forall i,j,k\in\{1,2\}.

Putting
\mathcal{H}_{1},\mathcal{H}_{2}
together into their direct sum
\mathcal{H}=\begin{pmatrix}\mathcal{H}_{1}\\
\mathcal{H}_{2}\end{pmatrix}
, and denoting by
p_{1},p_{2}
the projections on the subspaces
\mathcal{H}_{1},\mathcal{H}_{2}
, the above condition is equivalent with having a partial isometry
U\in L^{\infty}(\mathscr{G})\bar{\otimes}\mathcal{B}(\mathcal{H})
with

(\Delta_{Q}\otimes\mathrm{id})U=U_{[13]}U_{[23]},\qquad UU^{*}=\sum_{i,j=1}^{2}1_{ij}\otimes p_{i},\quad U^{*}U=\sum_{i,j=1}^{2}1_{ij}\otimes p_{j}.

For the convenience of language, we still refer to
U
as a ‘unitary
\mathscr{G}
-representation’. Modifying the usual argument for LCQG (see e.g. the proof of
[
Wor96
]
*Theorem 1.6.2, making use of the Baaj-Skandalis argument of
[
BS93
]
*Proposition 3.6),
one shows that such unitary
\mathscr{G}
-representations
U
are automatically continuous, i.e.

U\in\operatorname{M}(C_{0}(\mathscr{G})\otimes\mathcal{K}(\mathcal{H})).

We denote by
\mathrm{Rep}(\mathscr{G})
the W
∗
-category of unitary
\mathscr{G}
-representations. A morphism
T\in\operatorname{Mor}(U,U^{\prime})
in
\mathrm{Rep}(\mathscr{G})
is a bounded linear map satisfying
(1\otimes T)U=U^{\prime}(1\otimes T)
. Note that
T
automatically respects the decomposition of
\mathcal{H},\mathcal{H}^{\prime}
into direct sums. The W
∗
-category
\mathrm{Rep}(\mathscr{G})
is monoidal through

\left(\begin{pmatrix}\mathcal{H}_{1}\\
\mathcal{H}_{2}\end{pmatrix},U\right)\otimes_{\mathscr{G}}\left(\begin{pmatrix}\mathcal{H}_{1}^{\prime}\\
\mathcal{H}_{2}^{\prime}\end{pmatrix},U^{\prime}\right)=\left(\begin{pmatrix}\mathcal{H}_{1}\otimes\mathcal{H}_{1}^{\prime}\\
\mathcal{H}_{2}\otimes\mathcal{H}_{2}^{\prime}\end{pmatrix},U\otop U^{\prime}\right),\qquad U\otop U^{\prime}:=U_{[12]}U_{[13]}^{\prime}.

(2.1)

Now
Q_{*}
still has a convolution product
\omega*\chi=(\omega\otimes\chi)\Delta_{Q}
, with any unitary
\mathscr{G}
-representation
U
leading to a contractive representation

\hat{\pi}_{U}\colon Q_{*}\rightarrow\mathcal{B}(\mathcal{H}),\quad\omega\mapsto(\omega\otimes\mathrm{id})(U).

We then have the following extension of the results in
[
Kus01
]
*Section 5:

Theorem 2.2
.

The completion of
Q_{*}
with respect to

\|\omega\|_{u}:=\sup\{\|(\omega\otimes\mathrm{id})(U)\|\mid U\in\mathrm{Rep}(\mathscr{G})\}

is a C
∗
-algebra
\hat{Q}_{u}=C^{*}(\mathscr{G})
. Moreover, any representation of the form
\hat{\pi}_{U}
extends uniquely to a non-degenerate
*
-representation of
C^{*}(\mathscr{G})
, leading to an isomorphism of W
∗
-categories

\mathrm{Rep}(\mathscr{G})\leftrightarrow\mathrm{Rep}(\hat{Q}_{u}),\qquad U\mapsto\hat{\pi}_{U}.

(2.2)

The C
∗
-algebra
\hat{Q}_{u}=C^{*}(\mathscr{G})
splits again as

\hat{Q}_{u}=C^{*}(\mathscr{G})=\begin{pmatrix}\hat{P}_{u}&\hat{N}_{u}\\
\hat{O}_{u}&\hat{M}_{u}\end{pmatrix}=\begin{pmatrix}C^{*}(\mathbb{H})&C^{*}(\mathbb{X})\\
C^{*}(\mathbb{Y})&C^{*}(\mathbb{G})\end{pmatrix}=\begin{pmatrix}C^{*}(\mathbb{G}_{11})&C^{*}(\mathbb{G}_{12})\\
C^{*}(\mathbb{G}_{21})&C^{*}(\mathbb{G}_{22})\end{pmatrix},

(2.3)

where
C^{*}(\mathbb{G})
and
C^{*}(\mathbb{H})
are indeed the universal group C
∗
-algebras for
\mathbb{G}
and
\mathbb{H}
, and with (
2.3
) establishing a C
∗
-Morita equivalence between
C^{*}(\mathbb{G})
and
C^{*}(\mathbb{H})

[
DC09
]
*Theorem 7.6.4.

Note that if
C^{*}(\mathscr{G})
is
*
-represented on a Hilbert space
\mathcal{H}
, then the images of projections
e_{i}
establish a decomposition of
\mathcal{H}
into a direct sum
\mathcal{H}=\mathcal{H}_{1}\oplus\mathcal{H}_{2}
. Furthermore, such a decomposition is preserved by morphisms in
\mathrm{Rep}(C^{*}(\mathscr{G}))
.

There is now moreover a unique (degenerate)
*
-homomorphism

\Delta_{\hat{Q}_{u}}\colon\hat{Q}_{u}\rightarrow\operatorname{M}(\hat{Q}_{u}\otimes\hat{Q}_{u}),\qquad\Delta_{\hat{Q}_{u}}=\begin{pmatrix}\hat{\Delta}_{u,11}&\hat{\Delta}_{u,12}\\
\hat{\Delta}_{u,21}&\hat{\Delta}_{u,22}\end{pmatrix}

such that for all
U,U^{\prime}\in\mathrm{Rep}(\mathscr{G})
we have

(\omega\otimes\mathrm{id})(U_{[12]}U_{[13]}^{\prime})=(\hat{\pi}_{U}\otimes\hat{\pi}_{U^{\prime}})\Delta_{\hat{Q}_{u}}^{\mathrm{op}}(\omega),\qquad\forall\omega\in Q_{*}\subseteq C^{*}(\mathscr{G}).

(2.4)

Considering the strictly continuous extension of
\Delta_{\hat{Q}_{u}}
to
\operatorname{M}(C^{*}(\mathscr{G}))
, and viewing the diagonal units
1_{i}\in\operatorname{M}(C^{*}(\mathbb{G}_{ii}))
as elements
e_{i}\in\operatorname{M}(C^{*}(\mathscr{G}))
, one shows that
\Delta_{\hat{Q}_{u}}(e_{i})=e_{i}\otimes e_{i}
.

For non-degenerate
*
-representations
\hat{\pi},\hat{\pi}^{\prime}\in\mathrm{Rep}(C^{*}(\mathscr{G}))
we then write

\hat{\pi}*\hat{\pi}^{\prime}=\textrm{(co-restriction of) }(\hat{\pi}\otimes\hat{\pi}^{\prime})\Delta_{\hat{Q}_{u}}^{\mathrm{op}},

which is a non-degenerate
*
-representation of
C^{*}(\mathscr{G})
on
\mathcal{H}\otimes_{\mathscr{G}}\mathcal{H}^{\prime}
(see (
2.1
)). Then (
2.4
) can equivalently be written as

\hat{\pi}_{U\otop U^{\prime}}=\hat{\pi}_{U}*\hat{\pi}_{U^{\prime}},\qquad\forall U,U^{\prime}\in\mathrm{Rep}(\mathscr{G}).

In this way, we may naturally view
\mathrm{Rep}(C^{*}(\mathscr{G}))
as a monoidal W
∗
-category (using the opposite coproduct), such that (
2.2
) becomes a monoidal isomorphism.

Also the other results of
[
Kus01
]
have their analogues in this setting. First of all, there is a unique lift of the unitary antipode
R_{\hat{Q}}
to a
*
-preserving, anti-comultiplicative, anti-multiplicative involution

R_{\hat{Q}_{u}}\colon C^{*}(\mathscr{G})\rightarrow C^{*}(\mathscr{G}),

(2.5)

splitting up into maps

\hat{R}_{u,ij}\colon C^{*}(\mathbb{G}_{ij})\rightarrow C^{*}(\mathbb{G}_{ji}).

Secondly, by applying (
2.2
) with respect to a universal
*
-representation of
C^{*}(\mathbb{G})
, we find the existence of a ‘right half-lifted universal’ unitary
\mathscr{G}
-representation

\mathds{W}_{\hat{Q}_{u}[21]}^{*}\in\operatorname{M}(C_{0}(\mathscr{G})\otimes C^{*}(\mathscr{G}))

(2.6)

such that, for any unitary
\mathscr{G}
-representation
U
, we have

(\mathrm{id}\otimes\hat{\pi}_{U})(\mathds{W}_{\hat{Q}_{u}[21]}^{*})=U.

(2.7)

Writing

\pi^{\mathrm{red}}_{\hat{Q}_{u}}=\hat{\pi}_{\mathrm{W}_{\hat{Q}[21]}^{*}}\colon C^{*}(\mathscr{G})\rightarrow W^{*}(\mathscr{G})\subseteq\mathcal{B}(L^{2}(\mathscr{G}))

for the reducing map, the operator
\mathds{W}_{\hat{Q}_{u}}
satisfies

(\mathrm{id}\otimes\pi_{\hat{Q}_{u}}^{\mathrm{red}})\Delta_{\hat{Q}_{u}}(x)=\mathds{W}_{\hat{Q}_{u}}^{*}(1\otimes\pi^{\mathrm{red}}_{\hat{Q}_{u}}(x))\mathds{W}_{\hat{Q}_{u}},\qquad\forall x\in C^{*}(\mathscr{G}).

(2.8)

As before,
\mathds{W}_{\hat{Q}_{u}}
breaks up into pieces

\hat{\mathds{W}}_{ij}\in\operatorname{M}(C^{*}(\mathbb{G}_{ji})\otimes C_{0}(\mathbb{G}_{ij})),

(2.9)

where the two off-diagonal corners may simply be interpreted as corners of the multiplier C
∗
-algebra of
C^{*}(\mathscr{G})\otimes C_{0}(\mathscr{G})
.

Finally, we also have the trivial unitary
\mathscr{G}
-representation given by
\mathcal{H}_{1}=\mathcal{H}_{2}=\mathbb{C}
and
U_{ij}=1_{ij}\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{G}_{ij})
for
i,j\in\{1,2\}
, which acts as the unit for
\otimes_{\mathscr{G}}
. The corresponding
*
-representation of
C^{*}(\mathscr{G})
will give us a non-degenerate
*
-homomorphism

\varepsilon_{\hat{Q}_{u}}\colon\hat{Q}_{u}\rightarrow M_{2}(\mathbb{C}),\qquad\begin{pmatrix}a&b\\
c&d\end{pmatrix}\mapsto\begin{pmatrix}\varepsilon_{\hat{\mathbb{H}}}(a)&\varepsilon_{\hat{\mathbb{X}}}(b)\\
\varepsilon_{\hat{\mathbb{Y}}}(c)&\varepsilon_{\hat{\mathbb{G}}}(d)\end{pmatrix}=\begin{pmatrix}\hat{\varepsilon}_{11}(a)&\hat{\varepsilon}_{12}(b)\\
\hat{\varepsilon}_{21}(c)&\hat{\varepsilon}_{22}(d)\end{pmatrix},

(2.10)

which we refer to as the
counit
for
\hat{\mathscr{G}}
.

2.2.
Actions of linking quantum groupoids

Let us now turn to actions of
\mathscr{G}
on W
∗
-algebras. We refer to
[
DC09
]
*Section 7 and Section 8 for details on the next results (see
[
Cre18
]
for similar results in the C
∗
-algebra setting).

Definition 2.3
.

Let
\mathscr{G}=(Q,\Delta_{Q},\{1_{ij}\})
be a linking quantum groupoid. An
action
of
\mathscr{G}
on a W
∗
-algebra
A
consists of a decomposition
A=A_{1}\oplus A_{2}
as a direct sum W
∗
-algebra, and a (non-unital) faithful, normal
*
-homomorphism
\gamma\colon A\rightarrow Q\bar{\otimes}A
such that, writing
1_{i}
for the unit of
A_{i}
, we have

(\Delta_{Q}\otimes\mathrm{id})\gamma=(\mathrm{id}\otimes\gamma)\gamma,\qquad\gamma(1_{i})=\sum_{j=1}^{2}1_{ij}\otimes 1_{j}.

Again, this definition can be given piecewise by asking that we have faithful, unital, normal
*
-homomorphisms

\gamma_{i}^{j}\colon A_{i}\rightarrow L^{\infty}(\mathbb{G}_{ij})\bar{\otimes}A_{j},

satisfying the piecewise coaction property

(\Delta_{ij}^{k}\otimes\mathrm{id})\gamma_{i}^{j}=(\mathrm{id}\otimes\gamma_{k}^{j})\gamma_{i}^{k},\qquad\forall i,j,k\in\{1,2\}.

There is the following analogue of the crossed product construction.

Definition 2.4
.

Let
\mathscr{G}
be a linking quantum groupoid acting on
A=A_{1}\oplus A_{2}
, and put

L^{2}(\mathscr{G})\underset{\mathbb{C}^{2}}{\otimes}L^{2}(A):=\bigoplus_{i,j=1}^{2}L^{2}(\mathbb{G}_{ij})\otimes L^{2}(A_{j})\subseteq L^{2}(\mathscr{G})\otimes L^{2}(A).

Then the
crossed product
W
∗
-algebra
\mathscr{G}\ltimes A
is defined as

[(\pi_{\hat{Q}}(x)\otimes 1)\gamma(a)\mid x\in\hat{Q},a\in A]^{\sigma\textrm{-weak}}\subseteq\mathcal{B}(L^{2}(\mathscr{G})\underset{\mathbb{C}^{2}}{\otimes}L^{2}(A)).

One shows that
\mathscr{G}\ltimes A
is indeed a W
∗
-algebra. Note that
\hat{Q}\otimes 1\nsubseteq\mathscr{G}\ltimes A
, although we
do
have a normal, faithful copy of
\hat{Q}
inside the crossed product via

x\mapsto(\pi_{\hat{Q}}(x)\otimes 1)\gamma(1).

In fact, writing
\hat{p}_{i}:=(\pi_{\hat{Q}}(e_{i})\otimes 1)\gamma(1)
, we can split

\mathscr{G}\ltimes A=\begin{pmatrix}\mathbb{G}_{11}\ltimes A_{1}&\mathbb{G}_{12}\ltimes A_{2}\\
\mathbb{G}_{21}\ltimes A_{1}&\mathbb{G}_{22}\ltimes A_{2}\end{pmatrix},\qquad\mathbb{G}_{ij}\ltimes A_{j}=\hat{p}_{i}(\mathscr{G}\ltimes A)\hat{p}_{j}.

(2.11)

The defining
*
-representation of
\mathscr{G}\ltimes A
then splits into components

\pi^{\ltimes,k}_{ij}\colon\mathbb{G}_{ij}\ltimes A_{j}\rightarrow\mathcal{B}(L^{2}(\mathbb{G}_{jk}),L^{2}(\mathbb{G}_{ik}))\bar{\otimes}A_{k}\subseteq\mathcal{B}(L^{2}(\mathbb{G}_{jk})\otimes L^{2}(A_{k}),L^{2}(\mathbb{G}_{ik})\otimes L^{2}(A_{k})),

with both
\pi^{\ltimes,k}=\oplus_{i,j}\pi^{\ltimes,k}_{ij}
faithful, normal
*
-representations of
\mathscr{G}\ltimes A
. The faithfulness follows from the (proof of the) next lemma, since a normal
*
-representation of a linking W
∗
-algebra that is faithful on one of the diagonal corners, is faithful on the whole algebra.

Lemma 2.5
.

The W
∗
-algebras
\mathbb{G}_{ii}\ltimes A_{i}
’s are canonically isomorphic with the usual crossed products, and the matrix decomposition (
2.11
) establishes a W
∗
-Morita equivalence between the
\mathbb{G}_{11}\ltimes A_{1}
and
\mathbb{G}_{22}\ltimes A_{2}
through the linking W
∗
-algebra
\mathscr{G}\ltimes A
.

Proof.

The fact that
\mathscr{G}\ltimes A
is a W
∗
-linking algebra follows immediately from the fact that the
e_{i}
’s are full projections for
\hat{Q}
. Furthermore,
\pi_{ii}^{\ltimes,i}(\mathbb{G}_{ii}\ltimes A_{i})
coincides on the nose with the usual LCQG crossed product.
∎

The W
∗
-algebra
\mathscr{G}\ltimes A
comes with a dual right action of

\hat{\mathscr{G}}=(W^{*}(\mathscr{G}),\Delta_{\hat{Q}})
via

\hat{\gamma}\colon\mathscr{G}\ltimes A\rightarrow(\mathscr{G}\ltimes A)\bar{\otimes}\hat{Q},\qquad\gamma(a)\mapsto\gamma(a)\otimes 1,\quad(\pi_{\hat{Q}}(x)\otimes 1)\gamma(1)\mapsto((\pi_{\hat{Q}}\otimes\pi_{\hat{Q}})(\Delta_{\hat{Q}}(x))_{[13]}(\gamma(1)\otimes 1).

For an action of this particular measured quantum groupoid, the lack of unitality for the associated (right) action is implemented by the requirement that the projections
\hat{p}_{i}\in\mathscr{G}\ltimes A
satisfy

\hat{\gamma}(\hat{p}_{i})=\hat{p}_{i}\otimes e_{i}.

2.3.
Canonical unitary implementation of an action of a linking quantum groupoid

The crossed product construction also gives us access to the construction of a canonical unitary implementation of an action of a linking quantum groupoid. This is what is accomplished in the full generality of measured quantum groupoids in
[
Eno10
]
, but let us again spell out the details in our simpler case of linking quantum groupoids, where one can directly mimick the constructions of
[
Vae01
]
.

Let
(A,\gamma)
be a (left)
\mathscr{G}
-W
∗
-algebra. One argues first that there is an nsf operator valued weight

(\mathrm{id}\otimes\mathrm{id}\otimes\varphi_{\hat{Q}})\circ\hat{\gamma}\colon\mathscr{G}\ltimes A\rightarrow\gamma(A)_{\mathrm{ext}}^{+}.

If then
\theta=\theta_{1}\oplus\theta_{2}
is an nsf weight on
A
, we get an induced nsf weight on
\mathscr{G}\ltimes A
by

\widetilde{\theta}\colon z\mapsto\theta(\gamma^{-1}((\mathrm{id}\otimes\mathrm{id}\otimes\varphi_{\hat{Q}})\hat{\gamma}(z))).

We can then endow
L^{2}(\mathscr{G})\underset{\mathbb{C}^{2}}{\otimes}L^{2}(A)
with a GNS map for
\widetilde{\theta}
by closing the linear extension of the map

(\pi_{\hat{Q}}(x)\otimes 1)\gamma(a)\mapsto\Lambda_{\hat{Q}}(x)\otimes\Lambda_{\theta}(a),\qquad\forall x\in\mathscr{N}_{\varphi_{\hat{Q}}}e_{j},1_{j}a\in\mathscr{N}_{\theta},

effectively identifying
L^{2}(\mathscr{G})\underset{\mathbb{C}^{2}}{\otimes}L^{2}(A)
with
L^{2}(\mathscr{G}\ltimes A)
(note that the left hand side elements are zero if
x\in\hat{Q}_{ij}
and
a\in A_{k}
with
k\neq j
).

We can now view the modular conjugation
J_{\mathscr{G}\ltimes A}
as a partial anti-isometry on
L^{2}(\mathscr{G})\otimes L^{2}(A)
with source and range projection equal to the projection on
L^{2}(\mathscr{G})\underset{\mathbb{C}^{2}}{\otimes}L^{2}(A)
, and splitting up into anti-unitaries

J^{\ltimes}_{ij}\colon L^{2}(\mathbb{G}_{ij})\otimes L^{2}(A_{j})\rightarrow L^{2}(\mathbb{G}_{ji})\otimes L^{2}(A_{i}).

Mimicking
[
Vae01
]
, we get the partial isometric implementation of
\gamma
as
3
3

3

Note that our convention on corepresentations is opposite to
[
Vae01
]
, which will result in our formulas being adjoints to the ones in
[
Vae01
]
.

U_{\gamma}=(J_{\hat{Q}}\otimes J_{A})J_{\mathscr{G}\ltimes A},

(2.12)

which is a unitary
\mathscr{G}
-representation with decomposition into unitaries

U_{\gamma,ij}\colon L^{2}(\mathbb{G}_{ij})\otimes L^{2}(A_{j})\rightarrow L^{2}(\mathbb{G}_{ij})\otimes L^{2}(A_{i}),\qquad U_{\gamma,ij}\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{G}_{ij})\bar{\otimes}\mathcal{B}(L^{2}(A_{j}),L^{2}(A_{i})).

It implements the action of
\mathbb{G}
on
A
via

\gamma(a)=U_{\gamma}^{*}(1\otimes a)U_{\gamma},\qquad\forall a\in A.

Let us note that for
i\in\{1,2\}
, the component
\gamma_{i}^{i}
is an action of
\mathbb{G}_{ii}
on
A_{i}
. Furthermore, as the above modular conjugations are compatible with taking projections, the unitary
U_{\gamma,ii}\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{G}_{ii})\bar{\otimes}\mathcal{B}(L^{2}(A_{i}))
will be equal to
U_{\gamma_{i}^{i}}
, the standard implementation of
\gamma^{i}_{i}
.

2.4.
Closed linking quantum subgroupoids

Assume that
(\hat{Q},\Delta_{\hat{Q}})
and
(\hat{Q}_{1},\Delta_{\hat{Q}_{1}})
define two linking quantum groupoids

\hat{Q}=W^{*}(\mathscr{G})=(W^{*}(\mathbb{G}_{ij}))_{i,j=1}^{2},\qquad\hat{Q}_{1}=W^{*}(\mathscr{G}_{1})=(W^{*}(\mathbb{G}_{1,ij}))_{i,j=1}^{2}.

We then say that
\mathscr{G}_{1}
is a
quotient linking quantum groupoid
of
\mathscr{G}
if we have given a
\Delta
-intertwining faithful, normal, unital
*
-homomorphism

\theta=\bigoplus_{i,j=1}^{2}\theta_{ij}\colon L^{\infty}(\mathscr{G}_{1})\rightarrow L^{\infty}(\mathscr{G}),\quad\theta_{ij}\colon L^{\infty}(\mathbb{G}_{1,ij})\rightarrow L^{\infty}(\mathbb{G}_{ij}),\qquad(\theta_{ik}\otimes\theta_{kj})\Delta_{1,ij}^{k}=\Delta_{ij}^{k}\theta_{ij}.

Borrowing terminology of locally compact quantum groups, we also say that
\hat{\mathscr{G}}_{1}
is a
closed quantum subgroupoid
of
\hat{\mathscr{G}}

[
Vae05
,
DKSS12
]
.

By means of the above
\theta_{ij}
’s, we get a surjective
*
-homomorphism

\hat{\theta}\colon C^{*}(\mathscr{G})\rightarrow C^{*}(\mathscr{G}_{1}),\qquad(\mathrm{id}\otimes\omega)(\mathds{W}_{\hat{Q}})\mapsto(\mathrm{id}\otimes\omega\theta)\mathds{W}_{\hat{Q}_{1}},\qquad\forall\omega\in Q_{*},

preserving the matrix decomposition:

\hat{\theta}=\begin{pmatrix}\hat{\theta}_{11}&\hat{\theta}_{12}\\
\hat{\theta}_{21}&\hat{\theta}_{22}\end{pmatrix},\qquad\hat{\theta}_{ij}\colon C^{*}(\mathbb{G}_{ij})\rightarrow C^{*}(\mathbb{G}_{1,ij}).

These maps then also preserve the coproducts:

(\hat{\theta}_{ij}\otimes\hat{\theta}_{ij})\hat{\Delta}_{u,ij}=\hat{\Delta}_{1,u,ij}\hat{\theta}_{ij}.

3.
Induction and standard Hilbert space for twisted actions

3.1.
Induction of actions

Let
\mathbb{G}
be a LCQG, and assume that
\mathbb{G}=\mathbb{G}_{22}
is a corner of a linking quantum groupoid
\mathscr{G}=\begin{pmatrix}\mathbb{G}_{11}&\mathbb{G}_{12}\\
\mathbb{G}_{21}&\mathbb{G}_{22}\end{pmatrix}=\begin{pmatrix}\mathbb{H}&\mathbb{X}\\
\mathbb{Y}&\mathbb{G}\end{pmatrix}
with associated function W
∗
-algebra

Q=L^{\infty}(\mathscr{G})=L^{\infty}(\mathbb{H})\oplus L^{\infty}(\mathbb{Y})\oplus L^{\infty}(\mathbb{X})\oplus L^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})=P\oplus O\oplus N\oplus M.

Let
(A,\gamma)
be a left
\mathbb{G}
-W
∗
-algebra. Then using (
1.11
), we define
4
4

4

Note that
\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}(A)
can be seen as a von Neumann algebraic version of the
cotensor product

N\overline{\square}A
, see
[
HS20
]
*Section 2.2.

\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}(A)=\{z\in N\bar{\otimes}A\mid(\alpha_{\mathbb{X}}\otimes\mathrm{id})z=(\mathrm{id}\otimes\gamma)z\}\subseteq N\bar{\otimes}A.

(3.1)

This is a W
∗
-algebra, which by the weak Fubini slice map property for W
∗
-algebras carries a left
\mathbb{H}
-action

\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}(\gamma)=(\gamma_{\mathbb{X}}\otimes\mathrm{id})_{\mid\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}(A)}\colon\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}(A)\rightarrow L^{\infty}(\mathbb{H})\bar{\otimes}\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}(A),

with
\gamma_{\mathbb{X}}
as in (
1.12
).

Definition 3.1
.

We call
\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}(A)
the
induced W
∗
-algebra
, and
\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}(\gamma)
the
induced
\mathbb{H}
-action
. If
\mathscr{G}
arises from a dual unitary
2
-cocycle
\hat{\Omega}\in\hat{M}\bar{\otimes}\hat{M}
as in (
1.13
), we also write

\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}(A)=A_{\hat{\Omega}}.

(3.2)

The following is
[
DC09
]
*Theorem 8.2.2. Note that the reflected groupoid (see Remark
1.5
) is used to define the second induction.

Theorem 3.2
.

The above correspondence is involutive: there is a natural identification of
\mathbb{G}
-W
∗
-algebras

(A,\gamma)\cong(\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{Y}}(\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}(A)),\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{Y}}(\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}(\gamma))),

provided through co-restricting the normal, unital
*
-homomorphism

A\rightarrow O\bar{\otimes}N\bar{\otimes}A,\quad a\mapsto(\Delta_{22}^{1}\otimes\mathrm{id})\gamma(a).

This theorem can be used to give a one-to-one-correspondence between actions of
\mathbb{G}
and actions of
\mathscr{G}
. Namely, assume that we are given a
\mathbb{G}
-W
∗
-algebra
(A,\gamma)
, put

\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathscr{G}}(A)=A_{1}\oplus A_{2}:=\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}(A)\oplus A.

(3.3)

We endow
\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathscr{G}}(A)
with an action
\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathscr{G}}(\gamma)
of
\mathscr{G}
via the normal, unital
*
-homomorphisms

\gamma_{i}^{j}\colon A_{i}\rightarrow L^{\infty}(\mathbb{G}_{ij})\bar{\otimes}A_{j}

defined as follows:

•

we have
\gamma_{2}^{2}=\gamma
,

•

the map
\gamma_{2}^{1}
is given by

\gamma_{2}^{1}=(\Delta_{22}^{1}\otimes\mathrm{id})\gamma\colon A_{2}\rightarrow L^{\infty}(\mathbb{G}_{21})\bar{\otimes}A_{1},

(3.4)

•

the map
\gamma_{1}^{2}
is just the inclusion map
\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}(A)\subseteq L^{\infty}(\mathbb{G}_{12})\bar{\otimes}A
, and finally

•

the map
\gamma_{1}^{1}
is

\gamma_{1}^{1}=(\Delta_{12}^{1}\otimes\mathrm{id})_{\mid A_{1}}=\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}(\gamma).

Proposition 3.3
.

The assignment
(A,\gamma)\mapsto(\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathscr{G}}(A),\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathscr{G}}(\gamma))
establishes a bijection between isomorphism classes of actions of
\mathbb{G}=\mathbb{G}_{22}
and
\mathscr{G}
.

Proof.

One easily checks that
\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathscr{G}}(\gamma)
is an action of
\mathscr{G}
on the von Neumann algebra
\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathscr{G}}(A)
.

Conversely, assume that
\mathscr{G}
acts with
\gamma
on
A=A_{1}\oplus A_{2}
. Then
\gamma_{1}^{1}
(resp.
\gamma_{2}^{2}
) establishes an action of
\mathbb{G}_{11}
on
A_{1}
(resp.
\mathbb{G}_{22}
on
A_{2}
). We claim that
\gamma_{1}^{2}\colon A_{1}\rightarrow\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}(A_{2})
is a well defined
\mathbb{G}_{11}
-equivariant isomorphism, where
\mathbb{G}_{11}
acts on
\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}(A_{2})
via

\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}(\gamma_{2}^{2})=(\Delta^{1}_{12}\otimes\mathrm{id})_{|\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}(A_{2})}=\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathscr{G}}(\gamma_{2}^{2})_{1}^{1}.

Indeed, observe first that the action condition implies
\gamma^{2}_{1}(A_{1})\subseteq\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}(A_{2})
, thus we can consider
\gamma^{2}_{1}
as an injective map
A_{1}\rightarrow\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}(A_{2})
. Next, as
(\Delta^{1}_{12}\otimes\mathrm{id})\gamma^{2}_{1}=(\mathrm{id}\otimes\gamma^{2}_{1})\gamma^{1}_{1}
, we have that
\gamma^{2}_{1}
is
\mathbb{G}_{11}
-equivariant. Consequently, after taking induction, we get an injective
\mathbb{G}_{22}
-equivariant map

\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{Y}}(A_{1})\ni z\mapsto(\mathrm{id}\otimes\gamma^{2}_{1})z\in\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{Y}}(\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}(A_{2})).

(3.5)

Using Theorem
3.2
we have

(\mathrm{id}\otimes\gamma_{1}^{2})(\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{Y}}(A_{1}))\supseteq(\mathrm{id}\otimes\gamma_{1}^{2})\gamma_{2}^{1}(A_{2})=(\Delta_{22}^{1}\otimes\mathrm{id})\gamma_{2}^{2}(A_{2})=\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{Y}}(\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}(A_{2})),

hence (
3.5
) is an isomorphism. Applying now the induction
\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}
to (
3.5
), we get a chain of isomorphisms

A_{1}\cong\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}(\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{Y}}(A_{1}))\ni z\mapsto(\mathrm{id}\otimes\mathrm{id}\otimes\gamma^{2}_{1})z\in\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}(\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{Y}}(\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}(A_{2})))\cong\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}(A_{2}),

(3.6)

where the first and the third isomorphisms are given by Theorem
3.2
. On elements, (
3.6
) is given by

\begin{split}A_{1}\ni a\mapsto(\Delta^{1}_{22}\otimes\mathrm{id})\gamma_{1}^{1}(a)&\mapsto(\Delta^{1}_{22}\otimes\mathrm{id}\otimes\mathrm{id})(\mathrm{id}\otimes\gamma^{2}_{1})\gamma_{1}^{1}(a)=(\Delta^{1}_{22}\otimes\mathrm{id}\otimes\mathrm{id})(\Delta^{1}_{12}\otimes\mathrm{id})\gamma_{1}^{2}(a)\\
&=(\Delta^{1}_{22}\otimes\mathrm{id}\otimes\mathrm{id})\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}(\gamma^{2}_{2})\gamma^{2}_{1}(a)\mapsto\gamma^{2}_{1}(a),\end{split}

hence it is equal to
\gamma_{1}^{2}
. This proves the claim.

Now, taking the direct sum with the identity, we obtain an isomorphism

\gamma^{2}_{1}\oplus\mathrm{id}\colon A=A_{1}\oplus A_{2}\cong\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}(A_{2})\oplus A_{2}=\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathscr{G}}(A_{2})

of von Neumann algebras; note that it preserves the direct sum decomposition. Next we check that it is
\mathscr{G}
-equivariant, where the right hand side is equipped with action
\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathscr{G}}(\gamma_{2}^{2})
, as explained above. Equivalently, we will check that
\gamma_{i}^{j}
is mapped to
\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathscr{G}}(\gamma_{2}^{2})_{i}^{j}
for
i,j\in\{1,2\}
.

This claim is trivial for
(i,j)=(2,2)
as
\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathscr{G}}(\gamma_{2}^{2})^{2}_{2}=\gamma^{2}_{2}
, and we have already checked it for
(i,j)=(1,1)
. If
(i,j)=(1,2)
then
\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathscr{G}}(\gamma^{2}_{2})_{1}^{2}
is the inclusion map
\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}(A_{2})\rightarrow L^{\infty}(\mathbb{G}_{12})\bar{\otimes}A_{2}
, hence the claim is also trivial. For
(i,j)=(2,1)
we have

(\mathrm{id}\otimes\gamma^{2}_{1})\gamma_{2}^{1}=(\Delta^{1}_{22}\otimes\mathrm{id})\gamma^{2}_{2}=\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathscr{G}}(\gamma_{2}^{2})^{1}_{2}.

∎

3.2.
Induction of representations

Let
\mathscr{G}
be a linking quantum groupoid between the LCQGs
\mathbb{G}
and
\mathbb{H}
.

As a particular case of induction of actions, we can induce unitary left
\mathbb{G}
-representations. Indeed, if
U\in M\bar{\otimes}\mathcal{B}(\mathcal{H})
is a unitary left
\mathbb{G}
-representation, endow
A:=\mathcal{B}\begin{pmatrix}\mathcal{H}\\
\mathbb{C}\end{pmatrix}=\begin{pmatrix}\mathcal{B}(\mathcal{H})&\mathcal{H}\\
\overline{\mathcal{H}}&\mathbb{C}\end{pmatrix}
with the left
\mathbb{G}
-action

\gamma_{U}\colon A\rightarrow M\bar{\otimes}A,\qquad\begin{pmatrix}x&\xi\\
\eta^{*}&\lambda\end{pmatrix}\mapsto\begin{pmatrix}U^{*}(1\otimes x)U&U^{*}(1\otimes\xi)\\
(1\otimes\eta^{*})U&1\otimes\lambda\end{pmatrix},

where we identify
\overline{\mathcal{H}}=\mathcal{H}^{*}
,
M\bar{\otimes}A=\begin{pmatrix}M\bar{\otimes}\mathcal{B}(\mathcal{H})&M\bar{\otimes}\mathcal{H}\\
M\bar{\otimes}\overline{\mathcal{H}}&M\bar{\otimes}\mathbb{C}\end{pmatrix}
and identify elements of
M\bar{\otimes}\mathcal{H}
,
M\bar{\otimes}\overline{\mathcal{H}}
with operators
L^{2}(M)\rightarrow L^{2}(M)\otimes\mathcal{H}
and
L^{2}(M)\otimes\mathcal{H}\rightarrow L^{2}(M)
as explained in the introduction. The induced W
∗
-algebra
\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}(A)
again splits as a matrix algebra, and the
12
-corner can be identified with
5
5

5

Similarly as
\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}(A)
, the space
\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}(\mathcal{H})
can be seen as an analytic version of cotensor product
N\overline{\square}\mathcal{H}
.

\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}(\mathcal{H})=\{\xi\in N\bar{\otimes}\mathcal{H}\mid(\alpha_{\mathbb{X}}\otimes\mathrm{id})\xi=(\mathrm{id}\otimes U^{*})\xi_{[13]}\}\subseteq\mathcal{B}(L^{2}(N),L^{2}(N)\otimes\mathcal{H}).

This is in fact a Hilbert space via

\langle\xi,\eta\rangle=\xi^{*}\eta,\qquad\forall\xi,\eta\in\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}(\mathcal{H}),

using that
(N,\alpha_{\mathbb{X}})
is ergodic. We then obtain an induced unitary
\mathbb{H}
-representation
\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}(U)
on
\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}(\mathcal{H})
via

\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}(U)^{*}(\eta\otimes\xi)=(\gamma_{\mathbb{X}}\otimes\mathrm{id})(\xi)(\eta\otimes 1),\qquad\forall\eta\in L^{2}(\mathbb{H}),\xi\in\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}(\mathcal{H}),

where the right hand side is seen as a map
L^{2}(\mathbb{X})\rightarrow L^{2}(\mathbb{H})\otimes L^{2}(\mathbb{X})\otimes\mathcal{H}
.

Indeed, it is straightforward to check that
\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}(U)^{*}
is a well defined isometry in
L^{\infty}(\mathbb{H})\bar{\otimes}\mathcal{B}(\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}(\mathcal{H}))
. We also have

(\Delta_{\mathbb{H}}\otimes\mathrm{id})(\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}(U)^{*})=\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}(U)^{*}_{[23]}\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}(U)^{*}_{[13]}.

(3.7)

Indeed, for
\eta_{1},\eta_{2}\in L^{2}(\mathbb{H}),\xi\in\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}(\mathcal{H})

\begin{split}&\quad\;(\Delta_{\mathbb{H}}\otimes\mathrm{id})(\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}(U)^{*})(\eta_{1}\otimes\eta_{2}\otimes\xi)=\mathrm{W}^{*}_{\mathbb{H}[12]}\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}(U)^{*}_{[23]}(\mathrm{W}_{\mathbb{H}}(\eta_{1}\otimes\eta_{2})\otimes\xi)\\
&=\mathrm{W}^{*}_{\mathbb{H}[12]}(1\otimes(\gamma_{\mathbb{X}}\otimes\mathrm{id})(\xi))(\mathrm{W}_{\mathbb{H}}(\eta_{1}\otimes\eta_{2})\otimes 1)=((\Delta_{\mathbb{H}}\otimes\mathrm{id})\gamma_{\mathbb{X}}\otimes\mathrm{id})(\xi)(\eta_{1}\otimes\eta_{2}\otimes 1)\\
&=((\mathrm{id}\otimes\gamma_{\mathbb{X}})\gamma_{\mathbb{X}}\otimes\mathrm{id})(\xi)(\eta_{1}\otimes\eta_{2}\otimes 1)\end{split}

and on the other hand

\begin{split}&\quad\;\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}(U)^{*}_{[23]}\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}(U)^{*}_{[13]}(\eta_{1}\otimes\eta_{2}\otimes\xi)=\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}(U)^{*}_{[23]}(\gamma_{\mathbb{X}}\otimes\mathrm{id})(\xi)_{[134]}(\eta_{1}\otimes\eta_{2}\otimes 1)\\
&=((\mathrm{id}\otimes\gamma_{\mathbb{X}})\gamma_{\mathbb{X}}\otimes\mathrm{id})(\xi)(\eta_{1}\otimes\eta_{2}\otimes 1),\end{split}

which proves (
3.7
). Consequently,
[
BDS13
]
*Corollary 4.15 implies that
\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}(U)
is unitary.

Definition 3.4
.

We call
(\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}(\mathcal{H}),\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}(U))\in\mathrm{Rep}(\mathbb{H})
the
induced unitary
\mathbb{H}
-representation
from
(\mathcal{H},U)\in\mathrm{Rep}(\mathbb{G})
.

The above induction can also be performed on the level of
\mathscr{G}
itself. Namely, starting from
(\mathcal{H},U)\in\mathrm{Rep}(\mathbb{G})
, put

\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathscr{G}}(\mathcal{H})=\begin{pmatrix}\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}(\mathcal{H})\\
\mathcal{H}\end{pmatrix},

and define on it the unitary
\mathscr{G}
-representation
\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathscr{G}}(U)
, for whose components (see Definition
2.1
) we use the shorthand notation
U_{ij}
where

U_{22}=U,\qquad U_{11}=\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}(U)

(3.8)

and
U_{12},U_{21}
are defined via

\begin{split}U_{12}^{*}&\colon L^{2}(\mathbb{G}_{12})\otimes\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}(\mathcal{H})\rightarrow L^{2}(\mathbb{G}_{12})\otimes\mathcal{H},\quad\xi\otimes z\mapsto z\xi,\\
U_{21}^{*}&\colon L^{2}(\mathbb{G}_{21})\otimes\mathcal{H}\rightarrow L^{2}(\mathbb{G}_{21})\otimes\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}(\mathcal{H}),\quad\xi\mapsto(\Delta_{22}^{1}\otimes\mathrm{id})(U^{*})\xi_{[13]}.\end{split}

(3.9)

Lemma 3.5
.

\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathscr{G}}(U)
is a unitary representation of
\mathscr{G}
on
\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathscr{G}}(\mathcal{H})
.

Proof.

It is easily checked that
U_{ij}^{*}
is an isometry in
L^{\infty}(\mathbb{G}_{ij})\bar{\otimes}\mathcal{B}(\mathcal{H}_{i},\mathcal{H}_{j})
(where
\mathcal{H}_{1}=\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}(\mathcal{H})
,
\mathcal{H}_{2}=\mathcal{H}
) and
U_{ii}
are unitary. Next we claim that
(\Delta_{ij}^{k}\otimes\mathrm{id})(U_{ij})=U_{ik[13]}U_{kj[23]}
for
i,j,k\in\{1,2\}
, or equivalently

(\Delta_{ij}^{k}\otimes\mathrm{id})(U_{ij}^{*})=U_{kj[23]}^{*}U_{ik[13]}^{*}.

(3.10)

Let us show this for
i=j=1,k=2
. Take
\eta_{1}\in L^{2}(\mathbb{G}_{12})
,
\eta_{2}\in L^{2}(\mathbb{G}_{21})
,
\xi\in\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}(\mathcal{H})
, then

\begin{split}&\quad\;(\Delta^{2}_{11}\otimes\mathrm{id})(U^{*}_{11})(\eta_{1}\otimes\eta_{2}\otimes\xi)=(\Delta^{2}_{11}\otimes\mathrm{id})(\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}(U)^{*})(\eta_{1}\otimes\eta_{2}\otimes\xi)\\
&=\Sigma_{[12]}\hat{\mathrm{W}}^{1}_{12[12]}\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}(U)^{*}_{[13]}\hat{\mathrm{W}}^{1*}_{12[12]}(\eta_{2}\otimes\eta_{1}\otimes\xi)\\
&=\Sigma_{[12]}\hat{\mathrm{W}}^{1}_{12[12]}(\gamma_{\mathbb{X}}\otimes\mathrm{id})(\xi)_{[134]}(\hat{\mathrm{W}}^{1*}_{12}(\eta_{2}\otimes\eta_{1}))\\
&=(\Delta_{11}^{2}\otimes\mathrm{id}\otimes\mathrm{id})(\Delta_{12}^{1}\otimes\mathrm{id})(\xi)(\eta_{1}\otimes\eta_{2})\\
&=(\mathrm{id}\otimes\Delta_{22}^{1}\otimes\mathrm{id})(\Delta_{12}^{2}\otimes\mathrm{id})(\xi)(\eta_{1}\otimes\eta_{2})\\
&=(\mathrm{id}\otimes\Delta_{22}^{1}\otimes\mathrm{id})(\alpha_{\mathbb{X}}\otimes\mathrm{id})(\xi)(\eta_{1}\otimes\eta_{2})\\
&=(\mathrm{id}\otimes\Delta_{22}^{1}\otimes\mathrm{id})((\mathrm{id}\otimes U^{*})\xi_{[13]})(\eta_{1}\otimes\eta_{2})\\
&=(\Delta_{22}^{1}\otimes\mathrm{id})(U^{*})_{[234]}\xi_{[14]}(\eta_{1}\otimes\eta_{2})\end{split}

and on the other hand

\begin{split}&\quad\;U^{*}_{21[23]}U^{*}_{12[13]}(\eta_{1}\otimes\eta_{2}\otimes\xi)=\Sigma_{[12]}U^{*}_{21[13]}U^{*}_{12[23]}(\eta_{2}\otimes\eta_{1}\otimes\xi)=\Sigma_{[12]}U^{*}_{21[13]}(\eta_{2}\otimes\xi\eta_{1})\\
&=\Sigma_{[12]}(\Delta^{1}_{22}\otimes\mathrm{id})(U^{*})_{[134]}(\eta_{2}\otimes\xi\eta_{1})_{[124]}=(\Delta^{1}_{22}\otimes\mathrm{id})(U^{*})_{[234]}(\eta_{2}\otimes\xi\eta_{1})_{[214]},\end{split}

which proves the claim. The remaining equations (
3.10
) can be checked in a similar way.

Since
\Delta_{11}^{2}
is a unital
*
-homomorphism, the left hand side of

(\Delta_{11}^{2}\otimes\mathrm{id})(U_{11})=U_{12[13]}U_{21[23]}

(3.11)

is unitary, in particular it is an isometry. Now, we already know that
U_{12}
is a contraction. If
U_{21}
is not an isometry, we can find
\zeta\in L^{2}(\mathbb{G}_{21})\otimes\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}(\mathcal{H})
such that
\|U_{21}\zeta\|<\|\zeta\|
. For any
0\neq\eta\in L^{2}(\mathbb{G}_{12})
we have

\|\eta\|\,\|\zeta\|=\|(\Delta_{11}^{2}\otimes\mathrm{id})(U_{11})(\eta\otimes\zeta)\|=\|U_{12[13]}U_{21[23]}(\eta\otimes\zeta)\|\leq\|\eta\|\,\|U_{21}\zeta\|<\|\eta\|\,\|\zeta\|

which gives a contradiction. Consequently,
U_{21}
is unitary. Now equation (
3.11
) implies that
U_{12}
is also unitary and consequently
\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathscr{G}}(U)
is indeed a unitary representation.
∎

Theorem 3.6
.

The assignment

\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}\colon\mathrm{Rep}(\mathbb{G})\rightarrow\mathrm{Rep}(\mathbb{H}),\qquad(\mathcal{H},U)\mapsto(\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}(\mathcal{H}),\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}(U)),\quad T\mapsto 1\otimes T

(3.12)

becomes an equivalence of tensor W
∗
-categories through the unique unitaries determined by

u_{\mathcal{H},\mathcal{H}^{\prime}}\colon\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}(\mathcal{H})\otimes\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}(\mathcal{H}^{\prime})\rightarrow\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}(\mathcal{H}\otimes\mathcal{H}^{\prime}),\qquad\xi\otimes\eta\mapsto\eta_{[13]}\xi_{[12]}.

(3.13)

Proof.

Observe that the C
∗
-algebras
C^{*}(\mathbb{G}),C^{*}(\mathscr{G})
are C
∗
-Morita equivalent (
2.3
), hence the C
∗
-algebraic induction gives an equivalence of W
∗
-categories
\mathrm{Ind}^{C^{*}}_{\mathscr{G}}\colon\mathrm{Rep}(C^{*}(\mathbb{G}))\rightarrow\mathrm{Rep}(C^{*}(\mathscr{G}))
(see
[
RW98
]
*Example 3.6, Theorem 3.29). Consider the restriction functor

\mathrm{Res}^{C^{*}}_{\mathscr{G}}\colon\mathrm{Rep}(C^{*}(\mathscr{G}))\rightarrow\mathrm{Rep}(C^{*}(\mathbb{G})),\qquad(\mathcal{H},\hat{\pi})\mapsto(\mathcal{H}_{2},\hat{\pi}_{2}),\quad T_{1}\oplus T_{2}\mapsto T_{2}

with
\mathcal{H}=\mathcal{H}_{1}\oplus\mathcal{H}_{2}
and
\hat{\pi}_{2}
determined through

\begin{pmatrix}0\\
\hat{\pi}_{2}(x)\xi\end{pmatrix}=\hat{\pi}\begin{pmatrix}0&0\\
0&x\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}0\\
\xi\end{pmatrix},\qquad\forall\xi\in\mathcal{H}_{2},x\in C^{*}(\mathbb{G}).

It follows from the concrete description of
\mathrm{Ind}^{C^{*}}_{\mathscr{G}}
that
\mathrm{Res}^{C^{*}}_{\mathscr{G}}\circ\mathrm{Ind}^{C^{*}}_{\mathscr{G}}\cong\mathrm{id}
, in particular
\mathrm{Res}^{C^{*}}_{\mathscr{G}}
is also an equivalence of W
∗
-categories.

This restriction functor is clearly monoidal, through the identity map

\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{Res}(\pi\ast\pi^{\prime})}=\mathcal{H}_{\pi,2}\otimes\mathcal{H}_{\pi^{\prime},2}=\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{Res}(\pi)*\mathrm{Res}(\pi^{\prime})}.

Using the tensor W
∗
-equivalences
\mathrm{Rep}(\mathscr{G})\cong\mathrm{Rep}(C^{*}(\mathscr{G}))
and
\mathrm{Rep}(\mathbb{G})\cong\mathrm{Rep}(C^{*}(\mathbb{G}))
, we then also find the tensor W
∗
-equivalence

\mathrm{Res}_{\mathscr{G}}\colon\mathrm{Rep}(\mathscr{G})\rightarrow\mathrm{Rep}(\mathbb{G}),\qquad(\mathcal{H},U)\mapsto(\mathcal{H}_{2},U_{22}),\quad T_{1}\oplus T_{2}\mapsto T_{2}

the coherence map again being the identity.

Now, consider the induction functor

\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathscr{G}}\colon\mathrm{Rep}(\mathbb{G})\rightarrow\mathrm{Rep}(\mathscr{G}),\quad(\mathcal{H},U)\mapsto(\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathscr{G}}(\mathcal{H}),\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathscr{G}}(U)),\quad T\mapsto(1\otimes T)\oplus T.

Since
\mathrm{Res}_{\mathscr{G}}\circ\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathscr{G}}=\mathrm{id}
, we conclude that
\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathscr{G}}
is an equivalence of W
∗
-categories. This then already shows that (
3.12
) will be an equivalence of W
∗
-categories.

To see that it is monoidal through (
3.13
), we note first that
u_{\mathcal{H},\mathcal{H}^{\prime}}
in (
3.13
) is clearly an isometric map and it is not difficult to check that
u_{\mathcal{H},\mathcal{H}^{\prime}}^{*}
is an intertwiner of unitary
\mathbb{H}
-representations. Furthermore,
u_{\mathcal{H},\mathcal{H}^{\prime}}
lifts to an isometric intertwiner

\widetilde{u}_{\mathcal{H},\mathcal{H}^{\prime}}\colon\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathscr{G}}(\mathcal{H})\otimes_{\mathscr{G}}\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathscr{G}}(\mathcal{H}^{\prime})\rightarrow\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathscr{G}}(\mathcal{H}\otimes_{\mathbb{G}}\mathcal{H}^{\prime}),\qquad\begin{pmatrix}\xi\otimes\eta\\
\zeta\end{pmatrix}\mapsto\begin{pmatrix}\eta_{[13]}\xi_{[12]}\\
\zeta\end{pmatrix}

(3.14)

of unitary
\mathscr{G}
-representations (see (
2.4
)). Let us check this for index
21
. Take
\zeta=\zeta_{1}\otimes\zeta_{2}\in\mathcal{H}\otimes\mathcal{H}^{\prime},\theta\in L^{2}(\mathbb{G}_{21})
. We have

\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathscr{G}}(U\otop U^{\prime})_{21}^{*}(1\otimes\widetilde{u}_{\mathcal{H},\mathcal{H}^{\prime}})(\theta\otimes\zeta)=\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathscr{G}}(U\otop U^{\prime})_{21}^{*}(\theta\otimes\zeta)=(\Delta_{22}^{1}\otimes\mathrm{id})(U_{[12]}U^{\prime}_{[13]})^{*}(\theta\otimes 1\otimes\zeta)

and on the other hand

\begin{split}&\quad\;(1\otimes\widetilde{u}_{\mathcal{H},\mathcal{H}^{\prime}})(\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathscr{G}}(U)\otop\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathscr{G}}(U^{\prime}))_{21}^{*}(\theta\otimes\zeta)=(1\otimes\widetilde{u}_{\mathcal{H},\mathcal{H}^{\prime}})(\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathscr{G}}(U)_{21[12]}\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathscr{G}}(U^{\prime})_{21[13]})^{*}(\theta\otimes\zeta_{1}\otimes\zeta_{2})\\
&=(1\otimes\widetilde{u}_{\mathcal{H},\mathcal{H}^{\prime}})\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathscr{G}}(U^{\prime})_{21[14]}^{*}(\Delta^{1}_{22}\otimes\mathrm{id})(U^{*})_{[123]}(\theta\otimes 1\otimes\zeta_{1}\otimes\zeta_{2})\\
&=(1\otimes\widetilde{u}_{\mathcal{H},\mathcal{H}^{\prime}})(\Delta^{1}_{22}\otimes\mathrm{id})(U^{\prime*})_{[145]}(\Delta^{1}_{22}\otimes\mathrm{id})(U^{*})_{[123]}(\theta\otimes 1\otimes\zeta_{1}\otimes 1\otimes\zeta_{2})\\
&=(\Delta^{1}_{22}\otimes\mathrm{id})(U^{\prime*})_{[124]}(\Delta^{1}_{22}\otimes\mathrm{id})(U^{*})_{[123]}(\theta\otimes 1\otimes\zeta_{1}\otimes\zeta_{2})\\
&=(\Delta^{1}_{22}\otimes\mathrm{id})(U^{\prime*}_{[13]}U^{*}_{[12]})(\theta\otimes 1\otimes\zeta_{1}\otimes\zeta_{2}).\end{split}

The remaining equations can be checked in a similar way. Since
\tilde{u}_{\mathcal{H},\mathcal{H}^{\prime}}
becomes the identity under
\mathrm{Res}_{\mathscr{G}}
, we must necessarily have that
\widetilde{u}_{\mathcal{H},\mathcal{H}^{\prime}}
is a unitary, providing the monoidal structure on
\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathscr{G}}
. It then follows that this claim holds for the
u_{\mathcal{H},\mathcal{H}^{\prime}}
as well.
∎

Remark 3.7
.

It follows from the proof of Theorem
3.6
that, after the identifications
\mathrm{Rep}(\mathbb{G})\cong\mathrm{Rep}(C^{*}(\mathbb{G}))
and
