\mathrm{Rep}(\mathscr{G})\cong\mathrm{Rep}(C^{*}(\mathscr{G}))
, we get a natural isomorphism between induction functors:

\bigl(\,\mathrm{Ind}^{C^{*}}_{\mathscr{G}}\colon\mathrm{Rep}(C^{*}(\mathbb{G}))\rightarrow\mathrm{Rep}(C^{*}(\mathscr{G}))\,\bigr)\quad\cong\quad\bigl(\,\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathscr{G}}\colon\mathrm{Rep}(\mathbb{G})\rightarrow\mathrm{Rep}(\mathscr{G})\,\bigr);

a conclusion which is not immediate just by looking at the definitions.

We will also need a more concrete form of the natural isomorphism

\bigl(\,\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}\colon\mathrm{Rep}(\mathbb{G})\rightarrow\mathrm{Rep}(\mathbb{H})\,\bigr)\quad\cong\quad\bigl(\,\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}^{C^{*}}\colon\mathrm{Rep}(C^{*}(\mathbb{G}))\rightarrow\mathrm{Rep}(C^{*}(\mathbb{H}))\,\bigr).

To make this precise, let us define

\widetilde{\mathrm{Ind}}_{\mathbb{X}}\colon\mathrm{Rep}(C^{*}(\mathbb{G}))\rightarrow\mathrm{Rep}(C^{*}(\mathbb{H}))

(3.15)

as the composition of
\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}
with the isomorphisms
\mathrm{Rep}(C^{*}(\mathbb{G}))\cong\mathrm{Rep}(\mathbb{G})
and
\mathrm{Rep}(\mathbb{H})\cong\mathrm{Rep}(C^{*}(\mathbb{H}))
, and similarly define

\widetilde{\mathrm{Ind}}_{\mathscr{G}}\colon\mathrm{Rep}(C^{*}(\mathbb{G}))\rightarrow\mathrm{Rep}(C^{*}(\mathscr{G}))

(3.16)

as the composition of
\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathscr{G}}
with
\mathrm{Rep}(C^{*}(\mathbb{G}))\cong\mathrm{Rep}(\mathbb{G})
and
\mathrm{Rep}(\mathscr{G})\cong\mathrm{Rep}(C^{*}(\mathscr{G}))
.

Lemma 3.8
.

Let
(\mathcal{H},U)\in\mathrm{Rep}(\mathbb{G})
with the associated
*
-representation
(\mathcal{H},\hat{\pi})\in\mathrm{Rep}(C^{*}(\mathbb{G}))
, and define
\hat{\pi}_{ij}
to be the components of
\widetilde{Ind}_{\mathscr{G}}(\hat{\pi})\colon C^{*}(\mathscr{G})\rightarrow\mathcal{B}(Ind_{\mathscr{G}}(\mathcal{H}))
. The family of unitaries

\theta_{(\mathcal{H},\hat{\pi})}\colon C^{*}(\mathbb{X})\otimes_{C^{*}(\mathbb{G})}\mathcal{H}\ni b\otimes\xi\mapsto\hat{\pi}_{12}(b)\xi\in\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}(\mathcal{H})

establishes a natural isomorphism

\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}^{C^{*}}\cong\widetilde{\mathrm{Ind}}_{\mathbb{X}}.

Proof.

It is straightforward to check that
\theta_{(\mathcal{H},\hat{\pi})}
is well-defined and isometric. Let us prove that it is surjective, using that
\widetilde{\mathrm{Ind}}_{\mathscr{G}}(\hat{\pi})
is non-degenerate:

\begin{split}\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}(\mathcal{H})&=\overline{\operatorname{span}}\{\hat{\pi}_{11}(a)\xi+\hat{\pi}_{12}(b)\eta\mid a\in C^{*}(\mathbb{H}),\xi\in\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}(\mathcal{H}),b\in C^{*}(\mathbb{X}),\eta\in\mathcal{H}\}\\
&=\overline{\operatorname{span}}\{\hat{\pi}_{12}(b^{\prime})\hat{\pi}_{21}(c)\xi+\hat{\pi}_{12}(b)\eta\mid c\in C^{*}(\mathbb{Y}),\xi\in\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}(\mathcal{H}),b,b^{\prime}\in C^{*}(\mathbb{X}),\eta\in\mathcal{H}\}\\
&\subseteq\overline{\operatorname{span}}\{\hat{\pi}_{12}(b)\xi\mid b\in C^{*}(\mathbb{X}),\xi\in\mathcal{H}\}\subseteq\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}(\mathcal{H}).\end{split}

Next we check that
\theta_{(\mathcal{H},\hat{\pi})}
is intertwiner: for
a\in C^{*}(\mathbb{H}),b\in C^{*}(\mathbb{X}),\xi\in\mathcal{H}
we have

\begin{split}&\quad\;\theta_{(\mathcal{H},\hat{\pi})}\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}^{C^{*}}(\hat{\pi})(a)(b\otimes\xi)=\hat{\pi}_{12}(ab)\xi=\hat{\pi}_{11}(a)\hat{\pi}_{12}(b)\xi=\widetilde{\mathrm{Ind}}_{\mathbb{X}}(\hat{\pi})(a)\theta_{(\mathcal{H},\hat{\pi})}(b\otimes\xi).\end{split}

Finally, it is easy to see that the family
(\theta_{(\mathcal{H},\hat{\pi})})_{(\mathcal{H},\hat{\pi})\in\mathrm{Rep}(C^{*}(\mathbb{G}))}
is natural, which ends the proof.
∎

3.3.
Induction of actions

Let
\mathscr{G}
be a linking quantum groupoid between the LCQGs
\mathbb{G}
and
\mathbb{H}
. Let
(A,\gamma)
be a left
\mathbb{G}
-action on a W
∗
-algebra
A
, and let
U_{\gamma}
be the canonical unitary
\mathbb{G}
-representation on
L^{2}(A)
implementing
\gamma
.

Theorem 3.9
.

There is a canonical identification of unitary
\mathbb{H}
-representations

I\colon(L^{2}(\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}(A)),U_{\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}(\gamma)})\cong(\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}(L^{2}(A)),\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}(U_{\gamma})).

Moreover, the induced left
\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}(A)
-representation on
\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}(L^{2}(A))
is given by left multiplication;

I\pi_{\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}(A)}(w)I^{*}\eta=(\mathrm{id}\otimes\pi_{A})(w)\eta,\qquad\forall w\in\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}(A),\eta\in\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}(L^{2}(A)).

Proof.

Consider
\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathscr{G}}(A)=A_{1}\oplus A_{2}=\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}(A)\oplus A
, with
\mathscr{G}
-action
\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathscr{G}}(\gamma)
and implementation
U_{\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathscr{G}}(\gamma)}
as in (
3.3
) and Section
2.3
. Define

I\colon L^{2}(\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}(A))\rightarrow\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}(L^{2}(A))\subseteq L^{\infty}(\mathbb{X})\bar{\otimes}L^{2}(A),\qquad\xi\mapsto U_{\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathscr{G}}(\gamma),12}^{*}(1\otimes\xi).

Clearly
I
is an isometric map
L^{2}(\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}(A))\rightarrow L^{\infty}(\mathbb{X})\bar{\otimes}L^{2}(A)
, and it is easy to check that the image of
I
indeed lands in
\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}(L^{2}(A))
. The isometry
I
is an intertwiner between the unitary
\mathbb{H}
-representations
U_{\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}(\gamma)}
and
\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}(U_{\gamma})
. Indeed, for
\xi\in L^{2}(\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}(A)),\eta\in L^{2}(\mathbb{H})
we have

\begin{split}&\quad\;\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}(U_{\gamma})^{*}(1\otimes I)(\eta\otimes\xi)=\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}(U_{\gamma})^{*}U^{*}_{\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathscr{G}}(\gamma),12[23]}(\eta\otimes 1\otimes\xi)=(\gamma_{\mathbb{X}}\otimes\mathrm{id})(U^{*}_{\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathscr{G}}(\gamma),12}(1\otimes\xi))\,(\eta\otimes 1)\\
&=(\Delta_{12}^{1}\otimes\mathrm{id})(U^{*}_{\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathscr{G}}(\gamma),12}(1\otimes\xi))\,(\eta\otimes 1)=U^{*}_{\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathscr{G}}(\gamma),12[23]}U^{*}_{\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathscr{G}}(\gamma),11[13]}(\eta\otimes 1\otimes\xi)\end{split}

and

(1\otimes I)U_{\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}(\gamma)}^{*}(\eta\otimes\xi)=U^{*}_{\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathscr{G}}(\gamma),12[23]}U^{*}_{\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}(\gamma)[13]}(\eta\otimes 1\otimes\xi)=U^{*}_{\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathscr{G}}(\gamma),12[23]}U^{*}_{\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathscr{G}}(\gamma),11[13]}(\eta\otimes 1\otimes\xi)

which proves the claim. Note that we have used
U_{\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathscr{G}}(\gamma),11}=U_{\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}(\gamma)}
. Next we define an isometric map

\widetilde{I}=I\oplus\mathrm{id}\colon\begin{pmatrix}L^{2}(\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}(A))\\
L^{2}(A)\end{pmatrix}\rightarrow\begin{pmatrix}\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}(L^{2}(A))\\
L^{2}(A)\end{pmatrix}

and we claim that
\tilde{I}
is an intertwiner between
U_{\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathscr{G}}(\gamma)}
and
\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathscr{G}}(U_{\gamma})
. Let us check that

\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathscr{G}}(U_{\gamma})_{21}(1\otimes I)=U_{\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathscr{G}}(\gamma),21}\colon L^{2}(\mathbb{Y})\otimes L^{2}(\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}(A))\rightarrow L^{2}(\mathbb{Y})\otimes L^{2}(A)

(3.17)

or equivalently
(1\otimes I)U_{\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathscr{G}}(\gamma),21}^{*}=\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathscr{G}}(U_{\gamma})_{21}^{*}
. Take
\eta\in L^{2}(\mathbb{Y}),\zeta\in L^{2}(A)
: using
U_{\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathscr{G}}(\gamma),22}=U_{\gamma}
, we calculate

(1\otimes I)U^{*}_{\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathscr{G}}(\gamma),21}(\eta\otimes\zeta)=U^{*}_{\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathscr{G}}(\gamma),12[23]}U^{*}_{\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathscr{G}}(\gamma),21[13]}(\eta\otimes 1\otimes\zeta)

and

\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathscr{G}}(U_{\gamma})^{*}_{21}(\eta\otimes\zeta)=(\Delta_{22}^{1}\otimes\mathrm{id})(U_{\gamma}^{*})(\eta\otimes 1\otimes\zeta)=(\Delta_{22}^{1}\otimes\mathrm{id})(U_{\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathscr{G}}(\gamma),22}^{*})(\eta\otimes 1\otimes\zeta),

which proves that (
3.17
) holds. The remaining equations can be proved in a similar way. Equation (
3.17
) implies

U_{\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathscr{G}}(\gamma),21}(1\otimes I^{*})=\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathscr{G}}(U_{\gamma})_{21}

which proves that
I^{*}
is isometric and consequently
I
is a unitary. Alternatively, in the proof of Theorem
3.6
we have argued that
\operatorname{Res}\colon\mathrm{Rep}(C^{*}(\mathscr{G}))\rightarrow\mathrm{Rep}(C^{*}(\mathbb{G}))
is an equivalence of categories. Since it maps the morphism
\tilde{I}
to the unitary
\mathrm{id}
, it follows that
\tilde{I}
must be a unitary as well.

Recall that
\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathscr{G}}(\gamma)_{1}^{2}\colon\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}(A)\rightarrow L^{\infty}(\mathbb{X})\bar{\otimes}A
is the inclusion map, thus

U_{\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathscr{G}}(\gamma),12}^{*}(1\otimes\pi_{\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}(A)}(w))U_{\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathscr{G}}(\gamma),12}=(\mathrm{id}\otimes\pi_{A})(w)

for
w\in\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}(A)
and

\begin{split}&\quad\;I\pi_{\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}(A)}(w)I^{*}(I\xi)=I\pi_{\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}(A)}(w)\xi=U^{*}_{\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathscr{G}}(\gamma),12}(1\otimes\pi_{\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}(A)}(w)\xi)\\
&=(\mathrm{id}\otimes\pi_{A})(w)U^{*}_{\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathscr{G}}(\gamma),12}(1\otimes\xi)=(\mathrm{id}\otimes\pi_{A})(w)I\xi\end{split}

for
w\in\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}(A),\xi\in L^{2}(\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}(A))
. Since
I
is surjective, this proves the second claim.
∎

The following lemma will be useful to record for calculations in the presence of a unitary
2
-cocycle.

Lemma 3.10
.

Let
(A,\gamma)
be a
\mathbb{G}
-W
∗
-algebra. Under the identification in Theorem
3.9
, the modular conjugation
J_{\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}(A)}
of
\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}(A)
is the unique anti-linear map
\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}(L^{2}(A))\rightarrow\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}(L^{2}(A))
such that

(\hat{J}_{11}\otimes J_{\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}(A)})\bigl(\hat{\pi}_{12}^{1}(x)_{[1]}(\Delta_{22}^{1}\otimes\mathrm{id})(\gamma(a)U_{\gamma}^{*})_{[123]}((\hat{J}_{12}\otimes J_{A})(\hat{\pi}_{12}^{2}(z)\otimes 1)\gamma(b)\xi)_{[13]}\bigr)\\
=\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}(U_{\gamma})\hat{\pi}_{12}^{1}(z)_{[1]}(\Delta_{22}^{1}\otimes\mathrm{id})(\gamma(b)U_{\gamma}^{*})_{[123]}((\hat{J}_{12}\otimes J_{A})(\hat{\pi}_{12}^{2}(x)\otimes 1)\gamma(a)U_{\gamma}^{*}(\hat{J}_{22}\otimes J_{A})\xi)_{[13]}

(3.18)

for
\xi\in L^{2}(\mathbb{G})\otimes L^{2}(A)
,
x,z\in\hat{N}
and
a,b\in A
.

Proof.

Applying (
1.1
) to the W
∗
-Morita equivalence (
2.11
), we obtain a composition of unitary identifications

\begin{split}(\mathbb{G}_{12}\ltimes A)\overline{\otimes}_{\mathbb{G}_{22}\ltimes A}\overline{L^{2}(\mathbb{G}_{12})\otimes L^{2}(A)}&\cong(\mathbb{G}_{12}\ltimes A)\overline{\otimes}_{\mathbb{G}_{22}\ltimes A}\overline{L^{2}(\mathbb{G}_{12}\ltimes A)}\cong L^{2}(\mathbb{G}_{11}\ltimes\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}(A))\\
&\cong L^{2}(\mathbb{G}_{11})\otimes L^{2}(\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}(A))\cong L^{2}(\mathbb{G}_{11})\otimes\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}(L^{2}(A)).\end{split}

(3.19)

Following the precise prescription of the maps in (
3.19
), we see that this identification becomes

\begin{split}&\quad\;\hat{p}_{1}(\pi_{\hat{Q}}(x)\otimes 1)\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathscr{G}}(\gamma)(a)\hat{p}_{2}\otimes\overline{\hat{p}_{1}(\pi_{\hat{Q}}(z)\otimes 1)\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathscr{G}}(\gamma)(b)\hat{p}_{2}\xi}\\
&\mapsto\hat{p}_{1}(\pi_{\hat{Q}}(x)\otimes 1)\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathscr{G}}(\gamma)(a)\hat{p}_{2}J_{12}^{\ltimes}\hat{p}_{1}(\pi_{\hat{Q}}(z)\otimes 1)\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathscr{G}}(\gamma)(b)\hat{p}_{2}\xi\\
&\mapsto(1\otimes U^{*}_{\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathscr{G}}(\gamma),12})\bigl(\hat{p}_{1}(\pi_{\hat{Q}}(x)\otimes 1)\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathscr{G}}(\gamma)(a)\hat{p}_{2}J_{12}^{\ltimes}\hat{p}_{1}(\pi_{\hat{Q}}(z)\otimes 1)\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathscr{G}}(\gamma)(b)\hat{p}_{2}\xi\bigr)_{[13]}\end{split}

for
x,z\in\hat{N},a,b\in A,\xi\in L^{2}(\mathbb{G})\otimes L^{2}(A)
(the first and the third isomorphisms in (
3.19
) are formal identities). Let us remark that we consider
L^{2}(\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}(A))
as having one leg in this computation. Using (
2.12
), and by tracing to which space particular vectors belong to, we can simplify this expression to

\begin{split}&\quad\;U^{*}_{\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathscr{G}}(\gamma),12[23]}\pi_{\hat{Q}}(x)_{[1]}\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathscr{G}}(\gamma)_{2}^{1}(a)_{[13]}U^{*}_{\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathscr{G}}(\gamma),21[13]}\bigl((\hat{J}_{12}\otimes J_{A})(\pi_{\hat{Q}}(z)\otimes 1)\gamma(b)\xi\bigr)_{[13]}\\
&=\pi_{\hat{Q}}(x)_{[1]}(\mathrm{id}\otimes\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathscr{G}}(\gamma)_{1}^{2})\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathscr{G}}(\gamma)_{2}^{1}(a)U^{*}_{\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathscr{G}}(\gamma),12[23]}U^{*}_{\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathscr{G}}(\gamma),21[13]}\bigl((\hat{J}_{12}\otimes J_{A})(\pi_{\hat{Q}}(z)\otimes 1)\gamma(b)\xi\bigr)_{[13]}.\end{split}

Next, using that
\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathscr{G}}(\gamma)^{2}_{1}
is simply the inclusion map
\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}(A)\rightarrow L^{\infty}(\mathbb{G}_{12})\bar{\otimes}A
, we further obtain from (
3.4
) that

\begin{split}&\quad\;\pi_{\hat{Q}}(x)_{[1]}\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathscr{G}}(\gamma)_{2}^{1}(a)U^{*}_{\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathscr{G}}(\gamma),12[23]}U^{*}_{\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathscr{G}}(\gamma),21[13]}\bigl((\hat{J}_{12}\otimes J_{A})(\pi_{\hat{Q}}(z)\otimes 1)\gamma(b)\xi\bigr)_{[13]}\\
&=\pi_{\hat{Q}}(x)_{[1]}(\Delta^{1}_{22}\otimes\mathrm{id})\gamma(a)(\Delta_{22}^{1}\otimes\mathrm{id})(U^{*}_{\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathscr{G}}(\gamma),22})\bigl((\hat{J}_{12}\otimes J_{A})(\pi_{\hat{Q}}(z)\otimes 1)\gamma(b)\xi\bigr)_{[13]}\\
&=\pi_{\hat{Q}}(x)_{[1]}(\Delta^{1}_{22}\otimes\mathrm{id})(\gamma(a)U^{*}_{\gamma})\bigl((\hat{J}_{12}\otimes J_{A})(\pi_{\hat{Q}}(z)\otimes 1)\gamma(b)\xi\bigr)_{[13]}.\end{split}

Using (
2.12
) and Theorem
3.9
, we see that under the isomorphisms (
3.19
), the modular conjugation (
1.3
) is mapped to
\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}(U_{\gamma})^{*}(\hat{J}_{11}\otimes J_{\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}(A)})
. This gives (
3.18
).
∎

4.
Application to cocycle twists

4.1.
Unitary
2
-cocycles

Assume again that
\mathbb{G}=(M,\Delta)
is a LCQG, and assume that
\mathscr{G}
is a linking quantum groupoid with
\mathbb{G}
as its
22
corner. Then
\mathscr{G}
can be constructed from a dual unitary
2
-cocycle
\hat{\Omega}
for
\mathbb{G}
as in Section
1.5
, if and only if the underlying linking W
∗
-algebra
W^{*}(\mathscr{G})
is trivialisable, in the sense that there exists an isomorphism of W
∗
-algebras (with projection)

\hat{Q}=\begin{pmatrix}\hat{P}&\hat{N}\\
\hat{O}&\hat{M}\end{pmatrix}\cong\begin{pmatrix}\hat{M}&\hat{M}\\
\hat{M}&\hat{M}\end{pmatrix}.

(4.1)

Indeed, note first that if the isomorphism (
4.1
) holds, then we can choose it to be an identity on the lower right hand corner. Then the associated unitary
2
-cocycle is given by
\hat{\Omega}:=\hat{\Delta}_{12}(1_{\hat{M}})
. Any other choice of trivialisation (
4.1
) will lead to a cohomologous
2
-cocycle
\hat{\Omega}^{\prime}
, meaning that there exists a unitary
u\in\hat{M}
such that

(u\otimes u)\hat{\Omega}^{\prime}=\hat{\Omega}\Delta_{\hat{M}}(u).

(4.2)

We then refer to
u
as a
unitary coboundary
between
\hat{\Omega}
and
\hat{\Omega}^{\prime}
.

Fix in the following a dual unitary
2
-cocycle
\hat{\Omega}
for
\mathbb{G}
, with
W^{*}(\mathscr{G})=\begin{pmatrix}W^{*}(\mathbb{G})&W^{*}(\mathbb{G})\\
W^{*}(\mathbb{G})&W^{*}(\mathbb{G})\end{pmatrix}
as the associated linking quantum groupoid
\mathscr{G}
. Then we can also identify

L^{2}(\mathscr{G})\cong\begin{pmatrix}L^{2}(\mathbb{G})&L^{2}(\mathbb{G})\\
L^{2}(\mathbb{G})&L^{2}(\mathbb{G})\end{pmatrix},

where on the right hand side we have the direct sum of Hilbert spaces, with the standard structure and modular conjugation given by

\pi_{\hat{Q}}\begin{pmatrix}x&y\\
w&z\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}\xi&\eta\\
\zeta&\vartheta\end{pmatrix}=\begin{pmatrix}\pi_{\hat{M}}(x)\xi+\pi_{\hat{M}}(y)\zeta&\pi_{\hat{M}}(x)\eta+\pi_{\hat{M}}(y)\vartheta\\
\pi_{\hat{M}}(w)\xi+\pi_{\hat{M}}(z)\zeta&\pi_{\hat{M}}(w)\eta+\pi_{\hat{M}}(z)\vartheta\end{pmatrix},\qquad J_{\hat{Q}}\begin{pmatrix}\xi&\eta\\
\zeta&\vartheta\end{pmatrix}=\begin{pmatrix}J_{\hat{M}}\xi&J_{\hat{M}}\zeta\\
J_{\hat{M}}\eta&J_{\hat{M}}\vartheta\end{pmatrix}.

Through the natural GNS-representation of
L^{\infty}(\mathscr{G})
on
L^{2}(\mathscr{G})
via the dual weight construction, we also find that
L^{2}(\mathbb{G})
acts as the standard Hilbert space for the (generally distinct) W
∗
-algebras
L^{\infty}(\mathbb{G}_{ij})
.

A crucial role is played by the modular conjugation
J_{N}
for
N=L^{\infty}(\mathbb{X})=L^{\infty}(\mathbb{G}_{12})
under this construction. Indeed, its knowledge is sufficient to express concretely the multiplicative partial isometry
\mathrm{W}_{\hat{Q}}
of
\hat{Q}
: putting again
\hat{\mathrm{W}}=\mathrm{W}_{\hat{M}}
, it follows from
[
DC11b
]
*Proposition 6.5 and its proof that

\hat{\mathrm{W}}^{k}_{22}=\hat{\mathrm{W}},\quad\hat{\mathrm{W}}^{k}_{12}=\hat{\mathrm{W}}\hat{\Omega}^{*},

(4.3)

\hat{\mathrm{W}}^{k}_{21}=(J_{N}\otimes J_{\hat{M}})\hat{\Omega}\hat{\mathrm{W}}^{*}(J_{M}\otimes J_{\hat{M}}),\quad\hat{\mathrm{W}}^{k}_{11}=(J_{N}\otimes J_{\hat{M}})\hat{\Omega}\hat{\mathrm{W}}^{*}(J_{M}\otimes J_{\hat{M}})\hat{\Omega}^{*}

(4.4)

for
k\in\{1,2\}
(see equation (
1.5
) for the definition of these unitaries. Note also that the above formulas do not depend on
k
).

We can write these last formulas a bit differently as follows. First note that associated to
\hat{\Omega}
is the
conjugate unitary
2
-cocycle

\hat{\Omega}_{c}:=(\hat{R}\otimes\hat{R})(\hat{\Omega}_{[21]}^{*})\in\hat{M}\bar{\otimes}\hat{M}.

If we write

X_{\hat{\Omega}}:=J_{N}J_{M},

then it can be shown
[
DC11b
]
*Proposition 6.3 that
X_{\hat{\Omega}}\in\hat{M}
, and that
X_{\hat{\Omega}}
is a unitary coboundary between
\hat{\Omega}
and
\hat{\Omega}_{c}
,

(X_{\hat{\Omega}}\otimes X_{\hat{\Omega}})\hat{\Omega}_{c}=\hat{\Omega}\Delta_{\hat{M}}(X_{\hat{\Omega}}).

(4.5)

If we now recall the fundamental unitary
u_{\mathbb{G}}=\nu_{\mathbb{G}}^{i/8}J_{M}J_{\hat{M}}
, then by using (
0.11
) and (
0.10
) the formulas in (
4.4
) can be rewritten as

\hat{\mathrm{W}}^{k}_{21}=(X_{\hat{\Omega}}\otimes u_{\mathbb{G}})\hat{\Omega}_{c[21]}(1\otimes u_{\mathbb{G}})\hat{\mathrm{W}},\qquad\hat{\mathrm{W}}^{k}_{11}=(X_{\hat{\Omega}}\otimes u_{\mathbb{G}})\hat{\Omega}_{c[21]}(1\otimes u_{\mathbb{G}})\hat{\mathrm{W}}\hat{\Omega}^{*}.

Let us end with a more conceptual description of the element
X_{\hat{\Omega}}
. Recall from (
1.8
) the unitary antipode on
W^{*}(\mathscr{G})
. Then we see that in fact
X_{\hat{\Omega}}^{*}=\hat{R}_{12}(1_{\hat{M}})
. From (
1.10
), we can then also recover the other components of this unitary antipode using just the unitary antipode
\hat{R}
of
(W^{*}(\mathbb{G}),\Delta_{\hat{M}})
and the element
X_{\hat{\Omega}}
:

\hat{R}_{22}(x)=\hat{R}(x),\quad\hat{R}_{12}(x)=\hat{R}(x)X_{\hat{\Omega}}^{*},\quad\hat{R}_{21}(x)=X_{\hat{\Omega}}\hat{R}(x),\quad\hat{R}_{11}(x)=X_{\hat{\Omega}}\hat{R}(x)X_{\hat{\Omega}}^{*},\qquad\forall x\in W^{*}(\mathbb{G}).

(4.6)

4.2.
Universally continuous
2
-cocycles

Having the linking W
∗
-algebra
\hat{Q}=W^{*}(\mathscr{G})
of a linking quantum groupoid
\mathscr{G}=\begin{pmatrix}\mathbb{H}&\mathbb{X}\\
\mathbb{Y}&\mathbb{G}\end{pmatrix}
trivial, does not mean that also the associated universal (or even reduced) linking C
∗
-algebra
\hat{Q}_{u}=C^{*}(\mathscr{G})
is trivial (see e.g.
[
DC10
]
). We therefore introduce the following definition.

Definition 4.1
.

We say that a linking quantum groupoid
\mathscr{G}=\begin{pmatrix}\mathbb{H}&\mathbb{X}\\
\mathbb{Y}&\mathbb{G}\end{pmatrix}
has
trivialisable universal linking C
∗
-algebra
if there exists an isomorphism of C
∗
-algebras

C^{*}(\mathscr{G})=\begin{pmatrix}\hat{P}_{u}&\hat{N}_{u}\\
\hat{O}_{u}&\hat{M}_{u}\end{pmatrix}\cong\begin{pmatrix}\hat{M}_{u}&\hat{M}_{u}\\
\hat{M}_{u}&\hat{M}_{u}\end{pmatrix},

(4.7)

in a manner preserving the matrix decomposition.

Assume now that
\mathscr{G}
is a linking quantum groupoid with trivialisable universal linking C
∗
-algebra.

(1)

By composing with an automorphism of
\begin{pmatrix}\hat{M}_{u}&\hat{M}_{u}\\
\hat{M}_{u}&\hat{M}_{u}\end{pmatrix}
of the form
\theta\otimes\mathrm{id}_{M_{2}}
, we can assume that the isomorphism
\begin{pmatrix}\hat{P}_{u}&\hat{N}_{u}\\
\hat{O}_{u}&\hat{M}_{u}\end{pmatrix}\cong\begin{pmatrix}\hat{M}_{u}&\hat{M}_{u}\\
\hat{M}_{u}&\hat{M}_{u}\end{pmatrix}
preserves the matrix structure, and is the identity on the lower right hand corner.

(2)

The counit (
2.10
) induces a non-degenerate
*
-homomorphism

\begin{pmatrix}\hat{\varepsilon}_{11}&\hat{\varepsilon}_{12}\\
\hat{\varepsilon}_{21}&\hat{\varepsilon}_{22}\end{pmatrix}\colon\begin{pmatrix}\hat{M}_{u}&\hat{M}_{u}\\
\hat{M}_{u}&\hat{M}_{u}\end{pmatrix}\rightarrow\begin{pmatrix}\mathbb{C}&\mathbb{C}\\
\mathbb{C}&\mathbb{C}\end{pmatrix}

satisfying
\hat{\varepsilon}_{22}=\hat{\varepsilon}
, where
\hat{\varepsilon}
is the counit of
(\hat{M}_{u},\hat{\Delta}_{u})
. Write
\mu=\hat{\varepsilon}_{12}(1_{\hat{M}_{u}})\in\mathbb{T}
. By composing the isomorphism (
4.7
) with the automorphism
\begin{pmatrix}a&b\\
c&d\end{pmatrix}\mapsto\begin{pmatrix}\mu a\overline{\mu}&\mu b\\
c\overline{\mu}&d\end{pmatrix}
of
\begin{pmatrix}\hat{M}_{u}&\hat{M}_{u}\\
\hat{M}_{u}&\hat{M}_{u}\end{pmatrix}
, we can choose the trivialisation (
4.7
) in such a way that
\hat{\varepsilon}_{12}(1_{\hat{M}_{u}})=1
. We obtain
\hat{\varepsilon}_{ij}=\hat{\varepsilon}
. Indeed, we have

\begin{split}\begin{pmatrix}\hat{\varepsilon}_{11}(a)&0\\
0&0\end{pmatrix}&=\begin{pmatrix}\hat{\varepsilon}_{11}&\hat{\varepsilon}_{12}\\
\hat{\varepsilon}_{21}&\hat{\varepsilon}_{22}\end{pmatrix}\biggl(\begin{pmatrix}0&1_{\hat{M}_{u}}\\
0&0\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}0&0\\
0&a\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}0&0\\
1_{\hat{M}_{u}}&0\end{pmatrix}\biggr)\\
&=\begin{pmatrix}0&1\\
0&0\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}0&0\\
0&\hat{\varepsilon}_{22}(a)\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}0&0\\
1&0\end{pmatrix}=\begin{pmatrix}\hat{\varepsilon}(a)&0\\
0&0\end{pmatrix}\end{split}

and the remaining equalities can be checked in a similar way.

(3)

Assuming that we have made adjustments as in points
(1),(2)
, the reduction map induces via the isomorphism (
4.7
) a
*
-homomorphism

\pi_{\hat{Q}_{u}}^{\mathrm{red}}=\begin{pmatrix}\hat{\pi}_{\mathrm{red},11}&\hat{\pi}_{\mathrm{red},12}\\
\hat{\pi}_{\mathrm{red},21}&\hat{\pi}_{\mathrm{red},22}\end{pmatrix}\colon\begin{pmatrix}\hat{M}_{u}&\hat{M}_{u}\\
\hat{M}_{u}&\hat{M}_{u}\end{pmatrix}\rightarrow\begin{pmatrix}\hat{M}_{11}&\hat{M}_{12}\\
\hat{M}_{21}&\hat{M}_{22}\end{pmatrix}=\begin{pmatrix}\hat{P}&\hat{N}\\
\hat{O}&\hat{M},\end{pmatrix}

(4.8)

which is non-degenerate when interpreted as a a map into
\operatorname{M}(M_{2}(\mathbb{C})\otimes\mathcal{K}(L^{2}(M)))
. Under the isomorphism (
4.7
), the element
\begin{pmatrix}0&1_{\hat{M}_{u}}\\
0&0\end{pmatrix}
can be seen as living in
\operatorname{M}(\hat{Q}_{u})
, and we can define an element

u=\hat{\pi}_{\mathrm{red},12}(1_{\hat{M}_{u}})\in\hat{M}_{12}

which satisfies

u^{*}u=1_{\hat{M}},\qquad uu^{*}=1_{\hat{P}}.

Using this element, we obtain an isomorphism

\begin{pmatrix}\hat{M}_{11}&\hat{M}_{12}\\
\hat{M}_{21}&\hat{M}_{22}\end{pmatrix}\ni\begin{pmatrix}a&b\\
c&d\end{pmatrix}\mapsto\begin{pmatrix}u^{*}au&u^{*}b\\
cu&d\end{pmatrix}\in\begin{pmatrix}\hat{M}&\hat{M}\\
\hat{M}&\hat{M}\end{pmatrix}.

Composing with this isomorphism, the map (
4.8
) trivializes to

\begin{pmatrix}\hat{\pi}_{\mathrm{red}}&\hat{\pi}_{\mathrm{red}}\\
\hat{\pi}_{\mathrm{red}}&\hat{\pi}_{\mathrm{red}}\end{pmatrix}\colon\begin{pmatrix}\hat{M}_{u}&\hat{M}_{u}\\
\hat{M}_{u}&\hat{M}_{u}\end{pmatrix}\rightarrow\begin{pmatrix}\hat{M}&\hat{M}\\
\hat{M}&\hat{M}\end{pmatrix}.

(4)

By transport of structure we obtain coproducts on
\begin{pmatrix}\hat{M}_{u}&\hat{M}_{u}\\
\hat{M}_{u}&\hat{M}_{u}\end{pmatrix}
and
\begin{pmatrix}\hat{M}&\hat{M}\\
\hat{M}&\hat{M}\end{pmatrix}
.

Definition 4.2
.

We say that a linking quantum groupoid
\mathscr{G}=\begin{pmatrix}\mathbb{H}&\mathbb{X}\\
\mathbb{Y}&\mathbb{G}\end{pmatrix}
has
trivial universal linking C
∗
-algebra
if
W^{*}(\mathscr{G})=\begin{pmatrix}\hat{M}&\hat{M}\\
\hat{M}&\hat{M}\end{pmatrix}
and
C^{*}(\mathscr{G})=\begin{pmatrix}\hat{P}_{u}&\hat{N}_{u}\\
\hat{O}_{u}&\hat{M}_{u}\end{pmatrix}\cong\begin{pmatrix}\hat{M}_{u}&\hat{M}_{u}\\
\hat{M}_{u}&\hat{M}_{u}\end{pmatrix}
under a fixed isomorphism which preserves the matrix structure
6
6

6

We will neglect writing these isomorphisms explicitely.
, is identity on the lower right hand corner, in which
\hat{\varepsilon}_{ij}\cong\varepsilon_{\hat{M}_{u}}

and under which the reducing map corresponds to
\begin{pmatrix}\hat{\pi}_{\mathrm{red}}&\hat{\pi}_{\mathrm{red}}\\
\hat{\pi}_{\mathrm{red}}&\hat{\pi}_{\mathrm{red}}\end{pmatrix}
.

The discussion after Definition
4.1
shows that any linking quantum groupoid with trivialisable universal linking C
∗
-algebra leads to an isomorphic linking quantum groupoid, with trivial universal linking C
∗
-algebra.

Let
\mathscr{G}=\begin{pmatrix}\mathbb{H}&\mathbb{X}\\
\mathbb{Y}&\mathbb{G}\end{pmatrix}
be a linking quantum groupoid with trivial universal linking C
∗
-algebra. Then since
W^{*}(\mathbb{G}_{ij})=\hat{M}_{ij}=\hat{M}
, we also have
L^{2}(\mathbb{G}_{ij})=L^{2}(\mathbb{G})
. Define the unitary

\hat{\Omega}_{u}=\hat{\Delta}_{u,12}(1_{\hat{M}_{u}})\in\operatorname{M}(\hat{M}_{u}\otimes\hat{M}_{u}),

and call it the associated
universal dual unitary
2
-cocycle
for
\mathbb{G}
. We then also write

\hat{\Omega}=(\hat{\pi}_{\mathrm{red}}\otimes\hat{\pi}_{\mathrm{red}})(\hat{\Omega}_{u})\in\operatorname{M}(C_{\mathrm{red}}^{*}(\mathbb{G})\otimes C_{\mathrm{red}}^{*}(\mathbb{G}))\subseteq\hat{M}\bar{\otimes}\hat{M}

(4.9)

for the associated W
∗
-algebraic dual unitary
2
-cocycle of
\mathbb{G}
. Note that

(\hat{\varepsilon}\otimes\mathrm{id})\hat{\Omega}_{u}=(\mathrm{id}\otimes\hat{\varepsilon})\hat{\Omega}_{u}=1_{\hat{M}_{u}}.

Definition 4.3
.

We say that a unitary
2
-cocycle
\hat{\Omega}\in\hat{M}\bar{\otimes}\hat{M}

admits a universal lift
if it arises in the above way. The corresponding unitary
2
-cocycle
\hat{\Omega}_{u}=\hat{\Delta}_{u,12}(1_{\hat{M}_{u}})
is called a
universal lift
of
\hat{\Omega}
.

Remark 4.4
.

Let us stress that the requirement that
\hat{\Omega}
admits a universal lift in the sense of Definition
4.3
is in principle stronger than merely requiring the existence of a unitary
2
-cocycle
\hat{\Omega}_{u}
satisfying
(\hat{\pi}_{\mathrm{red}}\otimes\hat{\pi}_{\mathrm{red}})(\hat{\Omega}_{u})=\hat{\Omega}
. Indeed, in the latter case it is not clear if twisting the amplified comultiplication of
M_{2}(C^{*}(\mathbb{G}))
with
\hat{\Omega}_{u}
will lead to a copy of
(C^{*}(\mathscr{G}),\hat{\Delta}_{u})
. We do not know however of a concrete example illustrating this phenomenon.

Remark 4.5
.

A universal lift will never be unique unless
\hat{\mathbb{G}}
is coamenable, i.e.
C^{*}(\mathbb{G})\cong C^{*}_{\mathrm{red}}(\mathbb{G})
. Indeed, if
\hat{\mathbb{G}}
is not coamenable, we can take any non-trivial unitary
v\in\operatorname{M}(C^{*}(\mathbb{G}))
such that
\hat{\pi}_{\mathrm{red}}(v)=1_{\hat{M}}
and
\hat{\varepsilon}(v)=1
, and then

(v^{*}\otimes v^{*})\hat{\Omega}_{u}\hat{\Delta}_{u}(v)

is a universal lift of
\hat{\Omega}
as well. However, it is clear that this is the only thing that can go wrong: if
C
is a C
∗
-algebra, then any isomorphism

\begin{pmatrix}C&C\\
C&C\end{pmatrix}\rightarrow\begin{pmatrix}C&C\\
C&C\end{pmatrix}

that is the identity in the lower hand corner must be of the form

\begin{pmatrix}a&b\\
c&d\end{pmatrix}\mapsto\begin{pmatrix}vav^{*}&vb\\
cv^{*}&d\end{pmatrix}

for some unitary
v\in\operatorname{M}(C)
. In the case of a linking quantum groupoid this will lead to the coboundary between the respective two universal lifts.

Recall from Lemma
3.8
that, for any linking quantum groupoid, we have fixed a natural isomorphism
\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}^{C^{*}}\rightarrow\widetilde{\mathrm{Ind}}_{\mathbb{X}}
. Since the functor
\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}
is monoidal via unitaries
u_{\mathcal{H},\mathcal{H}^{\prime}}
by Theorem
3.6
, so is the functor
\widetilde{\mathrm{Ind}}_{\mathbb{X}}
, with the same family of unitaries. We hence obtain a monoidal structure on
\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}^{C^{*}}
which we denote by
v_{\mathcal{H},\mathcal{H}^{\prime}}
:

\begin{split}v_{\mathcal{H},\mathcal{H}^{\prime}}=&\theta_{(\mathcal{H}\otimes\mathcal{H}^{\prime},\hat{\pi}\ast\hat{\pi}^{\prime})}^{*}u_{\mathcal{H},\mathcal{H}^{\prime}}(\theta_{(\mathcal{H},\hat{\pi})}\otimes\theta_{(\mathcal{H}^{\prime},\hat{\pi}^{\prime})})\\
&\colon(C^{*}(\mathbb{X})\otimes_{C^{*}(\mathbb{G})}\mathcal{H})\otimes(C^{*}(\mathbb{X})\otimes_{C^{*}(\mathbb{G})}\mathcal{H}^{\prime})\rightarrow C^{*}(\mathbb{X})\otimes_{C^{*}(\mathbb{G})}(\mathcal{H}\otimes\mathcal{H}^{\prime}).\end{split}

(4.10)

for
(\mathcal{H},\hat{\pi}),(\mathcal{H}^{\prime},\hat{\pi}^{\prime})\in\mathrm{Rep}(C^{*}(\mathbb{G}))
.

Fix now a linking quantum groupoid as above with trivial universal linking C
∗
-algebra and the associated universal unitary
2
-cocycle
\hat{\Omega}_{u}
. Our next goal is to prove that we can modify the induction functor
\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}^{C^{*}}
to the identity one, but with non-trivial monoidal coherence maps (see Lemma
4.6
and Proposition
4.8
).

Define the identity functor

\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}^{C^{*},\mathrm{triv}}=\mathrm{id}\colon\mathrm{Rep}(C^{*}(\mathbb{G}))\rightarrow\mathrm{Rep}(C^{*}(\mathbb{H})),

implicitly using the isomorphism
C^{*}(\mathbb{G})\cong C^{*}(\mathbb{H})
, with non-trivial monoidal coherence maps given by

(\hat{\pi}\otimes\hat{\pi}^{\prime})\hat{\Omega}_{u[21]}^{*}\colon\mathcal{H}\otimes_{\mathbb{H}}\mathcal{H}^{\prime}\rightarrow\mathcal{H}\otimes_{\mathbb{G}}\mathcal{H}^{\prime},\quad\quad\forall(\mathcal{H},\hat{\pi}),(\mathcal{H}^{\prime},\hat{\pi}^{\prime})\in\mathrm{Rep}(C^{*}(\mathbb{G})).

(4.11)

It follows from Proposition
4.8
below that the above family in fact gives a tensor structure on
\mathrm{Ind}^{C^{*},\mathrm{triv}}_{\mathbb{X}}
.

First, we create a concrete link between
\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}^{C^{*}}
and
\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}^{C^{*},\mathrm{triv}}
. The proof of the next lemma is an easy exercise.

Lemma 4.6
.

For
(\mathcal{H},\hat{\pi})\in\mathrm{Rep}(C^{*}(\mathbb{G}))
, define a unitary map

\mathcal{O}_{(\mathcal{H},\hat{\pi})}\colon C^{*}(\mathbb{X})\otimes_{C^{*}(\mathbb{G})}\mathcal{H}\in a\otimes\xi\mapsto\hat{\pi}(a)\xi\in\mathcal{H}.

Then the family
(\mathcal{O}_{(\mathcal{H},\hat{\pi})})_{(\mathcal{H},\hat{\pi})\in\mathrm{Rep}(C^{*}(\mathbb{G}))}
forms a natural isomorphism
\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}^{C^{*}}\rightarrow\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}^{C^{*},\mathrm{triv}}
.

Next, we need an auxilliary result.

Lemma 4.7
.

Let
(\mathcal{H},U),(\mathcal{H}^{\prime},U^{\prime}),(\mathcal{H}\otimes\mathcal{H}^{\prime},U\otop U^{\prime})\in\mathrm{Rep}(\mathbb{G})
with the associated
*
-representations
\hat{\pi},\hat{\pi}^{\prime}
,
\hat{\pi}\ast\hat{\pi}^{\prime}
, and let
U_{12},U^{\prime}_{12},(U\otop U^{\prime})_{12}
be the
12
corners of the induced representations of
\mathscr{G}
, with associated maps
\hat{\pi}_{12},\hat{\pi}^{\prime}_{12},(\hat{\pi}\ast\hat{\pi}^{\prime})_{12}
. We have

(\hat{\pi}\ast\hat{\pi}^{\prime})_{12}(a)=u_{\mathcal{H},\mathcal{H}^{\prime}}(\hat{\pi}_{12}\otimes\hat{\pi}^{\prime}_{12})\hat{\Delta}_{u,12}^{op}(a),\qquad\forall a\in C^{*}(\mathbb{X}).

Proof.

Recall that
U_{12}^{*}\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{G}_{12})\bar{\otimes}\mathcal{B}(\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}(\mathcal{H}),\mathcal{H})
satisfies

U_{12}^{*}\colon L^{2}(\mathbb{G}_{12})\otimes\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}(\mathcal{H})\ni\xi\otimes z\mapsto z\xi\in L^{2}(\mathbb{G}_{12})\otimes\mathcal{H}

and similarly for
U^{\prime}_{12},(U\otop U^{\prime})_{12}
. We claim that

(U\otop U^{\prime})_{12}^{*}(1\otimes u_{\mathcal{H},\mathcal{H}^{\prime}})=U^{\prime*}_{[13]}U^{*}_{[12]}.

(4.12)

Indeed, we have

\begin{split}&\quad\;(U\otop U^{\prime})_{12}^{*}(1\otimes u_{\mathcal{H},\mathcal{H}^{\prime}})(\xi\otimes z\otimes w)=(U\otop U^{\prime})_{12}^{*}(\xi\otimes w_{[13]}z_{[12]})=w_{[13]}z_{[12]}\xi\\
&=U^{\prime*}_{[13]}(z_{[12]}\xi\otimes w)=U^{\prime*}_{[13]}U^{*}_{[12]}(\xi\otimes z\otimes w).\end{split}

for
\xi\in L^{2}(\mathbb{G}_{12}),z\in\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}(\mathcal{H}),w\in\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}(\mathcal{H}^{\prime})
.

Observe that (c.f.
[
DC09
]
*Lemma 11.1.5)

(\hat{\Delta}_{u,21}\otimes\mathrm{id})(\hat{\mathds{W}}_{12})=\hat{\mathds{W}}_{12[13]}\hat{\mathds{W}}_{12[23]}\;\Rightarrow\;(\mathrm{id}\otimes\hat{\Delta}_{u,12}^{op})(\hat{\mathds{W}}_{12[21]}^{*})=\hat{\mathds{W}}_{12[21]}^{*}\hat{\mathds{W}}_{12[31]}^{*},

hence, using (
4.12
),

\begin{split}&\quad\;(\mathrm{id}\otimes(\hat{\pi}_{12}\otimes\hat{\pi}^{\prime}_{12})\hat{\Delta}_{u,12}^{op})(\hat{\mathds{W}}_{12[21]}^{*})=(\mathrm{id}\otimes\hat{\pi}_{12})(\hat{\mathds{W}}_{12[21]}^{*})_{[12]}(\mathrm{id}\otimes\hat{\pi}^{\prime}_{12})(\hat{\mathds{W}}_{12[21]}^{*})_{[13]}=U_{12[12]}U^{\prime}_{12[13]}\\
&=(1\otimes u_{\mathcal{H},\mathcal{H}^{\prime}})^{*}(U\otop U^{\prime})_{12}=(1\otimes u_{\mathcal{H},\mathcal{H}^{\prime}})^{*}(\mathrm{id}\otimes(\hat{\pi}\ast\hat{\pi}^{\prime})_{12})(\hat{\mathds{W}}_{12[21]}^{*}).\end{split}

The claim now follows by slicing off the first leg.
∎

Finally, we prove that the natural isomorphism
(\mathcal{O}_{(\mathcal{H},\hat{\pi})})_{(\mathcal{H},\hat{\pi})\in\mathrm{Rep}(C^{*}(\mathbb{G}))}
respects the monoidal structures (
4.10
) and (
4.11
).

Proposition 4.8
.

For
(\mathcal{H},\hat{\pi}),(\mathcal{H}^{\prime},\hat{\pi}^{\prime})\in\mathrm{Rep}(C^{*}(\mathbb{G}))
we have

\mathcal{O}_{(\mathcal{H}\otimes\mathcal{H}^{\prime},\hat{\pi}\ast\hat{\pi}^{\prime})}v_{\mathcal{H},\mathcal{H}^{\prime}}(\mathcal{O}_{(\mathcal{H},\hat{\pi})}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{(\mathcal{H}^{\prime},\hat{\pi}^{\prime})})^{*}=(\hat{\pi}\otimes\hat{\pi}^{\prime})(\hat{\Omega}^{*}_{u[21]}).

(4.13)

Proof.

Using the definition (
4.10
) of
v_{\mathcal{H},\mathcal{H}^{\prime}}
, we see that (
4.13
) is equivalent to

(\mathcal{O}_{(\mathcal{H},\hat{\pi})}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{(\mathcal{H}^{\prime},\hat{\pi}^{\prime})})(\theta_{(\mathcal{H},\pi)}^{*}\otimes\theta_{(\mathcal{H}^{\prime},\pi^{\prime})}^{*})u_{\mathcal{H},\mathcal{H}^{\prime}}^{*}=(\hat{\pi}\otimes\hat{\pi}^{\prime})(\hat{\Omega}_{u[21]})\mathcal{O}_{(\mathcal{H}\otimes\mathcal{H}^{\prime},\hat{\pi}\ast\hat{\pi}^{\prime})}\theta_{(\mathcal{H}\otimes\mathcal{H}^{\prime},\pi\ast\pi^{\prime})}^{*}.

(4.14)

Fix
a\in C^{*}(\mathbb{X}),b,c\in C^{*}(\mathbb{G}),\zeta\in\mathcal{H},\zeta^{\prime}\in\mathcal{H}^{\prime}
. Then

(\hat{\pi}\ast\hat{\pi}^{\prime})_{12}(a)(\hat{\pi}(b)\zeta\otimes\hat{\pi}^{\prime}(c)\zeta^{\prime})
is a typical element of
\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}(\mathcal{H}\otimes\mathcal{H}^{\prime})
, in the sense that such elements span a dense subspace (see Lemma
3.8
). Then on the one hand, the right hand side of (
4.14
) evaluates on this element to

\begin{split}&\quad\;(\hat{\pi}\otimes\hat{\pi}^{\prime})(\hat{\Omega}_{u[21]})\mathcal{O}_{(\mathcal{H}\otimes\mathcal{H}^{\prime},\hat{\pi}\ast\hat{\pi}^{\prime})}\theta_{(\mathcal{H}\otimes\mathcal{H}^{\prime},\pi\ast\pi^{\prime})}^{*}\;(\hat{\pi}\ast\hat{\pi}^{\prime})_{12}(a)(\hat{\pi}(b)\zeta\otimes\hat{\pi}^{\prime}(c)\zeta^{\prime})\\
&=(\hat{\pi}\otimes\hat{\pi}^{\prime})(\hat{\Omega}_{u[21]})\mathcal{O}_{(\mathcal{H}\otimes\mathcal{H}^{\prime},\hat{\pi}\ast\hat{\pi}^{\prime})}\bigl(a\otimes(\hat{\pi}(b)\zeta\otimes\hat{\pi}^{\prime}(c)\zeta^{\prime})\bigr)\\
&=(\hat{\pi}\otimes\hat{\pi}^{\prime})(\hat{\Omega}_{u[21]})(\hat{\pi}\ast\hat{\pi}^{\prime})(a)(\hat{\pi}(b)\zeta\otimes\hat{\pi}^{\prime}(c)\zeta^{\prime})\\
&=(\hat{\pi}\otimes\hat{\pi}^{\prime})\hat{\Delta}_{u,12}^{op}(1)(\hat{\pi}\otimes\hat{\pi}^{\prime})\hat{\Delta}^{op}_{u,22}(a)(\hat{\pi}(b)\zeta\otimes\hat{\pi}^{\prime}(c)\zeta^{\prime})=(\hat{\pi}\otimes\hat{\pi}^{\prime})\hat{\Delta}_{u,12}^{op}(a)(\hat{\pi}(b)\zeta\otimes\hat{\pi}^{\prime}(c)\zeta^{\prime}).\end{split}

(4.15)

On the other hand, using Lemma
4.7
, we see that the left hand side of (
4.14
) evaluates to

\begin{split}&\quad\;(\mathcal{O}_{(\mathcal{H},\hat{\pi})}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{(\mathcal{H}^{\prime},\hat{\pi}^{\prime})})(\theta_{(\mathcal{H},\hat{\pi})}^{*}\otimes\theta_{(\mathcal{H}^{\prime},\hat{\pi}^{\prime})}^{*})u_{\mathcal{H},\mathcal{H}^{\prime}}^{*}\;(\hat{\pi}\ast\hat{\pi}^{\prime})_{12}(a)(\hat{\pi}(b)\zeta\otimes\hat{\pi}^{\prime}(c)\zeta^{\prime})\\
&=(\mathcal{O}_{(\mathcal{H},\hat{\pi})}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{(\mathcal{H}^{\prime},\hat{\pi}^{\prime})})(\theta_{(\mathcal{H},\hat{\pi})}^{*}\otimes\theta_{(\mathcal{H}^{\prime},\hat{\pi}^{\prime})}^{*})(\hat{\pi}_{12}\otimes\hat{\pi}^{\prime}_{12})\hat{\Delta}^{op}_{u,12}(a)(\hat{\pi}(b)\zeta\otimes\hat{\pi}^{\prime}(c)\zeta^{\prime})\\
&=(\mathcal{O}_{(\mathcal{H},\hat{\pi})}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{(\mathcal{H}^{\prime},\hat{\pi}^{\prime})})(\theta_{(\mathcal{H},\hat{\pi})}^{*}\otimes\theta_{(\mathcal{H}^{\prime},\hat{\pi}^{\prime})}^{*})(\hat{\pi}_{12}\otimes\hat{\pi}^{\prime}_{12})(\hat{\Delta}^{op}_{u,12}(a)(b\otimes c))(\zeta\otimes\zeta^{\prime}).\end{split}

We have
\hat{\Delta}^{op}_{u,12}(a)(b\otimes c)\in C^{*}(\mathbb{G})\otimes C^{*}(\mathbb{G})
, hence we can write
\hat{\Delta}^{op}_{u,12}(a)(b\otimes c)=\lim_{n\to\infty}\sum_{i=1}^{N_{n}}d^{(n)}_{1,i}\otimes d^{(n)}_{2,i}
in norm, for some
d^{(n)}_{1,i},d^{(n)}_{2,i}\in C^{*}(\mathbb{G})
. As
\hat{\pi}_{12},\hat{\pi}^{\prime}_{12}
are restrictions of
*
-homomorphisms, they are continuous and we further have

\begin{split}&\quad\;(\mathcal{O}_{(\mathcal{H},\hat{\pi})}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{(\mathcal{H}^{\prime},\hat{\pi}^{\prime})})(\theta_{(\mathcal{H},\hat{\pi})}^{*}\otimes\theta_{(\mathcal{H}^{\prime},\hat{\pi}^{\prime})}^{*})u_{\mathcal{H},\mathcal{H}^{\prime}}^{*}\;(\hat{\pi}\ast\hat{\pi}^{\prime})_{12}(a)(\hat{\pi}(b)\zeta\otimes\hat{\pi}^{\prime}(c)\zeta^{\prime})\\
&=\lim_{n\to\infty}\sum_{i=1}^{N_{n}}(\mathcal{O}_{(\mathcal{H},\hat{\pi})}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{(\mathcal{H}^{\prime},\hat{\pi}^{\prime})})(\theta_{(\mathcal{H},\hat{\pi})}^{*}\otimes\theta_{(\mathcal{H}^{\prime},\hat{\pi}^{\prime})}^{*})(\hat{\pi}_{12}(d^{(n)}_{1,i})\zeta\otimes\hat{\pi}^{\prime}_{12}(d^{(n)}_{2,i})\zeta^{\prime})\\
&=\lim_{n\to\infty}\sum_{i=1}^{N_{n}}(\mathcal{O}_{(\mathcal{H},\hat{\pi})}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{(\mathcal{H}^{\prime},\hat{\pi}^{\prime})})\bigl((d^{(n)}_{1,i}\otimes\zeta)\otimes(d^{(n)}_{2,i}\otimes\zeta^{\prime})\bigr)=\lim_{n\to\infty}\sum_{i=1}^{N_{n}}\hat{\pi}(d^{(n)}_{1,i})\zeta\otimes\hat{\pi}^{\prime}(d^{(n)}_{2,i})\zeta^{\prime}\\
&=(\hat{\pi}\otimes\hat{\pi}^{\prime})(\hat{\Delta}^{op}_{u,12}(a)(b\otimes c))(\zeta\otimes\zeta^{\prime})=(\hat{\pi}\otimes\hat{\pi}^{\prime})\hat{\Delta}^{op}_{u,12}(a)(\hat{\pi}(b)\zeta\otimes\hat{\pi}^{\prime}(c)\zeta^{\prime}).\end{split}

(4.16)

Equations (
4.15
), (
4.16
) show (
4.14
) and end the proof.
∎

Write again
\hat{R}_{u}\colon C^{*}(\mathbb{G})\rightarrow C^{*}(\mathbb{G})
for the lift of the unitary antipode to
C^{*}(\mathbb{G})
. Then we can also lift
\hat{\Omega}_{c}
to

\hat{\Omega}_{u,c}:=(\hat{R}_{u}\otimes\hat{R}_{u})(\hat{\Omega}_{u[21]}^{*})\in\operatorname{M}(\hat{M}_{u}\otimes\hat{M}_{u}).

(4.17)

The associated coboundary at the universal level is defined in an analogous way as the one at the reduced level, see (
4.2
). In fact:

Lemma 4.9
.

There exists a unique unitary coboundary
X_{\hat{\Omega},u}\in\operatorname{M}(C^{*}(\mathbb{G}))
between
\hat{\Omega}_{u}
and
\hat{\Omega}_{u,c}
such that

\hat{\pi}_{\mathrm{red}}(X_{\hat{\Omega},u})=X_{\hat{\Omega}}.

(4.18)

Proof.

Recall from (
2.5
) the universal unitary antipode
R_{\hat{Q}_{u}}
on
\hat{Q}_{u}
. If we then put

X_{\hat{\Omega},u}:=\hat{R}_{u,12}(1_{\hat{M}_{u}})^{*}=\hat{R}_{u,21}(1_{\hat{M}_{u}})\in\operatorname{M}(C^{*}(\mathbb{G})),

we find that

\begin{split}&\quad\;\hat{\Omega}_{u}\hat{\Delta}_{u,22}(X_{\hat{\Omega},u})=\hat{\Delta}_{u,12}(\hat{R}_{u,21}(1_{\hat{M}_{u}}))\\
&=(\hat{R}_{u,21}\otimes\hat{R}_{u,21})\hat{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op}}_{u,21}(1_{\hat{M}_{u}})=(\hat{R}_{u,21}\otimes\hat{R}_{u,21})(\hat{\Omega}^{*}_{u[21]})\\
&=(X_{\hat{\Omega},u}\otimes X_{\hat{\Omega},u})(\hat{R}_{u}\otimes\hat{R}_{u})(\hat{\Omega}_{u[21]}^{*})=(X_{\hat{\Omega},u}\otimes X_{\hat{\Omega},u})\hat{\Omega}_{u,c},\end{split}

i.e.
X_{\hat{\Omega},u}
is a coboundary between
\hat{\Omega}_{u}
and
\hat{\Omega}_{u,c}
. The formula (
4.18
) holds since
\pi^{\mathrm{red}}_{\hat{Q}_{u}}\colon C^{*}(\mathscr{G})\rightarrow W^{*}(\mathscr{G})
intertwines unitary antipodes.

To see that
X_{\hat{\Omega},u}
is uniquely determined by these conditions, let
\hat{\mathds{W}}\in\operatorname{M}(C^{*}(\mathbb{G})\otimes\mathcal{K}(L^{2}(\mathbb{G})))
be the half-lifted universal left multiplicative unitary for
\hat{\mathbb{G}}
. If then
Y\in\operatorname{M}(C^{*}(\mathbb{G}))
satisfies the conditions in the lemma, we see from the LCQG-analogon of (
2.8
) that

(X_{\hat{\Omega},u}\otimes X_{\hat{\Omega}})(\mathrm{id}\otimes\hat{\pi}_{\mathrm{red}})(\hat{\Omega}_{u,c})=(\mathrm{id}\otimes\hat{\pi}_{\mathrm{red}})(\hat{\Omega}_{u})\hat{\mathds{W}}^{*}(1\otimes X_{\hat{\Omega}})\hat{\mathds{W}}=(Y\otimes X_{\hat{\Omega}})(\mathrm{id}\otimes\hat{\pi}_{\mathrm{red}})(\hat{\Omega}_{u,c}),

showing that necessarily
Y=X_{\hat{\Omega},u}
.
∎

We let

\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathscr{G}}^{C^{*},\mathrm{triv}}\colon\mathrm{Rep}(C^{*}(\mathbb{G}))\rightarrow\mathrm{Rep}(C^{*}(\mathscr{G}))

(4.19)

be the functor given on objects by the extension of
\hat{\pi}_{U}\in\mathrm{Rep}(C^{*}(\mathbb{G}))
to a non-degenerate
*
-representation of
C^{*}(\mathscr{G})
on
\begin{pmatrix}\mathcal{H}\\
\mathcal{H}\end{pmatrix}
according to the trivialisation
C^{*}(\mathscr{G})\cong\begin{pmatrix}\hat{M}_{u}&\hat{M}_{u}\\
\hat{M}_{u}&\hat{M}_{u}\end{pmatrix}
. On morphisms,
\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathscr{G}}^{C^{*},\mathrm{triv}}
is given by
\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathscr{G}}^{C^{*},\mathrm{triv}}(T)=T\oplus T
. We let
\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathscr{G}}^{\mathrm{triv}}
be the associated functor
\mathrm{Rep}(\mathbb{G})\rightarrow\mathrm{Rep}(\mathscr{G})
.

Proposition 4.10
.

Consider
(\mathcal{H},U)
a unitary
\mathbb{G}
-representation, with associated
C^{*}(\mathbb{G})
-representation
\hat{\pi}_{U}
. The corresponding unitary left
\mathscr{G}
-representation
\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathscr{G}}^{\mathrm{triv}}(U)
is given by the unitaries

\displaystyle U_{22}^{*}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle U^{*},

(4.20)

\displaystyle U_{12}^{*}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle U^{*}(\hat{\pi}_{\mathrm{red}}\otimes\hat{\pi}_{U})(\hat{\Omega}_{u[21]}^{*}),

(4.21)

\displaystyle U_{21}^{*}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(u_{\mathbb{G}}\hat{\pi}_{\mathrm{red}}(-)u_{\mathbb{G}}^{*}\otimes\hat{\pi}_{U})((1\otimes X_{\hat{\Omega},u})\hat{\Omega}_{u,c})U^{*},

(4.22)

\displaystyle U_{11}^{*}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(u_{\mathbb{G}}\hat{\pi}_{\mathrm{red}}(-)u_{\mathbb{G}}^{*}\otimes\hat{\pi}_{U})((1\otimes X_{\hat{\Omega},u})\hat{\Omega}_{u,c})U^{*}(\hat{\pi}_{\mathrm{red}}\otimes\hat{\pi}_{U})(\hat{\Omega}_{u[21]}^{*}).

(4.23)

Proof.

Recall the notations (
2.8
) and (
2.9
) for the dual half-lifted universal multiplicative unitary for
\mathscr{G}
and its components. In our specific case it then follows that we have

\hat{\mathds{W}}_{22}(\mathrm{id}\otimes\hat{\pi}_{\mathrm{red}})(\hat{\Omega}_{u}^{*})=\hat{\mathds{W}}_{22}(\mathrm{id}\otimes\hat{\pi}_{\mathrm{red}})\hat{\Delta}_{u,21}(1_{\hat{M}_{u}})=\hat{\mathds{W}}_{12}.

Similarly, using that
(R_{\hat{Q}_{u}}\otimes R_{Q})\mathds{W}_{\hat{Q}_{u}}=\mathds{W}_{\hat{Q}_{u}}
, where
R_{Q}(x)=J_{\hat{Q}}x^{*}J_{\hat{Q}}
for
x\in Q
and the universal analogon of (
4.6
), we obtain

\begin{split}&\quad\;\hat{\mathds{W}}_{21}=(\hat{R}_{u,21}\otimes\hat{J}(-)^{*}\hat{J})\hat{\mathds{W}}_{12}=(X_{\hat{\Omega},u}\otimes 1)(\hat{R}_{u,22}\otimes\hat{J}(-)^{*}\hat{J})(\hat{\mathds{W}}_{22}(\mathrm{id}\otimes\hat{\pi}_{\mathrm{red}})(\hat{\Omega}_{u}^{*}))\\
&=(X_{\hat{\Omega},u}\otimes 1)(\mathrm{id}\otimes u_{\mathbb{G}}\hat{\pi}_{\mathrm{red}}(-)u_{\mathbb{G}}^{*})((\hat{R}_{u,22}\otimes\hat{R}_{u,22})\hat{\Omega}_{u}^{*})\hat{\mathds{W}}_{22}=(X_{\hat{\Omega},u}\otimes 1)(\mathrm{id}\otimes u_{\mathbb{G}}\hat{\pi}_{\mathrm{red}}(-)u_{\mathbb{G}}^{*})(\hat{\Omega}_{u,c[21]})\hat{\mathds{W}}_{22}.\end{split}

The formula

\hat{\mathds{W}}_{11}=(X_{\hat{\Omega},u}\otimes 1)(\mathrm{id}\otimes u_{\mathbb{G}}\hat{\pi}_{\mathrm{red}}(-)u_{\mathbb{G}}^{*})(\hat{\Omega}_{u,c[21]})\hat{\mathds{W}}_{22}(\mathrm{id}\otimes\hat{\pi}_{\mathrm{red}})(\hat{\Omega}_{u}^{*})

follows completely similarly. The proposition now follows from (
2.7
).
∎

We can now also show the triviality of induction on the level of induced
\mathbb{G}
-representations. Let us write
\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}^{\mathrm{triv}}
for the W
∗
-functor

\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}^{\mathrm{triv}}\colon\mathrm{Rep}(\mathbb{G})\rightarrow\mathrm{Rep}(\mathbb{H}),\qquad\mathrm{Rep}(\mathbb{G})\ni(\mathcal{H},U)\mapsto(\mathcal{H},\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathscr{G}}^{\mathrm{triv}}(U)_{11})\in\mathrm{Rep}(\mathbb{H}),

with monoidal coherence through (
4.11
). On morphisms
\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}^{\mathrm{triv}}
is defined as the identity. Observe that
\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}^{\mathrm{triv}}
corresponds to
\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}^{C^{*},\mathrm{triv}}
under the identifications
\mathrm{Rep}(\mathbb{G})\cong\mathrm{Rep}(C^{*}(\mathbb{G}))
,
\mathrm{Rep}(\mathbb{H})\cong\mathrm{Rep}(C^{*}(\mathbb{H}))
.

Proposition 4.11
.

We have a monoidal unitary natural transformation of tensor W
∗
-functors
\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}^{\mathrm{triv}}\cong\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}
via

\theta_{(\mathcal{H},\hat{\pi}_{U})}\mathcal{O}^{*}_{(\mathcal{H},\hat{\pi}_{U})}\colon\mathcal{H}\cong\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}(\mathcal{H}),\qquad\xi\mapsto U^{*}(\hat{\pi}_{\mathrm{red}}\otimes\hat{\pi}_{U})(\hat{\Omega}_{u[21]}^{*})(1\otimes\xi)

(4.24)

for
(\mathcal{H},U)\in\mathrm{Rep}(\mathbb{G})
.

Proof.

We already know that the family of unitary operators
\theta_{(\mathcal{H},\hat{\pi}_{U})}\mathcal{O}^{*}_{(\mathcal{H},\hat{\pi}_{U})}
establishes a monoidal unitary natural transformation
\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}^{\mathrm{triv}}\rightarrow\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}
(see Lemma
3.8
, the discussion after Remark
4.5
, Lemma
4.6
and Proposition
4.8
).

It is left to check the expression (
4.24
). Take
a=(\mathrm{id}\otimes\omega_{\eta,\eta^{\prime}})(\hat{\mathds{W}}_{21})\in C^{*}(\mathbb{X})
for
\eta,\eta^{\prime}\in L^{2}(\mathbb{G})
and
\xi\in\mathcal{H}
. Denote by
\hat{\pi}_{U,ij}
the
*
-representation of
C^{*}(\mathbb{G}_{ij})
corresponding to
U_{ij}=\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathscr{G}}(U)_{ij}
. We then calculate, using equations (
3.9
) and (
4.21
),

\begin{split}&\quad\;\theta_{(\mathcal{H},\hat{\pi}_{U})}\mathcal{O}^{*}_{(\mathcal{H},\hat{\pi}_{U})}(\hat{\pi}_{U}(a)\xi)=\theta_{(\mathcal{H},\hat{\pi}_{U})}(a\otimes\xi)=\hat{\pi}_{U,12}(a)\xi=\hat{\pi}_{U,12}((\omega_{\eta,\eta^{\prime}}\otimes\mathrm{id})(\hat{\mathds{W}}_{21[21]}))\xi\\
&=(\omega_{\eta,\eta^{\prime}}\otimes\mathrm{id})\bigl((\mathrm{id}\otimes\hat{\pi}_{U,21})(\hat{\mathds{W}}_{21[21]}^{*})^{*}\bigr)\xi=(\omega_{\eta,\eta^{\prime}}\otimes\mathrm{id})(U_{21}^{*})\xi=(\eta^{*}\otimes\mathrm{id})(U_{21}^{*}(\eta^{\prime}\otimes\xi))\\
&=(\eta^{*}\otimes\mathrm{id})((\Delta^{1}_{22}\otimes\mathrm{id})(U^{*})(\eta^{\prime}\otimes 1\otimes\xi))=(\eta^{*}\otimes\mathrm{id})(U_{12[23]}^{*}U_{21[13]}^{*}(\eta^{\prime}\otimes 1\otimes\xi))\\
&=(\mathrm{id}\otimes\hat{\pi}_{U,21})(\hat{\mathds{W}}_{12[21]})(1\otimes\hat{\pi}_{U,12}((\omega_{\eta,\eta^{\prime}}\otimes\mathrm{id})\hat{\mathds{W}}_{21[21]})\xi)=(\mathrm{id}\otimes\hat{\pi}_{U})(\hat{\mathds{W}}_{12[21]})(1\otimes\hat{\pi}_{U}(a)\xi)\\
&=U^{*}(\hat{\pi}_{\mathrm{red}}\otimes\hat{\pi}_{U})(\hat{\Omega}^{*}_{u[21]})(1\otimes\hat{\pi}_{U}(a)\xi)\end{split}

Since
\hat{\pi}_{U}
is non-degenerate, this ends the proof.
∎

4.3.
Modular conjugation for induced actions

Assume again that
\mathscr{G}=\begin{pmatrix}\mathbb{H}&\mathbb{X}\\
\mathbb{Y}&\mathbb{G}\end{pmatrix}
has trivial universal linking C
∗
-algebra, with the associated universal dual unitary
2
-cocycle
\hat{\Omega}_{u}
for
\mathbb{G}
.

Assume that
(A,\gamma)
is a
\mathbb{G}
-W
∗
-algebra. By Theorem
3.9
and the identification (
4.24
), we can canonically identify
L^{2}(A)
as a standard Hilbert space for
\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}(A)
.

Theorem 4.12
.

The modular conjugation map for the above standard representation of
\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}(A)
on
L^{2}(A)
is given by

J_{\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}(A)}\colon L^{2}(A)\rightarrow L^{2}(A),\qquad J_{\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}(A)}=\hat{\pi}_{U_{\gamma}}(X_{\hat{\Omega},u})J_{A}.

(4.25)

Proof.

We will write
U_{\gamma,ij}
for the components of the trivial induction
\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathscr{G}}^{\mathrm{triv}}(U_{\gamma})
and use its description from Proposition
4.10
.

Take
\xi\in L^{2}(\mathbb{G})\otimes L^{2}(A)
,
x,z\in\hat{M}
and
a,b\in A
and observe that

(\Delta^{1}_{22}\otimes\mathrm{id})(\gamma(a)U_{\gamma}^{*})=U_{\gamma,12[23]}^{*}U_{\gamma,21[13]}^{*}a_{[3]}.

Lemma
3.10
gives us a formula involving the action of
J_{\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}(A)}
on
\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}(L^{2}(A))\cong_{I}L^{2}(\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}(A))
:

\begin{split}&\quad\;(J_{\hat{M}}\otimes J_{\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}(A)})\bigl(\hat{\pi}_{12}^{1}(x)_{[1]}U_{\gamma[23]}^{*}(\hat{\pi}_{\mathrm{red}}\otimes\hat{\pi}_{U_{\gamma}})(\hat{\Omega}^{*}_{u[21]})_{[23]}U^{*}_{\gamma,21[13]}a_{[3]}((J_{\hat{M}}\otimes J_{A})(\hat{\pi}_{12}^{2}(z)\otimes 1)\gamma(b)\xi)_{[13]}\bigr)\\
&=\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}(U_{\gamma})\hat{\pi}_{12}^{1}(z)_{[1]}U_{\gamma[23]}^{*}(\hat{\pi}_{\mathrm{red}}\otimes\!\hat{\pi}_{U_{\gamma}})(\hat{\Omega}^{*}_{u[21]})_{[23]}U^{*}_{\gamma,21[13]}b_{[3]}((J_{\hat{M}}\otimes J_{A})(\hat{\pi}_{12}^{2}(x)\otimes\!1)\gamma(a)U_{\gamma}^{*}(J_{\hat{M}}\otimes\!J_{A})\xi)_{[13]}.\end{split}

Next we use the identification

L^{2}(A)\ni\eta\mapsto U^{*}_{\gamma}(\hat{\pi}_{\mathrm{red}}\otimes\hat{\pi}_{U_{\gamma}})(\hat{\Omega}^{*}_{u[21]})(1\otimes\eta)\in\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}(L^{2}(A))

from Proposition
4.11
and set
a=b=1,x=z=1
:

(J_{\hat{M}}\otimes J_{\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}(A)})U^{*}_{\gamma,21}(J_{\hat{M}}\otimes J_{A})\xi=U_{\gamma,11}U^{*}_{\gamma,21}(J_{\hat{M}}\otimes J_{A})U_{\gamma}^{*}(J_{\hat{M}}\otimes J_{A})\xi=U_{\gamma,11}U^{*}_{\gamma,21}U_{\gamma}\xi

(we are abusing notation and now consider
J_{\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}(A)}
as acting on
L^{2}(A)
). Note that we have used
U_{\gamma}=(J_{\hat{M}}\otimes J_{A})U_{\gamma}^{*}(J_{\hat{M}}\otimes J_{A})

[
Vae01
]
*Proposition 3.7. Using again the formulas from Proposition
4.10
gives

(J_{\hat{M}}\otimes J_{\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}(A)})u_{\mathbb{G}[1]}(\hat{\pi}_{\mathrm{red}}\otimes\hat{\pi}_{U_{\gamma}})((1\otimes X_{\hat{\Omega},u})\hat{\Omega}_{u,c})u_{\mathbb{G}[1]}U_{\gamma}^{*}(J_{\hat{M}}\otimes J_{A})=(\hat{\pi}_{\mathrm{red}}\otimes\hat{\pi}_{U_{\gamma}})(\hat{\Omega}_{u[21]})U_{\gamma}.

An easy simplification using
J_{A}\hat{\pi}_{U_{\gamma}}(x)^{*}J_{A}=\hat{\pi}_{U_{\gamma}}(\hat{R}_{u}(x))
for
x\in C^{*}(\mathbb{G})
(
[
Vae01
]
*Proposition 3.7) gives

J_{\hat{M}}\otimes J_{\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}(A)}=(J_{\hat{M}}\otimes J_{A})\hat{\pi}_{U_{\gamma}}(X_{\hat{\Omega},u})_{[2]}^{*}.

Taking adjoints and slicing off the first leg ends the proof.
∎

5.
Application to braided tensor products

5.1.
Linking quantum groupoid for a bicharacter

We resume the setting discussed at the end of Section
1.5
. So,
\mathbb{G}_{1}=(M_{1},\Delta_{1}),\mathbb{G}_{2}=(M_{2},\Delta_{2})
are two LCQG, and we put

\mathbb{G}=\mathbb{G}_{1}\times\mathbb{G}_{2}=(M,\Delta)=(M_{1}\bar{\otimes}M_{2},\Delta_{\otimes}).

Then

\hat{\mathbb{G}}=\hat{\mathbb{G}}_{1}\times\hat{\mathbb{G}}_{2}=(\hat{M},\hat{\Delta})=(\hat{M}_{1}\bar{\otimes}\hat{M}_{2},\hat{\Delta}_{\otimes}).

Lemma 5.1
.

The universal group C
∗
-algebra of
\mathbb{G}=\mathbb{G}_{1}\times\mathbb{G}_{2}
is given by

(\hat{M}_{u},\hat{\Delta}_{u}),\qquad\hat{M}_{u}=C^{*}(\mathbb{G}_{1}\times\mathbb{G}_{2})=\hat{M}_{1,u}\otimes_{\max}\hat{M}_{2,u},

where

\hat{\Delta}_{u}(x\otimes y)=\hat{\Delta}_{1,u}(x)_{[13]}\hat{\Delta}_{2,u}(y)_{[24]}\in\operatorname{M}((\hat{M}_{1,u}\otimes_{\max}\hat{M}_{2,u})\otimes(\hat{M}_{1,u}\otimes_{\max}\hat{M}_{2,u})).

Proof.

As explained in
[
Kra21
]
*Proposition 13.32, we can identify

C^{*}(\mathbb{G})=C^{*}(\mathbb{G}_{1})\otimes_{\max}C^{*}(\mathbb{G}_{2}),\qquad\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{W}$}}\>\!_{\mathbb{G}}=\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{W}$}}\>\!_{\mathbb{G}_{1}[13]}\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{W}$}}\>\!_{\mathbb{G}_{2}[24]}

(with leg numbering notation in
(C_{0}(\mathbb{G}_{1})\otimes C_{0}(\mathbb{G}_{2}))\otimes(C^{*}(\mathbb{G}_{1})\otimes_{\max}C^{*}(\mathbb{G}_{2}))
). Consequently

\begin{split}(\mathrm{id}\otimes\mathrm{id}\otimes\hat{\Delta}_{u})(\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{W}$}}\>\!_{\mathbb{G}_{1}[13]}\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{W}$}}\>\!_{\mathbb{G}_{2}[24]})&=(\mathrm{id}\otimes\hat{\Delta}_{u})\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{W}$}}\>\!_{\mathbb{G}}=\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{W}$}}\>\!_{\mathbb{G}[1256]}\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{W}$}}\>\!_{\mathbb{G}[1234]}=\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{W}$}}\>\!_{\mathbb{G}_{1}[15]}\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{W}$}}\>\!_{\mathbb{G}_{2}[26]}\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{W}$}}\>\!_{\mathbb{G}_{1}[13]}\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{W}$}}\>\!_{\mathbb{G}_{2}[24]}\\
&=(\mathrm{id}\otimes\hat{\Delta}_{1,u})(\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{W}$}}\>\!_{\mathbb{G}_{1}})_{[135]}(\mathrm{id}\otimes\hat{\Delta}_{2,u})(\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{W}$}}\>\!_{\mathbb{G}_{2}})_{[246]}\end{split}

and the claim follows.
∎

It is then also easily shown that the associated reduced group C
∗
-algebra is

C^{*}_{\mathrm{red}}(\mathbb{G}_{1}\times\mathbb{G}_{2})=C^{*}_{\mathrm{red}}(\mathbb{G}_{1})\otimes C^{*}_{\mathrm{red}}(\mathbb{G}_{2})

with
\hat{\pi}_{\mathrm{red}}\colon C^{*}(\mathbb{G}_{1})\otimes_{\max}C^{*}(\mathbb{G}_{2})\rightarrow C^{*}_{\mathrm{red}}(\mathbb{G}_{1})\otimes C^{*}_{\mathrm{red}}(\mathbb{G}_{2})
being the composition of
\hat{\pi}_{1,\mathrm{red}}\otimes_{\max}\hat{\pi}_{2,\mathrm{red}}
with the tensor product quotient map. The universal unitary antipode of
\mathbb{G}_{1}\times\mathbb{G}_{2}
is

\hat{R}_{u}=\hat{R}_{1,u}\otimes_{\max}\hat{R}_{2,u}

(
[
Kus01
]
*Proposition 9.3).

Assume now that we are given a unitary skew bicharacter

\widehat{\mathcal{X}}\in\hat{M}_{1}\bar{\otimes}\hat{M}_{2},

with associated unitary
2
-cocycle

\hat{\Omega}:=\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{[32]}\in\hat{M}\bar{\otimes}\hat{M}=(\hat{M}_{1}\bar{\otimes}\hat{M}_{2})\bar{\otimes}(\hat{M}_{1}\bar{\otimes}\hat{M}_{2}).

(5.1)

for
(\hat{M},\hat{\Delta})
. It can be shown that
\widehat{\mathcal{X}}
lifts (uniquely) to a unitary skew bicharacter
\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{u}\in\operatorname{M}(\hat{M}_{1,u}\otimes\hat{M}_{2,u})

[
Kus01
]
*Section 6 (see also
[
Daw16
]
*Section 2.1). In particular, we can make sense of the unitary
2
-cocycle

\hat{\Omega}_{u}:=\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{u[32]}\in\operatorname{M}((\hat{M}_{1,u}\otimes_{\max}\hat{M}_{2,u})\otimes(\hat{M}_{1,u}\otimes_{\max}\hat{M}_{2,u}))=\operatorname{M}(\hat{M}_{u}\otimes\hat{M}_{u}).

In the next theorem, we use the terminology as introduced in Definition
4.3
.

Theorem 5.2
.

The unitary
2
-cocycle
\hat{\Omega}
for
(\hat{M}_{1}\bar{\otimes}\hat{M}_{2},\hat{\Delta}_{\otimes})
admits
\hat{\Omega}_{u}
as a universal lift.

Although this statement looks quite innocuous, it is not at all clear to us how to obtain it in a straightforward way. The proof we offer is quite technical, so we relegate it to the separate subsection
5.4
. Note that the theorem seems non-trivial, even in the case when
\hat{\mathbb{G}}_{1}
and
\hat{\mathbb{G}}_{2}
are both coamenable!

Consider now the conjugate universal unitary
2
-cocycle
\hat{\Omega}_{u,c}
defined by (
4.17
).

Lemma 5.3
.

We have

\hat{\Omega}_{u,c}=\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{u[14]}^{*}.

Proof.

This follows immediately from the definition (
4.17
) and the fact that

\hat{R}_{u}=\hat{R}_{1,u}\otimes_{\max}\hat{R}_{2,u},\qquad(\hat{R}_{1,u}\otimes\hat{R}_{2,u})\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{u}=\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{u}.

∎

Consider now the associated universal element as in Lemma
4.9
, so

X_{\hat{\Omega},u}\in\operatorname{M}(C^{*}(\mathbb{G}))=\operatorname{M}(C^{*}(\mathbb{G}_{1})\otimes_{\max}C^{*}(\mathbb{G}_{2})).

Lemma 5.4
.

Under the quotient map

\hat{\theta}\colon C^{*}(\mathbb{G}_{1})\otimes_{\max}C^{*}(\mathbb{G}_{2})\rightarrow C^{*}(\mathbb{G}_{1})\otimes C^{*}(\mathbb{G}_{2}),

we have that

X_{\hat{\Omega},u}\mapsto\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{u}^{*}.

Proof.

The maps

\begin{split}\hat{\pi}_{1}=\mathrm{id}_{C^{*}(\mathbb{G}_{1})}\times\hat{\varepsilon}_{2}&\colon C^{*}(\mathbb{G})\rightarrow C^{*}(\mathbb{G}_{1}),\qquad x_{1}\otimes x_{2}\mapsto\hat{\varepsilon}_{2}(x_{2})x_{1},\qquad\forall x_{1}\in C^{*}(\mathbb{G}_{1}),x_{2}\in C^{*}(\mathbb{G}_{2}),\\
\hat{\pi}_{2}=\hat{\varepsilon}_{1}\times\mathrm{id}_{C^{*}(\mathbb{G}_{2})}&\colon C^{*}(\mathbb{G})\rightarrow C^{*}(\mathbb{G}_{2}),\qquad x_{1}\otimes x_{2}\mapsto\hat{\varepsilon}_{1}(x_{1})x_{2},\qquad\forall x_{1}\in C^{*}(\mathbb{G}_{1}),x_{2}\in C^{*}(\mathbb{G}_{2})\end{split}

define homomorphisms of universal linking quantum groupoids

\hat{\pi}_{i}^{(2)}=\begin{pmatrix}\hat{\pi}_{i}&\hat{\pi}_{i}\\
\hat{\pi}_{i}&\hat{\pi}_{i}\end{pmatrix}\colon C^{*}(\mathscr{G})\rightarrow M_{2}(C^{*}(\mathbb{G}_{i})),

where the right hands side is equipped with the trivial linking quantum groupoid structure
\begin{pmatrix}\hat{\Delta}_{i,u}&\hat{\Delta}_{i,u}\\
\hat{\Delta}_{i,u}&\hat{\Delta}_{i,u}\end{pmatrix}
. By the linking quantum groupoid analogon of
[
KV00
]
*Proposition 5.45 (see also
[
Kus01
]
*Remark 12.1), we get that

\hat{\pi}_{i}\circ\hat{R}_{u,kl}=\hat{R}_{i,u}\circ\hat{\pi}_{i},\qquad\forall i,k,l\in\{1,2\}.

Now since the coproduct of
C^{*}(\mathscr{G})
is obtained by cocycle twisting with
\hat{\Omega}_{u}=\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{u[32]}
, we see that

\hat{\theta}=(\hat{\pi}_{1}\otimes\hat{\pi}_{2})\hat{\Delta}_{u,12}.

It follows that

\begin{split}\hat{\theta}(X_{\hat{\Omega},u})=(\hat{\pi}_{1}\otimes\hat{\pi}_{2})\hat{\Delta}_{u,12}(\hat{R}_{u,21}(1))&=(\hat{\pi}_{1}\otimes\hat{\pi}_{2})(\hat{R}_{u,21}\otimes\hat{R}_{u,21})\hat{\Delta}_{u,21}^{\mathrm{op}}(1)=(\hat{R}_{1,u}\otimes\hat{R}_{2,u})(\hat{\pi}_{1}\otimes\hat{\pi}_{2})\hat{\Delta}_{u,21}^{\mathrm{op}}(1)\\
&=(\hat{R}_{1,u}\otimes\hat{R}_{2,u})(\hat{\pi}_{1}\otimes\hat{\pi}_{2})(\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{u[14]}^{*})=(\hat{R}_{1,u}\otimes\hat{R}_{2,u})(\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{u}^{*})=\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{u}^{*}.\end{split}

∎

In the following, we write again

W^{*}(\mathscr{G})=\begin{pmatrix}W^{*}(\mathbb{H})&W^{*}(\mathbb{X})\\
W^{*}(\mathbb{Y})&W^{*}(\mathbb{G})\end{pmatrix}

for the linking quantum groupoid
\mathscr{G}
associated to the cocycle
\hat{\Omega}
. In particular, we can consider the associated right Galois object
(L^{\infty}(\mathbb{X}),\alpha_{\mathbb{X}})
as in Section
1.4
.

Lemma 5.5
.

The modular conjugation of
N=L^{\infty}(\mathbb{X})
is
J_{N}=(J_{1}\otimes J_{2})\widehat{\mathcal{X}}
.

Proof.

According to (
4.4
), for
a\in\{1,2\}
we have

\hat{\mathrm{W}}^{a}_{11}=(J_{N}\otimes J_{\hat{M}})\hat{\Omega}\hat{\mathrm{W}}^{*}(J_{M}\otimes J_{\hat{M}})\hat{\Omega}^{*}.

(5.2)

Recall that
W^{*}(\mathbb{H})=\hat{M}_{1}\bar{\otimes}\hat{M}_{2}
with comultiplication
\hat{\Omega}\Delta_{\hat{M}}(\cdot)\hat{\Omega}^{*}
; see Section
1.5
. This means, that
\hat{\mathbb{H}}
can be also constructed as a double crossed product, for quantum groups
\mathbb{G}_{1}^{op},\mathbb{G}_{2}
and matching
\operatorname{Ad}(\widehat{\mathcal{X}})
(
[
BV05
]
*Definition 3.1). After small simplifications, Theorem 5.3 in
[
BV05
]
gives

\hat{\mathrm{W}}^{a}_{11}=\hat{\mathrm{W}}_{1[13]}\bigl(\widehat{\mathcal{X}}(\hat{J}_{1}\otimes\hat{J}_{2})\widehat{\mathcal{X}}(\hat{J}_{1}\otimes\hat{J}_{2})\bigr)^{*}_{[34]}\hat{\mathrm{W}}_{2[24]}\bigl(\widehat{\mathcal{X}}(\hat{J}_{1}\otimes\hat{J}_{2})\widehat{\mathcal{X}}(\hat{J}_{1}\otimes\hat{J}_{2})\bigr)_{[34]}

which can be manipulated to

\hat{\mathrm{W}}^{a}_{11}=(J_{1}\otimes J_{2}\otimes\hat{J}_{1}\otimes\hat{J}_{2})\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{[12]}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{[32]}\hat{\mathrm{W}}_{1[13]}^{*}\hat{\mathrm{W}}_{2[24]}^{*}(J_{1}\otimes J_{2}\otimes\hat{J}_{1}\otimes\hat{J}_{2})\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{[32]}^{*}.

(5.3)

Comparing expressions (
5.2
) and (
5.3
) leads to the claim
J_{N}=(J_{1}\otimes J_{2})\widehat{\mathcal{X}}
.
∎

For
i\in\{1,2\}
, the locally compact quantum group
\mathbb{G}_{i}
acts on
L^{\infty}(\mathbb{G}_{i})
by the translation action, i.e. via comultiplication. The bicharacter
\widehat{\mathcal{X}}\in L^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}}_{1})\bar{\otimes}L^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}}_{2})
then allows us to define the braided tensor product
L^{\infty}(\mathbb{G}_{1})\overline{\boxtimes}L^{\infty}(\mathbb{G}_{2})
(see the Introduction). Let us denote the generators in this specific case by
\varpi_{\boxtimes,1}(x_{1}),\varpi_{\boxtimes,2}(x_{2})\,(x_{1}\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{G}_{1}),x_{2}\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{G}_{2}))
, in order to avoid confusion with the generators of
A_{1}\overline{\boxtimes}A_{2}
later on.

Lemma 5.6
.

We have an isomorphism

\Theta_{\boxtimes}\colon L^{\infty}(\mathbb{G}_{1})\overline{\boxtimes}L^{\infty}(\mathbb{G}_{2})\rightarrow L^{\infty}(\mathbb{X})\colon z\mapsto\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{*}z\widehat{\mathcal{X}}

which acts on generators via

\varpi_{\boxtimes,1}(x_{1})\mapsto x_{1}\otimes 1,\quad\varpi_{\boxtimes,2}(x_{2})\mapsto\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{*}(1\otimes x_{2})\widehat{\mathcal{X}},\qquad\forall x_{i}\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{G}_{i}).

Furthermore

\alpha_{\mathbb{X}}(\Theta_{\boxtimes}\varpi_{\boxtimes,1}(x_{1}))=(\Theta_{\boxtimes}\varpi_{\boxtimes,1}\otimes\mathrm{id})\Delta_{1}(x_{1})_{[123]},\quad\alpha_{\mathbb{X}}(\Theta_{\boxtimes}\varpi_{\boxtimes,2}(x_{2}))=(\Theta_{\boxtimes}\varpi_{\boxtimes,2}\otimes\mathrm{id})\Delta_{2}(x_{2})_{[124]}

(5.4)

for
x_{1}\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{G}_{1}),x_{2}\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{G}_{2})
.

Proof.

We first compute that

\begin{split}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{[13]}\mathrm{W}_{2[23]}&=\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{[12]}^{*}(\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{[12]}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{[13]}\mathrm{W}_{2[23]})=\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{[12]}^{*}\hat{\mathrm{W}}_{2[32]}^{*}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{[12]}\hat{\mathrm{W}}_{2[32]}\mathrm{W}_{2[23]}=\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{[12]}^{*}\mathrm{W}_{2[23]}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{[12]}.\end{split}

(5.5)

Using (
4.3
) with
k\in\{1,2\}
, adjoint of (
5.5
) and the fact that the space
L^{\infty}(\mathbb{X})\subseteq\mathcal{B}(L^{2}(\mathbb{G}_{1})\otimes L^{2}(\mathbb{G}_{2}))
is closed under adjoints, we can write it as

\displaystyle L^{\infty}(\mathbb{X})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\{(\mathrm{id}\otimes\mathrm{id}\otimes\omega_{1}\otimes\omega_{2})(\hat{\mathrm{W}}^{k*}_{12[3412]})\mid\omega_{i}\in\mathcal{B}(L^{2}(\mathbb{G}_{i}))_{*}\}^{\prime\prime}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\{(\mathrm{id}\otimes\mathrm{id}\otimes\omega_{1}\otimes\omega_{2})(\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{[14]}\mathrm{W}_{1[13]}\mathrm{W}_{2[24]})\mid\omega_{i}\in\mathcal{B}(L^{2}(\mathbb{G}_{i}))_{*}\}^{\prime\prime}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\{(\mathrm{id}\otimes\mathrm{id}\otimes\omega_{2})(\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{[13]}\mathrm{W}_{2[23]})((\mathrm{id}\otimes\omega_{1})(\mathrm{W}_{1})\otimes 1)\mid\omega_{i}\in\mathcal{B}(L^{2}(\mathbb{G}_{i}))_{*}\}^{\prime\prime}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\{((\mathrm{id}\otimes\omega_{1})(\mathrm{W}_{1}^{*})\otimes 1)(\mathrm{id}\otimes\mathrm{id}\otimes\omega_{2})(\mathrm{W}_{2[23]}^{*}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{[13]}^{*})\mid\omega_{i}\in\mathcal{B}(L^{2}(\mathbb{G}_{i}))_{*}\}^{\prime\prime}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle((L^{\infty}(\mathbb{G}_{1})\otimes 1)\cup\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{*}(1\otimes L^{\infty}(\mathbb{G}_{2}))\widehat{\mathcal{X}})^{\prime\prime}

Comparing with (
0.8
), we see that this is just a conjugation of
L^{\infty}(\mathbb{G}_{1})\overline{\boxtimes}L^{\infty}(\mathbb{G}_{2})
by
\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{*}
, and we find the concrete isomorphism

\Theta_{\boxtimes}\colon L^{\infty}(\mathbb{G}_{1})\overline{\boxtimes}L^{\infty}(\mathbb{G}_{2})\cong L^{\infty}(\mathbb{X}),\quad\varpi_{\boxtimes,1}(x_{1})\mapsto x_{1}\otimes 1,\quad\varpi_{\boxtimes,2}(x_{2})\mapsto\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{*}(1\otimes x_{2})\widehat{\mathcal{X}},\qquad\forall x_{i}\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{G}_{i}).

Take
x_{1}=(\omega_{1}\otimes\mathrm{id})\hat{\mathrm{W}}_{1},x_{2}=(\omega_{2}\otimes\mathrm{id})\hat{\mathrm{W}}_{2}
for
\omega_{1}\in\mathcal{B}(L^{2}(\mathbb{G}_{1}))_{*},\omega_{2}\in\mathcal{B}(L^{2}(\mathbb{G}_{2}))_{*}
. We will prove

\alpha_{\mathbb{X}}\Theta_{\boxtimes}\bigl(\varpi_{\boxtimes,1}(x_{1})\varpi_{\boxtimes,2}(x_{2})\bigr)=(\Theta_{\boxtimes}\varpi_{\boxtimes,1}\otimes\mathrm{id})\Delta_{1}(x_{1})_{[123]}\,(\Theta_{\boxtimes}\varpi_{\boxtimes,2}\otimes\mathrm{id})\Delta_{2}(x_{2})_{[124]},

(5.6)

from which (
5.4
) follows by a limit argument. First observe that using
\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{*}_{[13]}\hat{\mathrm{W}}_{2[23]}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{[13]}=\hat{\mathrm{W}}_{2[23]}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{*}_{[12]}
we have

\begin{split}\Theta_{\boxtimes}\bigl(\varpi_{\boxtimes,1}(x_{1})\varpi_{\boxtimes,2}(x_{2})\bigr)&=\bigl((\omega_{1}\otimes\mathrm{id})\hat{\mathrm{W}}_{1}\otimes 1\bigr)\,\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{*}\bigl(1\otimes(\omega_{2}\otimes\mathrm{id})\hat{\mathrm{W}}_{2}\bigr)\widehat{\mathcal{X}}\\
&=(\omega_{1}\otimes\omega_{2}\otimes\mathrm{id}\otimes\mathrm{id})\bigl(\hat{\mathrm{W}}_{1[13]}\hat{\mathrm{W}}_{2[24]}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{*}_{[32]}\bigr).\end{split}

Consequently, using (
1.7
) and (
4.3
)

\begin{split}&\quad\;\alpha_{\mathbb{X}}\Theta_{\boxtimes}\bigl(\varpi_{\boxtimes,1}(x_{1})\varpi_{\boxtimes,2}(x_{2})\bigr)=(\omega_{1}\otimes\omega_{2}\otimes\Delta_{12}^{2})(\hat{\mathrm{W}}_{1[13]}\hat{\mathrm{W}}_{2[24]}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{*}_{[32]})\\
&=(\omega_{1}\otimes\omega_{2}\otimes\mathrm{id}^{\otimes 4})(\hat{\mathrm{W}}_{1[15]}\hat{\mathrm{W}}_{2[26]}\hat{\mathrm{W}}_{1[13]}\hat{\mathrm{W}}_{2[24]}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{*}_{[32]})\\
&=(\omega_{1}\otimes\mathrm{id}^{\otimes 4})(\hat{\mathrm{W}}_{1[14]}\hat{\mathrm{W}}_{1[12]})\,(\omega_{2}\otimes\mathrm{id}^{\otimes 4})(\hat{\mathrm{W}}_{2[15]}\hat{\mathrm{W}}_{2[13]}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{*}_{[21]})\\
&=(\Theta_{\boxtimes}\varpi_{\boxtimes,1}\otimes\mathrm{id})\Delta_{1}((\omega_{1}\otimes\mathrm{id})\hat{\mathrm{W}}_{1})_{[123]}\,(\Theta_{\boxtimes}\varpi_{\boxtimes,2}\otimes\mathrm{id})\Delta_{2}((\omega_{2}\otimes\mathrm{id})\hat{\mathrm{W}}_{2})_{[124]}\\
&=(\Theta_{\boxtimes}\varpi_{\boxtimes,1}\otimes\mathrm{id})\Delta_{1}(x_{1})_{[123]}\,(\Theta_{\boxtimes}\varpi_{\boxtimes,2}\otimes\mathrm{id})\Delta_{2}(x_{2})_{[124]}.\end{split}

This shows (
5.6
) and ends the proof.
∎

Fix left
\mathbb{G}_{i}
-W
∗
-algebras
(A_{i},\gamma_{i})
and equip
A_{1}\bar{\otimes}A_{2}
with the left
\mathbb{G}_{1}\times\mathbb{G}_{2}
action

\gamma_{\otimes}(a_{1}\otimes a_{2})=\gamma_{1}(a_{1})_{[13]}\gamma_{2}(a_{2})_{[24]}

for
a_{1}\in A_{1},a_{2}\in A_{2}
. We want to obtain an alternative description of the braided tensor product
A_{1}\overline{\boxtimes}A_{2}
as the induced algebra
\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}(A_{1}\bar{\otimes}A_{2})
(see Theorem
5.10
).

Lemma 5.7
.

We have an inclusion of von Neumann algebras

\bigl((\Theta_{\boxtimes}\varpi_{\boxtimes,1}\otimes\mathrm{id})(\gamma_{1}(A_{1}))_{[123]}\cup(\Theta_{\boxtimes}\varpi_{\boxtimes,2}\otimes\mathrm{id})(\gamma_{2}(A_{2}))_{[124]}\bigr)^{\prime\prime}\subseteq\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}(A_{1}\bar{\otimes}A_{2})

(5.7)

inside
\mathcal{B}(L^{2}(\mathbb{G}_{1})\otimes L^{2}(\mathbb{G}_{2})\otimes L^{2}(A_{1})\otimes L^{2}(A_{2}))
.

Proof.

By definition, we have

\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}(A_{1}\bar{\otimes}A_{2})=\{z\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{X})\bar{\otimes}A_{1}\bar{\otimes}A_{2}\mid(\alpha_{\mathbb{X}}\otimes\mathrm{id}\otimes\mathrm{id})z=(\mathrm{id}\otimes\mathrm{id}\otimes\gamma_{\otimes})z\}.

(5.8)

The claim now follows immediately from Lemma
5.6
.
∎

Next we want to show that (
5.7
) is an equality. It will be convenient to introduce an auxilliary notation for the left hand side:

\mathcal{M}=\bigl((\Theta_{\boxtimes}\varpi_{\boxtimes,1}\otimes\mathrm{id})(\gamma_{1}(A_{1}))_{[123]}\cup(\Theta_{\boxtimes}\varpi_{\boxtimes,2}\otimes\mathrm{id})(\gamma_{2}(A_{2}))_{[124]}\bigr)^{\prime\prime}.

(5.9)

For future reference, let us note that generators of
\mathcal{M}
are

(\Theta_{\boxtimes}\varpi_{\boxtimes,1}\otimes\mathrm{id})(\gamma_{1}(a_{1}))_{[123]}=\gamma_{1}(a_{1})_{[13]},\quad(\Theta_{\boxtimes}\varpi_{\boxtimes,2}\otimes\mathrm{id})(\gamma_{2}(a_{2}))_{[124]}=\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{*}_{[12]}\gamma_{2}(a_{2})_{[24]}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{[12]}

(5.10)

for
a_{1}\in A_{1},a_{2}\in A_{2}
.

Recall that the quantum group
\mathbb{H}
(dual to
D_{\widehat{\mathcal{X}}}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}}_{1},\widehat{\mathbb{G}}_{2})
) acts on
\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}(A_{1}\bar{\otimes}A_{2})
via

\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}(\gamma_{\otimes})=(\Delta_{12}^{1}\otimes\mathrm{id}\otimes\mathrm{id})_{\mid\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}(A_{1}\bar{\otimes}A_{2})}\colon\mathbb{H}\curvearrowright\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}(A_{1}\bar{\otimes}A_{2}).

Lemma 5.8
.

\mathcal{M}\subseteq\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}(A_{1}\bar{\otimes}A_{2})
is invariant under the action of
\mathbb{H}
, i.e.

\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}(\gamma_{\otimes})(\mathcal{M})\subseteq L^{\infty}(\mathbb{H})\bar{\otimes}\mathcal{M}.

Proof.

Let us first establish a description of
\Delta_{12}^{1}
: take
a\in\{1,2\}
. For
y\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{X})
we have using (
4.4
) and Lemma
5.5
(or directly equation (
5.3
)) and
\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{[34]}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{[14]}\mathrm{W}_{1[13]}=\mathrm{W}_{1[13]}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{[14]}
that

\begin{split}&\quad\;\Delta_{12}^{1}(y)=\bigl(\hat{\mathrm{W}}_{11}^{a}(y\otimes 1\otimes 1)\hat{\mathrm{W}}_{11}^{a*}\bigr)_{[3412]}\\
&=(\hat{J}_{1}\otimes\hat{J}_{2}\otimes J_{1}\otimes J_{2})\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{[34]}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{[14]}\mathrm{W}_{1[13]}\mathrm{W}_{2[24]}(\hat{J}_{1}\otimes\hat{J}_{2}\otimes J_{1}\otimes J_{2})\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{[14]}^{*}\\
&\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad y_{[34]}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{[14]}(\hat{J}_{1}\otimes\hat{J}_{2}\otimes J_{1}\otimes J_{2})\mathrm{W}_{2[24]}^{*}\mathrm{W}_{1[13]}^{*}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{[14]}^{*}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{[34]}^{*}(\hat{J}_{1}\otimes\hat{J}_{2}\otimes J_{1}\otimes J_{2})\\
&=(\hat{J}_{1}\otimes\hat{J}_{2}\otimes J_{1}\otimes J_{2})\mathrm{W}_{1[13]}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{[14]}\mathrm{W}_{2[24]}(\hat{J}_{1}\otimes\hat{J}_{2}\otimes J_{1}\otimes J_{2})\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{[14]}^{*}\\
&\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad y_{[34]}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{[14]}(\hat{J}_{1}\otimes\hat{J}_{2}\otimes J_{1}\otimes J_{2})\mathrm{W}_{2[24]}^{*}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{[14]}^{*}\mathrm{W}_{1[13]}^{*}(\hat{J}_{1}\otimes\hat{J}_{2}\otimes J_{1}\otimes J_{2}).\end{split}

Now, take
a_{1}\in A_{1}
and calculate using (
5.10
) that

\begin{split}&\quad\;\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}(\gamma_{\otimes})\bigl((\Theta_{\boxtimes}\varpi_{\boxtimes,1}\otimes\mathrm{id})(\gamma_{1}(a_{1}))_{[123]}\bigr)=(\Delta_{12}^{1}\otimes\mathrm{id}\otimes\mathrm{id})(\gamma_{1}(a_{1})_{[13]})\\
&=(\hat{J}_{1}\otimes\hat{J}_{2}\otimes J_{1}\otimes J_{2}\otimes J_{A_{1}}\otimes J_{A_{2}})\mathrm{W}_{1[13]}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{[14]}\mathrm{W}_{2[24]}(\hat{J}_{1}\otimes\hat{J}_{2}\otimes J_{1}\otimes J_{2}\otimes J_{A_{1}}\otimes J_{A_{2}})\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{[14]}^{*}\\
&\quad\gamma_{1}(a_{1})_{[35]}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{[14]}(\hat{J}_{1}\otimes\hat{J}_{2}\otimes J_{1}\otimes J_{2}\otimes J_{A_{1}}\otimes J_{A_{2}})\mathrm{W}_{2[24]}^{*}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{[14]}^{*}\mathrm{W}_{1[13]}^{*}(\hat{J}_{1}\otimes\hat{J}_{2}\otimes J_{1}\otimes J_{2}\otimes J_{A_{1}}\otimes J_{A_{2}})\\
&=\mathrm{W}_{1[13]}^{*}\gamma_{1}(a_{1})_{[35]}\mathrm{W}_{1[13]}=(\Delta_{1}\otimes\mathrm{id})\gamma_{1}(a_{1})_{[135]}=(\mathrm{id}\otimes\gamma_{1})\gamma_{1}(a_{1})_{[135]},\end{split}

(5.11)

which belongs to
\mathcal{B}(L^{2}(\mathbb{G}_{1})\otimes L^{2}(\mathbb{G}_{2}))\bar{\otimes}\mathcal{M}
. On the other hand, this element also belongs to the space
L^{\infty}(\mathbb{H})\bar{\otimes}\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}(A_{1}\bar{\otimes}A_{2})
by Lemma
5.7
. Thus
[
Tak02
]
*Corollary 5.10 gives

\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}(\gamma_{\otimes})\bigl((\Theta_{\boxtimes}\varpi_{\boxtimes,1}\otimes\mathrm{id})(\gamma_{1}(a_{1}))_{[123]}\bigr)\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{H})\bar{\otimes}\mathcal{M}.

Next, take
a_{2}\in A_{2}
and calculate using again (
5.10
)

\begin{split}&\quad\;\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}(\gamma_{\otimes})\bigl((\Theta_{\boxtimes}\varpi_{\boxtimes,2}\otimes\mathrm{id})(\gamma_{2}(a_{2}))_{[124]}\bigr)=(\Delta_{12}^{1}\otimes\mathrm{id}\otimes\mathrm{id})(\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{*}_{[12]}\gamma_{2}(a_{2})_{[24]}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{[12]})\\
&=(\hat{J}_{1}\otimes\hat{J}_{2}\otimes J_{1}\otimes J_{2}\otimes J_{A_{1}}\otimes J_{A_{2}})\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{[34]}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{[14]}\mathrm{W}_{1[13]}\mathrm{W}_{2[24]}(\hat{J}_{1}\otimes\hat{J}_{2}\otimes J_{1}\otimes J_{2}\otimes J_{A_{1}}\otimes J_{A_{2}})\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{[14]}^{*}\\
&\quad\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{*}_{[34]}\gamma_{2}(a_{2})_{[46]}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{[34]}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{[14]}(\hat{J}_{1}\otimes\hat{J}_{2}\otimes J_{1}\otimes J_{2}\otimes J_{A_{1}}\otimes J_{A_{2}})\mathrm{W}_{2[24]}^{*}\mathrm{W}_{1[13]}^{*}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{[14]}^{*}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{[34]}^{*}\\
&\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad(\hat{J}_{1}\otimes\hat{J}_{2}\otimes J_{1}\otimes J_{2}\otimes J_{A_{1}}\otimes J_{A_{2}})\\
&=\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{[34]}^{*}(\hat{J}_{1}\otimes\hat{J}_{2}\otimes J_{1}\otimes J_{2}\otimes J_{A_{1}}\otimes J_{A_{2}})\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{[14]}\mathrm{W}_{2[24]}(\hat{J}_{1}\otimes\hat{J}_{2}\otimes J_{1}\otimes J_{2}\otimes J_{A_{1}}\otimes J_{A_{2}})\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{[14]}^{*}\mathrm{W}_{1[13]}^{*}\\
&\quad\gamma_{2}(a_{2})_{[46]}\mathrm{W}_{1[13]}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{[14]}(\hat{J}_{1}\otimes\hat{J}_{2}\otimes J_{1}\otimes J_{2}\otimes J_{A_{1}}\otimes J_{A_{2}})\mathrm{W}_{2[24]}^{*}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{[14]}^{*}(\hat{J}_{1}\otimes\hat{J}_{2}\otimes J_{1}\otimes J_{2}\otimes J_{A_{1}}\otimes J_{A_{2}})\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{[34]}\\
&=\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{[34]}^{*}u_{\mathbb{G}_{1}[1]}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{[14]}^{*}u_{\mathbb{G}_{1}[1]}\mathrm{W}_{2[24]}^{*}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{[14]}^{*}\gamma_{2}(a_{2})_{[46]}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{[14]}\mathrm{W}_{2[24]}u_{\mathbb{G}_{1}[1]}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{[14]}^{*}u_{\mathbb{G}_{1}[1]}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{[34]}.\end{split}

(5.12)

Consider
\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{u}
, the universal lift of bicharacter
\widehat{\mathcal{X}}
. We can write
\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{u}=(\Phi\otimes\mathrm{id}){\mathds{V}\!\!\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{V}$}}}_{2}
for a non-degenerate
*
-homomorphism
\Phi\colon C_{0}^{u}(\mathbb{G}_{2})\rightarrow\operatorname{M}(C_{0}^{u}(\hat{\mathbb{G}}_{1}))
which respects comultiplication (see
[
Kus01
]
*Proposition 6.5,
[
MRW12
]
*Proposition 4.2 and also
[
DCKr24
]
*Lemma 2.5). We will write

\Phi^{\operatorname{vN}}\colon C_{0}^{u}(\mathbb{G}_{2})^{**}\rightarrow C_{0}^{u}(\hat{\mathbb{G}}_{1})^{**},\qquad\hat{\pi}^{\operatorname{vN}}_{1,\mathrm{red}}\colon C_{0}^{u}(\hat{\mathbb{G}}_{1})^{**}\rightarrow L^{\infty}(\hat{\mathbb{G}}_{1})

for the unique extensions of
\Phi,\hat{\pi}_{1,\mathrm{red}}
to normal, unital
*
-homomorphisms. We will also need the von Neumann algebraic versions
\Delta^{u,r,\operatorname{vN}}_{2},\gamma_{2}^{u}
of the maps
\Delta^{u,r}_{2},\gamma_{2}
(see the discussion at the beginning of
[
DCKr24
]
*Section 4.3). Using these objects and
[
DCKr24
]
*Lemma 4.7, we can calculate

\begin{split}&\quad\;\mathrm{W}_{2[24]}^{*}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{[14]}^{*}\gamma_{2}(a_{2})_{[46]}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{[14]}\mathrm{W}_{2[24]}\\
&=(\hat{\pi}_{1,\mathrm{red}}^{\operatorname{vN}}\Phi^{\operatorname{vN}}\otimes\mathrm{id}\otimes\mathrm{id}\otimes\mathrm{id})\bigl(\mathrm{W}^{*}_{2[23]}\mathds{W}^{*}_{2[13]}\gamma_{2}(a_{2})_{[34]}\mathds{W}^{*}_{2[13]}\mathrm{W}^{*}_{2[23]}\bigr)_{[1246]}\\
&=(\hat{\pi}_{1,\mathrm{red}}^{\operatorname{vN}}\Phi^{\operatorname{vN}}\otimes\mathrm{id}\otimes\mathrm{id}\otimes\mathrm{id})(\mathrm{id}\otimes\Delta_{2}\otimes\mathrm{id})(\Delta_{2}^{u,r,\operatorname{vN}}\otimes\mathrm{id})\gamma_{2}(a_{2})_{[1246]}\\
&=(\hat{\pi}_{1,\mathrm{red}}^{\operatorname{vN}}\Phi^{\operatorname{vN}}\otimes\mathrm{id}\otimes\mathrm{id}\otimes\mathrm{id})(\mathrm{id}\otimes\mathrm{id}\otimes\gamma_{2})(\mathrm{id}\otimes\gamma_{2})\gamma_{2}^{u}(a_{2})_{[1246]}.\end{split}

Since
C_{0}^{u}(\mathbb{G}_{2})\odot L^{\infty}(\mathbb{G}_{2})\odot A_{2}
is weak
∗
-dense in
C_{0}^{u}(\mathbb{G}_{2})^{**}\bar{\otimes}L^{\infty}(\mathbb{G}_{2})\bar{\otimes}A_{2}
, we can write

C_{0}^{u}(\mathbb{G}_{2})^{**}\bar{\otimes}L^{\infty}(\mathbb{G}_{2})\bar{\otimes}A_{2}\ni(\mathrm{id}\otimes\gamma_{2})\gamma_{2}^{u}(a_{2})=\underset{i\in I}{w^{*}\text{-}\lim}\sum_{n=1}^{N_{i}}a_{n,i}\otimes b_{n,i}\otimes c_{n,i}

for some
a_{n,i}\in C_{0}^{u}(\mathbb{G}_{2}),b_{n,i}\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{G}_{2}),c_{n,i}\in A_{2}
. Consequently

\begin{split}&\quad\;\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}(\gamma_{\otimes})\bigl((\Theta_{\boxtimes}\varpi_{\boxtimes,2}\otimes\mathrm{id})(\gamma_{2}(a_{2}))_{[124]}\bigr)\\
&=\underset{i\in I}{w^{*}\text{-}\lim}\sum_{n=1}^{N_{i}}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{[34]}^{*}u_{\mathbb{G}_{1}[1]}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{[14]}^{*}u_{\mathbb{G}_{1}[1]}\bigl(\hat{\pi}_{1,\mathrm{red}}\Phi(a_{n,i})\otimes b_{n,i}\otimes\gamma_{2}(c_{n,i})\bigr)_{[1246]}u_{\mathbb{G}_{1}[1]}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{[14]}u_{\mathbb{G}_{1}[1]}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{[34]}\\
&=\underset{i\in I}{w^{*}\text{-}\lim}\sum_{n=1}^{N_{i}}\bigl(\hat{\pi}_{1,\mathrm{red}}\Phi(a_{n,i})\otimes b_{n,i}\bigr)_{[12]}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{[34]}^{*}u_{\mathbb{G}_{1}[1]}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{[14]}^{*}u_{\mathbb{G}_{1}[1]}\gamma_{2}(c_{n,i})_{[46]}u_{\mathbb{G}_{1}[1]}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{[14]}u_{\mathbb{G}_{1}[1]}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{[34]}\\
&=\underset{i\in I}{w^{*}\text{-}\lim}\sum_{n=1}^{N_{i}}\bigl(\hat{\pi}_{1,\mathrm{red}}\Phi(a_{n,i})\otimes b_{n,i}\bigr)_{[12]}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{[34]}^{*}u_{\mathbb{G}_{1}[1]}(\hat{\pi}_{1,\mathrm{red}}^{\operatorname{vN}}\Phi^{\operatorname{vN}}\otimes\mathrm{id}\otimes\mathrm{id})(\Delta_{2}^{u,r,\operatorname{vN}}\otimes\mathrm{id})\gamma_{2}(c_{n,i})_{[146]}u_{\mathbb{G}_{1}[1]}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{[34]}\\
&=\underset{i\in I}{w^{*}\text{-}\lim}\sum_{n=1}^{N_{i}}\bigl(\hat{\pi}_{1,\mathrm{red}}\Phi(a_{n,i})\otimes b_{n,i}\bigr)_{[12]}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{[34]}^{*}u_{\mathbb{G}_{1}[1]}(\hat{\pi}_{1,\mathrm{red}}^{\operatorname{vN}}\Phi^{\operatorname{vN}}\otimes\gamma_{2})\gamma_{2}^{u}(c_{n,i})_{[146]}u_{\mathbb{G}_{1}[1]}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{[34]}\end{split}

which is easily seen (by slicing off legs
12
) to belong to
\mathcal{B}(L^{2}(\mathbb{G}_{1})\otimes L^{2}(\mathbb{G}_{2}))\bar{\otimes}\mathcal{M}
. As before we conclude that

\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}(\gamma_{\otimes})\bigl((\Theta_{\boxtimes}\varpi_{\boxtimes,2}\otimes\mathrm{id})(\gamma_{2}(a_{2}))_{[124]}\bigr)\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{H})\bar{\otimes}\mathcal{M},

which ends the proof.
∎

Next we prove that the inclusion of Lemma
5.7
is in fact an equality.

Lemma 5.9
.

\mathcal{M}=\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}(A_{1}\bar{\otimes}A_{2})
.

Proof.

The main strategy behind the proof of Lemma
5.9
