is to use the result stating that taking second induction takes us back to the algebra that we have started with. Let us be more precise. Consider the reflected linking quantum groupoid
\mathscr{H}=\begin{pmatrix}\mathbb{G}&\mathbb{Y}\\
\mathbb{X}&\mathbb{H}\end{pmatrix}
(Remark
1.5
). Note that the comultiplication maps of
\widehat{\mathscr{H}}
are those of
\widehat{\mathscr{G}}
, appropriately swapped. Since the LCQG
\mathbb{H}
acts on
\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}(A_{1}\bar{\otimes}A_{2})
and
\mathcal{M}
, we can consider induction along
\mathbb{Y}
:

\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{Y}}(\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}(A_{1}\bar{\otimes}A_{2}))=\{z\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{Y})\bar{\otimes}\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}(A_{1}\bar{\otimes}A_{2})\mid(\Delta_{\mathscr{H},12}^{2}\otimes\mathrm{id}^{\otimes 4})z=(\mathrm{id}\otimes\mathrm{id}\otimes\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}(\gamma_{\otimes}))z\}

and

\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{Y}}(\mathcal{M})=\{z\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{Y})\bar{\otimes}\mathcal{M}\mid(\Delta_{\mathscr{H},12}^{2}\otimes\mathrm{id}^{\otimes 4})z=(\mathrm{id}\otimes\mathrm{id}\otimes\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}(\gamma_{\otimes}))z\},

where
\Delta_{\mathscr{H},ij}^{k}
are the comultiplication maps of
\mathscr{H}
. Clearly we have

\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{Y}}(\mathcal{M})\subseteq\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{Y}}(\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}(A_{1}\bar{\otimes}A_{2}))
. Now,
[
DC09
]

*Theorem 8.2.2 and its proof states that

\Xi\colon A_{1}\bar{\otimes}A_{2}\ni a\mapsto(\Delta^{1}_{22}\otimes\mathrm{id}\otimes\mathrm{id})\gamma_{\otimes}(a)\in\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{Y}}(\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}(A_{1}\bar{\otimes}A_{2}))

is a well defined isomorphism. We claim that

\Xi(A_{1}\bar{\otimes}A_{2})\subseteq\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{Y}}(\mathcal{M}).

(5.13)

If this is true, then

\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{Y}}(\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}(A_{1}\bar{\otimes}A_{2}))=\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{Y}}(\mathcal{M})

with equal induced action of
\mathbb{G}=\mathbb{G}_{1}\times\mathbb{G}_{2}
, hence taking yet another induction (along
\mathbb{X}
) and using again
[
DC09
]
*Theorem 8.2.2 gives us
\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}(A_{1}\bar{\otimes}A_{2})=\mathcal{M}
.

Thus it is enough to prove (
5.13
). First we need a more concrete description of the map
\Delta_{22}^{1}
. Take
a\in\{1,2\},x\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})
. Then we have, using (
4.4
) and Lemma
5.5
,

\begin{split}&\quad\;\Delta^{1}_{22}(x)=\bigl(\hat{\mathrm{W}}^{a}_{21}(x\otimes 1\otimes 1)\hat{\mathrm{W}}^{a*}_{21}\bigr)_{[3412]}\\
&=(\hat{J}_{1}\otimes\hat{J}_{2}\otimes J_{1}\otimes J_{2})\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{[34]}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{[14]}\hat{\mathrm{W}}^{*}_{1[31]}\hat{\mathrm{W}}^{*}_{2[42]}(\hat{J}_{1}\otimes\hat{J}_{2}\otimes J_{1}\otimes J_{2})\\
&\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad x_{[34]}(\hat{J}_{1}\otimes\hat{J}_{2}\otimes J_{1}\otimes J_{2})\hat{\mathrm{W}}_{2[42]}\hat{\mathrm{W}}_{1[31]}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{[14]}^{*}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{[34]}^{*}(\hat{J}_{1}\otimes\hat{J}_{2}\otimes J_{1}\otimes J_{2})\\
&=(\hat{J}_{1}\otimes\hat{J}_{2}\otimes J_{1}\otimes J_{2})\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{[34]}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{[14]}(\hat{J}_{1}\otimes\hat{J}_{2}\otimes J_{1}\otimes J_{2})\mathrm{W}_{1[13]}^{*}\mathrm{W}_{2[24]}^{*}\\
&\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad x_{[34]}\mathrm{W}_{2[24]}\mathrm{W}_{1[13]}(\hat{J}_{1}\otimes\hat{J}_{2}\otimes J_{1}\otimes J_{2})\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{[14]}^{*}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{[34]}^{*}(\hat{J}_{1}\otimes\hat{J}_{2}\otimes J_{1}\otimes J_{2})\\
&=\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{[34]}^{*}u_{\mathbb{G}_{1}[1]}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{[14]}^{*}u_{\mathbb{G}_{1}[1]}\Delta_{\mathbb{G}}(x)u_{\mathbb{G}_{1}[1]}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{[14]}u_{\mathbb{G}_{1}[1]}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{[34]}.\end{split}

(5.14)

Using equation (
5.14
), we can now show (
5.13
) on generators. First, take
a_{1}\in A_{1}
and calculate

\begin{split}&\quad\;\Xi(a_{1}\otimes 1)=(\Delta^{1}_{22}\otimes\mathrm{id}\otimes\mathrm{id})(\gamma_{1}(a_{1})_{[13]})\\
&=(\hat{J}_{1}\otimes\hat{J}_{2}\otimes J_{1}\otimes J_{2}\otimes J_{A_{1}}\otimes J_{A_{2}})\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{[34]}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{[14]}\mathrm{W}_{1[13]}\mathrm{W}_{2[24]}(\hat{J}_{1}\otimes\hat{J}_{2}\otimes J_{1}\otimes J_{2}\otimes J_{A_{1}}\otimes J_{A_{2}})\\
&\quad\quad\quad\quad\gamma_{1}(a_{1})_{[35]}(\hat{J}_{1}\otimes\hat{J}_{2}\otimes J_{1}\otimes J_{2}\otimes J_{A_{1}}\otimes J_{A_{2}})\mathrm{W}^{*}_{2[24]}\mathrm{W}^{*}_{1[13]}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{[14]}^{*}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{[34]}^{*}(\hat{J}_{1}\otimes\hat{J}_{2}\otimes J_{1}\otimes J_{2}\otimes J_{A_{1}}\otimes J_{A_{2}})\\
&=(\hat{J}_{1}\otimes\hat{J}_{2}\otimes J_{1}\otimes J_{2}\otimes J_{A_{1}}\otimes J_{A_{2}})\mathrm{W}_{1[13]}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{[14]}(\hat{J}_{1}\otimes\hat{J}_{2}\otimes J_{1}\otimes J_{2}\otimes J_{A_{1}}\otimes J_{A_{2}})\\
&\quad\quad\quad\quad\gamma_{1}(a_{1})_{[35]}(\hat{J}_{1}\otimes\hat{J}_{2}\otimes J_{1}\otimes J_{2}\otimes J_{A_{1}}\otimes J_{A_{2}})\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{[14]}^{*}\mathrm{W}^{*}_{1[13]}(\hat{J}_{1}\otimes\hat{J}_{2}\otimes J_{1}\otimes J_{2}\otimes J_{A_{1}}\otimes J_{A_{2}})\\
&=\mathrm{W}_{1[13]}^{*}\gamma_{1}(a_{1})_{[35]}\mathrm{W}_{1[13]}=(\Delta_{1}\otimes\mathrm{id})\gamma_{1}(a_{1})_{[135]}=(\mathrm{id}\otimes\gamma_{1})\gamma_{1}(a_{1})_{[135]},\end{split}

which belongs to
\mathcal{B}(L^{2}(\mathbb{G}_{1})\otimes L^{2}(\mathbb{G}_{2}))\bar{\otimes}\mathcal{M}
by (
5.10
). Since also
\Xi(a_{1}\otimes 1)\in\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{Y}}(\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}(A_{1}\bar{\otimes}A_{2}))
, it follows that
\Xi(a_{1}\otimes 1)\in\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{Y}}(\mathcal{M})
. Next, take
a_{2}\in A_{2}
. We have

\begin{split}&\quad\;\Xi(1\otimes a_{2})=(\Delta_{22}^{1}\otimes\mathrm{id}\otimes\mathrm{id})(\gamma_{2}(a_{2})_{[24]})\\
&=\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{[34]}^{*}u_{\mathbb{G}_{1}[1]}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{[14]}^{*}u_{\mathbb{G}_{1}[1]}(\Delta_{\mathbb{G}}\otimes\mathrm{id}\otimes\mathrm{id})(\gamma_{2}(a_{2})_{[24]})u_{\mathbb{G}_{1}[1]}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{[14]}u_{\mathbb{G}_{1}[1]}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{[34]}\\
&=\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{[34]}^{*}u_{\mathbb{G}_{1}[1]}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{[14]}^{*}(\mathrm{id}\otimes\gamma_{2})\gamma_{2}(a_{2})_{[246]}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{[14]}u_{\mathbb{G}_{1}[1]}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{[34]}.\end{split}

Observe that (using notation introduced in the proof of Lemma
5.8
)

\begin{split}&\quad\;\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{[14]}^{*}(\mathrm{id}\otimes\gamma_{2})\gamma_{2}(a_{2})_{[246]}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{[14]}=(\hat{\pi}_{1,\mathrm{red}}\Phi\otimes\mathrm{id})(\mathds{W}_{2})_{[14]}^{*}(\mathrm{id}\otimes\gamma_{2})\gamma_{2}(a_{2})_{[246]}(\hat{\pi}_{1,\mathrm{red}}\Phi\otimes\mathrm{id})(\mathds{W}^{*}_{2})_{[14]}\\
&=\bigl((\mathrm{id}\otimes\hat{\pi}_{1,\mathrm{red}}^{\operatorname{vN}}\Phi^{\operatorname{vN}}\otimes\mathrm{id}\otimes\mathrm{id})(\mathrm{id}\otimes\Delta^{u,r,\operatorname{vN}}\otimes\mathrm{id})(\mathrm{id}\otimes\gamma_{2})\gamma_{2}(a_{2})\bigr)_{[2146]}\\
&=\bigl((\mathrm{id}\otimes\hat{\pi}_{1,\mathrm{red}}^{\operatorname{vN}}\Phi^{\operatorname{vN}}\otimes\gamma_{2})(\mathrm{id}\otimes\gamma_{2}^{u})\gamma_{2}(a_{2})\bigr)_{[2146]},\end{split}

hence we can write

\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{[14]}^{*}(\mathrm{id}\otimes\gamma_{2})\gamma_{2}(a_{2})_{[246]}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{[14]}=\underset{i\in I}{w^{*}\text{-}\lim}\sum_{n=1}^{N_{i}}(\hat{\pi}_{1,\mathrm{red}}\Phi(b_{i,n})\otimes a_{i,n})_{[12]}\gamma_{2}(c_{i,n})_{[46]}

for
(\mathrm{id}\otimes\gamma^{u}_{2})\gamma_{2}(a_{2})=\underset{i\in I}{w^{*}\text{-}\lim}\sum_{n=1}^{N_{i}}a_{i,n}\otimes b_{i,n}\otimes c_{i,n}
and some
a_{i,n}\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{G}_{2})
,
b_{i,n}\in C_{0}^{u}(\mathbb{G}_{2})
,
c_{i,n}\in A_{2}
. Consequently

\Xi(1\otimes a_{2})=\underset{i\in I}{w^{*}\text{-}\lim}\sum_{n=1}^{N_{i}}u_{\mathbb{G}_{1}[1]}(\hat{\pi}_{1,\mathrm{red}}\Phi(b_{i,n})\otimes a_{i,n})_{[12]}u_{\mathbb{G}_{1}[1]}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{*}_{[34]}\gamma_{2}(c_{i,n})_{[46]}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{[34]},

and (
5.10
) ends the proof of (
5.13
).
∎

As a corollary, we obtain an important result.

Theorem 5.10
.

We have an isomorphism of W
∗
-algebras

A_{1}\overline{\boxtimes}A_{2}\cong\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}(A_{1}\bar{\otimes}A_{2})

given on generators by

\begin{split}\pi_{\boxtimes,1}(a_{1})&\mapsto(\Theta_{\boxtimes}\varpi_{\boxtimes,1}\otimes\mathrm{id})(\gamma_{1}(a_{1}))_{[123]}=\gamma_{1}(a_{1})_{[13]},\\
\pi_{\boxtimes,2}(a_{2})&\mapsto(\Theta_{\boxtimes}\varpi_{\boxtimes,2}\otimes\mathrm{id})(\gamma_{2}(a_{2}))_{[124]}=\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{*}_{[12]}\gamma_{2}(a_{2})_{[24]}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{[12]}\end{split}

for
a_{1}\in A_{1},a_{2}\in A_{2}
.

Proof.

We have a faithful, normal
*
-representation of
A_{1}
on
L^{2}(\mathbb{G}_{1})\otimes L^{2}(A_{1})
and of
A_{2}
on
L^{2}(\mathbb{G}_{2})\otimes L^{2}(A_{2})
via

\gamma_{1}\colon A_{1}\rightarrow\mathcal{B}(L^{2}(\mathbb{G}_{1})\otimes L^{2}(A_{1})),\quad\gamma_{2}\colon A_{2}\rightarrow\mathcal{B}(L^{2}(\mathbb{G}_{2})\otimes L^{2}(A_{2})).

Moreover, the unitary representations
\mathrm{W}_{1[12]}
and
\mathrm{W}_{2[12]}
implement the actions
\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2}
. The corresponding
*
-representations of
C^{*}(\mathbb{G}_{1}),C^{*}(\mathbb{G}_{2})
are given by
\hat{\pi}_{1,\mathrm{red}}\otimes 1
and
\hat{\pi}_{2,\mathrm{red}}\otimes 1
. Since the braided tensor product is up to isomorphism independent of the choices of implementations
[
DCKr24
]
*Proposition 4.4, we obtain a faithful, normal
*
-homomorphism of
A_{1}\overline{\boxtimes}A_{2}
into
\mathcal{B}(L^{2}(\mathbb{G}_{1})\otimes L^{2}(A_{1})\otimes L^{2}(\mathbb{G}_{2})\otimes L^{2}(A_{2}))
:

A_{1}\overline{\boxtimes}A_{2}\ni\pi_{\boxtimes,1}(a_{1})\pi_{\boxtimes,2}(a_{2})\mapsto\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{[13]}\gamma_{1}(a_{1})_{[12]}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{[13]}^{*}\gamma_{2}(a_{2})_{[34]},\quad\forall a_{1}\in A_{1},a_{2}\in A_{2}.

Composing this map with
z\mapsto\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{*}_{[12]}z_{[1324]}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{[12]}
, we obtain an isomorphism

A_{1}\overline{\boxtimes}A_{2}\ni\pi_{\boxtimes,1}(a_{1})\pi_{\boxtimes,2}(a_{2})\mapsto\gamma_{1}(a_{1})_{[13]}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{[12]}^{*}\gamma_{2}(a_{2})_{[24]}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{[12]}\in\mathcal{M},\quad\forall a_{1}\in A_{1},a_{2}\in A_{2}.

Lemma
5.9
now ends the proof.
∎

In the following, we will use that if
\mathbb{G}_{1},\mathbb{G}_{2}
are LCQG’s and
(A_{1},\gamma_{1}),(A_{2},\gamma_{2})
are respectively a left
\mathbb{G}_{1}
- and a left
\mathbb{G}_{2}
-W
∗
-algebra, the standard implementation of the
\mathbb{G}_{1}\times\mathbb{G}_{2}
-action
\gamma_{\otimes}
on
A_{1}\bar{\otimes}A_{2}
is just

U_{\gamma_{1}[13]}U_{\gamma_{2}[24]}\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{G}_{1})\bar{\otimes}L^{\infty}(\mathbb{G}_{2})\bar{\otimes}\mathcal{B}(L^{2}(A_{1})\otimes L^{2}(A_{2})),

upon identifying
L^{2}(A_{1}\bar{\otimes}A_{2})\cong L^{2}(A_{1})\otimes L^{2}(A_{2})
.
This follows straightforwardly from the construction of the standard implementation in
[
Vae01
]
.

It follows from the previous theorem that we can identify

L^{2}(\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}(A_{1}\bar{\otimes}A_{2}))\cong L^{2}(A_{1}\overline{\boxtimes}A_{2}).

Theorem
3.9
identifies this space with
\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}(L^{2}(A_{1})\otimes L^{2}(A_{2}))
via a unitary
I
. Next, recall (Proposition
4.11
) that we have a unitary identification

\theta\mathcal{O}^{*}\colon L^{2}(A_{1})\otimes L^{2}(A_{2})\rightarrow\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}(L^{2}(A_{1})\otimes L^{2}(A_{2})),

where we have used simplified notation (neglecting subscripts). As will be clear from the proof of the next theorem, it is useful to compose these identifications with the unitary
(\hat{\pi}_{U_{\gamma_{1}}}\otimes\hat{\pi}_{U_{\gamma_{2}}})(\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{u})^{*}
acting on
L^{2}(A_{1})\otimes L^{2}(A_{2})
. We denote the resulting unitary by
I_{\boxtimes}
.

Theorem 5.11
.

Let
(A_{i},\gamma_{i})
be left
\mathbb{G}_{i}
-W
∗
-algebras. Consider the tensor product
\mathbb{G}
-W
∗
-algebra
(A_{1}\bar{\otimes}A_{2},\gamma_{\otimes})
. Then we can identify

I_{\boxtimes}=I^{*}\theta\mathcal{O}^{*}(\hat{\pi}_{U_{\gamma_{1}}}\otimes\hat{\pi}_{U_{\gamma_{2}}})(\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{u})^{*}\colon L^{2}(A_{1})\otimes L^{2}(A_{2})\cong L^{2}(A_{1}\overline{\boxtimes}A_{2})

(5.15)

in such a way that:

(1)

The standard representation of
A_{1}\overline{\boxtimes}A_{2}
becomes the
*
-representation (
0.7
).

(2)

The standard implementation of
\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}(\gamma_{\otimes})
becomes the unitary

(\hat{\pi}_{U_{\gamma_{1}}}\otimes\hat{\pi}_{U_{\gamma_{2}}})(\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{u})_{[34]}(\hat{\pi}_{1,\mathrm{red}}\otimes\hat{\pi}_{U_{\gamma_{2}}})(\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{u})_{[14]}U_{\gamma_{1}[13]}U_{\gamma_{2}[24]}(u_{\mathbb{G}_{1}}\hat{\pi}_{1,\mathrm{red}}(-)u_{\mathbb{G}_{1}}\otimes\hat{\pi}_{U_{\gamma_{2}}})(\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{u})_{[14]}.

(3)

The modular conjugation
J_{A_{1}\overline{\boxtimes}A_{2}}
becomes the anti-unitary

(\hat{\pi}_{U_{\gamma_{1}}}\otimes\hat{\pi}_{U_{\gamma_{2}}})(\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{u})(J_{A_{1}}\otimes J_{A_{2}})=(J_{A_{1}}\otimes J_{A_{2}})(\hat{\pi}_{U_{\gamma_{1}}}\otimes\hat{\pi}_{U_{\gamma_{2}}})(\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{u}^{*}).

(5.16)

Proof.

As recalled above, by Proposition
4.11
and Theorem
5.2
, we have an identification

\theta\mathcal{O}^{*}\colon L^{2}(A_{1})\otimes L^{2}(A_{2})\rightarrow\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}(L^{2}(A_{1})\otimes L^{2}(A_{2})),\\
\xi_{1}\otimes\xi_{2}\mapsto U_{\gamma_{1}[13]}^{*}U_{\gamma_{2}[24]}^{*}(\hat{\pi}_{1,\mathrm{red}}\otimes\hat{\pi}_{U_{\gamma_{2}}})(\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{u})_{[14]}^{*}(1\otimes 1\otimes\xi_{1}\otimes\xi_{2}).

(5.17)

By Theorem
3.9
, we can identify (via the unitary
I
) the natural
*
-representation of
\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}(A_{1}\bar{\otimes}A_{2})
on the right hand side with the standard representation. Next, we have the identification

\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}(A_{1}\bar{\otimes}A_{2})\cong A_{1}\overline{\boxtimes}A_{2}

from Theorem
5.10
, which allows us to realise the standard representation of
A_{1}\overline{\boxtimes}A_{2}
on
L^{2}(A_{1})\otimes L^{2}(A_{2})
. A lengthy but straightforward computation using the pentagon equation, shows that it is given explicitely by

A_{1}\overline{\boxtimes}A_{2}\rightarrow\mathcal{B}(L^{2}(A_{1})\otimes L^{2}(A_{2})),\\
\pi_{\boxtimes,1}(a_{1})\pi_{\boxtimes,2}(a_{2})\mapsto(\hat{\pi}_{U_{\gamma_{1}}}\otimes\hat{\pi}_{U_{\gamma_{2}}})(\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{u})^{*}\pi_{\boxtimes,1}(a_{1})\pi_{\boxtimes,2}(a_{2})(\hat{\pi}_{U_{\gamma_{1}}}\otimes\hat{\pi}_{U_{\gamma_{2}}})(\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{u}).

Finally the identification (
5.15
) is then taken to be
I^{*}\theta\mathcal{O}^{*}
, precomposed with the unitary
(\hat{\pi}_{U_{\gamma_{1}}}\otimes\hat{\pi}_{U_{\gamma_{2}}})(\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{u})^{*}\in\mathcal{B}(L^{2}(A_{1})\otimes L^{2}(A_{2}))
:

I_{\boxtimes}=I^{*}\theta\mathcal{O}^{*}(\hat{\pi}_{U_{\gamma_{1}}}\otimes\hat{\pi}_{U_{\gamma_{2}}})(\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{u})^{*}\colon L^{2}(A_{1})\otimes L^{2}(A_{2})\rightarrow L^{2}(A_{1}\overline{\boxtimes}A_{2}),\\
\xi_{1}\otimes\xi_{2}\mapsto I^{*}U_{\gamma_{1}[13]}^{*}U_{\gamma_{2}[24]}^{*}(\hat{\pi}_{1,\mathrm{red}}\otimes\hat{\pi}_{U_{\gamma_{2}}})(\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{u})_{[14]}^{*}(\hat{\pi}_{U_{\gamma_{1}}}\otimes\hat{\pi}_{U_{\gamma_{2}}})(\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{u})^{*}_{[34]}(1\otimes 1\otimes\xi_{1}\otimes\xi_{2}).

(5.18)

The formula for the standard implementation now follows from Proposition
4.10
, Lemma
5.3
and Lemma
5.4
.

To conclude, the first identity in (
5.16
) follows immediately from Theorem
4.12
and Lemma
5.4
, while the alternative formula holds since the modular conjugation is self-adjoint (alternatively, it can be obtained using
[
Vae01
]
*Proposition 3.7).
∎

At the end of this section, we use the established theorems to deduce two results concerning faithfulness of states on
A_{1}\overline{\boxtimes}A_{2}
, transported from
A_{1}\bar{\otimes}A_{2}
. First we present a more general result.

Proposition 5.12
.

Let
(A_{i},\gamma_{i})
be left
\mathbb{G}_{i}
-W
∗
-algebras, and consider the tensor product
\mathbb{G}
-W
∗
-algebra
(A_{1}\bar{\otimes}A_{2},\gamma_{\otimes})
. Assume that
\omega\in(A_{1}\bar{\otimes}A_{2})_{*}^{+}
is a normal state, invariant under the
\mathbb{G}
-action
\gamma_{\otimes}
, and let
\xi\in L^{2}(A_{1})\otimes L^{2}(A_{2})
be the unit vector in the standard positive cone of
A_{1}\bar{\otimes}A_{2}
implementing
\omega
. Consider the normal state
\nu=\omega_{\xi}\in(A_{1}\overline{\boxtimes}A_{2})_{*}^{+}
, where we let
A_{1}\overline{\boxtimes}A_{2}
act on
L^{2}(A_{1})\otimes L^{2}(A_{2})
via the defining representation.

(1)

We have
\nu(\pi_{\boxtimes,1}(a_{1})\pi_{\boxtimes,2}(a_{2}))=\omega(a_{1}\otimes a_{2})
for
a_{1}\in A_{1},a_{2}\in A_{2}
.

(2)

If
\omega
is faithful, then
\nu
is also faithful.

Proof.

Recall first that, since
\omega
is invariant under the action of
\mathbb{G}
, we have
U_{\gamma_{1}[13]}U_{\gamma_{2}[24]}(\zeta\otimes\xi)=\zeta\otimes\xi
for
\zeta\in L^{2}(\mathbb{G})

[
DCKr24
]
*Lemma 7.5. It follows that

(\hat{\pi}_{U_{\gamma_{1}}}(x)\otimes\hat{\pi}_{U_{\gamma_{2}}}(y))\xi=\varepsilon^{u}_{\hat{\mathbb{G}}}(x\otimes y)\xi,\qquad\forall x\otimes y\in C^{*}(\mathbb{G}).

Theorem
5.11
allows us to treat
L^{2}(A_{1})\otimes L^{2}(A_{2})
as the standard Hilbert space for
A_{1}\overline{\boxtimes}A_{2}
with modular conjugation
I_{\boxtimes}^{*}J_{A_{1}\overline{\boxtimes}A_{2}}I_{\boxtimes}
. For
a_{1}\in A_{1},a_{2}\in A_{2}
we have

\nu(\pi_{\boxtimes,1}(a_{1})\pi_{\boxtimes,2}(a_{2}))=\bigl\langle(\hat{\pi}_{U_{\gamma_{1}}}\otimes\hat{\pi}_{U_{\gamma_{2}}})(\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{u})^{*}\xi\,,\,(a_{1}\otimes 1)(\hat{\pi}_{U_{\gamma_{1}}}\otimes\hat{\pi}_{U_{\gamma_{2}}})(\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{u})^{*}(1\otimes a_{2})\xi\bigr\rangle\\
=\langle\xi,(a_{1}\otimes a_{2})\xi\rangle=\omega(a_{1}\otimes a_{2}),

using that
(\varepsilon^{u}_{\hat{\mathbb{G}}_{1}}\otimes\mathrm{id})\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{u}=1
in the penultimate step. The vector
\xi
is invariant under
I_{\boxtimes}^{*}J_{A_{1}\overline{\boxtimes}A_{2}}J_{\boxtimes}
, as follows from the concrete formula for
J_{A_{1}\overline{\boxtimes}A_{2}}
in Theorem
5.11
. Using this and the above calculation, we get

\begin{split}&\quad\;(I_{\boxtimes}^{*}J_{A_{1}\overline{\boxtimes}A_{2}}I_{\boxtimes})\pi_{\boxtimes,1}(a_{1})\pi_{\boxtimes,2}(a_{2})(I_{\boxtimes}^{*}J_{A_{1}\overline{\boxtimes}A_{2}}I_{\boxtimes})\xi=(I_{\boxtimes}^{*}J_{A_{1}\overline{\boxtimes}A_{2}}I_{\boxtimes})(\hat{\pi}_{U_{\gamma_{1}}}\otimes\hat{\pi}_{U_{\gamma_{2}}})(\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{u})(a_{1}\otimes a_{2})\xi.\end{split}

(5.19)

If
\omega
is faithful, then
\xi
is cyclic for
(A_{1}\bar{\otimes}A_{2})^{\prime}
, and as
(J_{A_{1}}\otimes J_{A_{2}})\xi=\xi
, we find that
\xi
is cyclic for
A_{1}\bar{\otimes}A_{2}
. Equation (
5.19
) implies that
\xi
is cyclic (hence also separating) for
(A_{1}\overline{\boxtimes}A_{2})^{\prime}
. Consequently
\nu
is faithful.
∎

Next we specify to braided tensor products of states, see
[
DCKr24
]
*Proposition 5.1.

Corollary 5.13
.

Let
(A_{i},\gamma_{i})
be left
\mathbb{G}_{i}
-W
∗
-algebras. Consider the tensor product
\mathbb{G}
-W
∗
-algebra
(A_{1}\bar{\otimes}A_{2},\!\gamma_{\otimes})
. Let
\omega_{i}\in(A_{i})_{*}^{+}
be faithful, normal states, invariant under the
\mathbb{G}_{i}
-action, and consider the braided tensor product state

\omega_{1}\boxtimes\omega_{2}\colon A_{1}\overline{\boxtimes}A_{2}\ni\pi_{\boxtimes,1}(a_{1})\pi_{\boxtimes,2}(a_{2})\mapsto\omega_{1}(a_{1})\omega_{2}(a_{2})\in\mathbb{C}.

Then
\omega_{1}\boxtimes\omega_{2}
is a faithful, normal state.

Proof.

Consider first
\omega_{1}\otimes\omega_{2}\in(A_{1}\bar{\otimes}A_{2})_{*}^{+}
. It is well known that
\omega_{1}\otimes\omega_{2}
is a faithful, normal state, and since each
\omega_{i}
is invariant under the action of
\mathbb{G}_{i}
, we have that
\omega_{1}\otimes\omega_{2}
is invariant under the action of
\mathbb{G}
. Denoting
\xi_{i}\in L^{2}(A_{i})
vectors in the standard positive cones implementing
\omega_{i}
, we have that
\xi_{1}\otimes\xi_{2}
is the vector in the standard positive cone of
L^{2}(A_{1}\bar{\otimes}A_{2})
implementing
\omega_{1}\otimes\omega_{2}
. Now the claim follows from
[
DCKr24
]
*Proposition 5.1 and Proposition
5.12
.
∎

5.2.
Braided tensor product for quasi-triangular quantum groups

We finally come back to proving the theorem stated in the introduction. Assume that
\mathbb{L}=(L,\Delta)
is a LCQG, and put
\mathbb{G}_{1}=\mathbb{G}_{2}=\mathbb{L}
. Let
\widehat{\mathcal{X}}\in\hat{L}\bar{\otimes}\hat{L}
be a quasi-triangular structure, so
\widehat{\mathcal{X}}
is a unitary skew bicharacter and moreover

\widehat{\mathcal{X}}\hat{\Delta}(x)\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{*}=\hat{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op}}(x),\qquad\forall x\in\hat{L}.

(5.20)

Let
\hat{\mathbb{G}}=\hat{\mathbb{G}}_{1}\times\hat{\mathbb{G}}_{2}
,
\hat{\Omega}=\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{[32]}
and let
\hat{\mathbb{H}}=D_{\widehat{\mathcal{X}}}(\hat{\mathbb{G}}_{1},\hat{\mathbb{G}}_{2})
be the generalized Drinfeld double associated to
\widehat{\mathcal{X}}
.

Lemma 5.14
.

We have
\mathbb{L}
as a closed quantum subgroup of
\mathbb{H}
via

\iota_{\mathbb{L}}\colon W^{*}(\mathbb{L})\rightarrow W^{*}(\mathbb{H})=W^{*}(\mathbb{L})\bar{\otimes}W^{*}(\mathbb{L}),\qquad x\mapsto\hat{\Delta}(x).

Proof.

This is a normal embedding preserving the coproduct, since (
5.20
) implies

\hat{\Omega}\hat{\Delta}_{\otimes}(\hat{\Delta}(x))\hat{\Omega}^{*}=(\hat{\Delta}\otimes\hat{\Delta})\hat{\Delta}(x)

for
x\in\hat{L}
.
∎

It follows that if we have an
\mathbb{H}
-W
∗
-algebra
A
, we can restrict the action to obtain an
\mathbb{L}
-W
∗
-algebra
A
(see
[
DCKr24
]
*Section 2.2).

We now recall from
[
DC09
]
*Lemma 6.5.4 that, in the general situation of a closed quantum subgroup
\mathbb{L}\subseteq\mathbb{H}
and an
\mathbb{H}
-W
∗
-algebra
(A,\gamma)
, its restriction
(A,\gamma_{\mid\mathbb{L}})
satisfies

(U_{\gamma})_{\mid\mathbb{L}}=U_{\gamma_{\mid\mathbb{L}}},

where on the left we write the restriction of a unitary representation to a closed quantum subgroup (see
[
DCKr24
]
*Section 2.2). The following theorem then follows by combining this observation with Theorem
5.11
.

Theorem 5.15
.

Let
(\mathbb{L},\widehat{\mathcal{X}})
be a quasi-triangular quantum group, and let
(A_{1},\gamma_{1}),(A_{2},\gamma_{2})
be two
\mathbb{L}
-W
∗
-algebras. Then the
\mathbb{H}
-action on
A_{1}\overline{\boxtimes}A_{2}
restricts to the
\mathbb{L}
-action given by (
0.9
), and there is a canonical unitary intertwiner of unitary
\mathbb{L}
-representations

I_{\boxtimes}\colon L^{2}(A_{1})\otimes L^{2}(A_{2})\cong L^{2}(A_{1}\overline{\boxtimes}A_{2}),\qquad U_{\gamma_{1}}\otop U_{\gamma_{2}}\cong U_{\gamma_{1}\bowtie\gamma_{2}}.

(5.21)

This unitary intertwines the standard representation of
A_{1}\overline{\boxtimes}A_{2}
with the identity representation (
0.8
), and satisfies

J_{A_{1}\overline{\boxtimes}A_{2}}\cong(\hat{\pi}_{U_{\gamma_{1}}}\otimes\hat{\pi}_{U_{\gamma_{2}}})(\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{u})(J_{A_{1}}\otimes J_{A_{2}}).

(5.22)

Proof.

For the unitary
L^{2}(A_{1})\otimes L^{2}(A_{2})\rightarrow L^{2}(A_{1}\overline{\boxtimes}A_{2})
we take
I_{\boxtimes}=I^{*}\theta\mathcal{O}^{*}(\hat{\pi}_{U_{\gamma_{1}}}\otimes\hat{\pi}_{U_{\gamma_{2}}})(\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{u})^{*}
, as in Theorem
5.11
. It was already established that it intertwines the identity and standard representation of
A_{1}\overline{\boxtimes}A_{2}
, and satisfies (
5.22
).

Next, we claim that the
\mathbb{H}
-action
\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}(\gamma_{\otimes})
on
\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}(A_{1}\bar{\otimes}A_{2})
restricts to the
\mathbb{L}
-action
\gamma_{1}\bowtie\gamma_{2}
on
A_{1}\overline{\boxtimes}A_{2}
under the isomorphism

A_{1}\overline{\boxtimes}A_{2}\cong\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}(A_{1}\bar{\otimes}A_{2})

(Theorem
5.10
). Denoting this isomorphism by
\Upsilon
, we want to show

\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}(\gamma_{\otimes})_{|\mathbb{L}}\Upsilon=(\mathrm{id}\otimes\Upsilon)(\gamma_{1}\bowtie\gamma_{2}).

(5.23)

Since
\mathbb{L}
is a closed quantum subgroup of
\mathbb{H}
, there is a left action

\Delta_{\mathbb{L},\mathbb{H}}\colon\mathbb{L}\curvearrowright L^{\infty}(\mathbb{H})\colon\Delta_{\mathbb{L},\mathbb{H}}(x)=(\mathrm{id}\otimes\iota_{\mathbb{L}})(\mathrm{W}_{\mathbb{L}})^{*}(1\otimes x)(\mathrm{id}\otimes\iota_{\mathbb{L}})(\mathrm{W}_{\mathbb{L}}),\qquad\forall x\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{H})

and the restricted action
\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}(\gamma_{\otimes})_{|\mathbb{L}}
is characterised by

(\Delta_{\mathbb{L},\mathbb{H}}\otimes\mathrm{id}^{\otimes 4})\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}(\gamma_{\otimes})=(\mathrm{id}\otimes\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}(\gamma_{\otimes}))\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}(\gamma_{\otimes})_{|\mathbb{L}}.

Take
a_{1}\in A_{1},a_{2}\in A_{2}
. Using (
5.11
) we calculate

\begin{split}&\quad\;(\mathrm{id}\otimes\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}(\gamma_{\otimes}))\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}(\gamma_{\otimes})_{|\mathbb{L}}\bigl(\Upsilon(\pi_{\boxtimes,1}(a_{1}))\bigr)=(\Delta_{\mathbb{L},\mathbb{H}}\otimes\mathrm{id}^{\otimes 4})\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}(\gamma_{\otimes})\bigl(\Upsilon(\pi_{\boxtimes,1}(a_{1}))\bigr)\\
&=(\Delta_{\mathbb{L},\mathbb{H}}\otimes\mathrm{id}^{\otimes 4})\bigl((\mathrm{id}\otimes\gamma_{1})\gamma_{1}(a_{1})_{[135]}\bigr)=\mathrm{W}_{\mathbb{L}[12]}^{*}\mathrm{W}_{\mathbb{L}[13]}^{*}(\mathrm{id}\otimes\gamma_{1})\gamma_{1}(a_{1})_{[246]}\mathrm{W}_{\mathbb{L}[13]}\mathrm{W}_{\mathbb{L}[12]}\\
&=(\Delta_{1}\otimes\gamma_{1})\gamma_{1}(a_{1})_{[1246]}=(\mathrm{id}\otimes\mathrm{id}\otimes\gamma_{1})(\mathrm{id}\otimes\gamma_{1})\gamma_{1}(a_{1})_{[1246]}\\
&=(\mathrm{id}\otimes\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}(\gamma_{\otimes})\Upsilon)(\mathrm{id}\otimes\pi_{\boxtimes,1})\gamma_{1}(a_{1})=(\mathrm{id}\otimes\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}(\gamma_{\otimes})\Upsilon)(\gamma_{1}\bowtie\gamma_{2})\bigl(\pi_{\boxtimes,1}(a_{1})\bigr).\end{split}

(5.24)

Next we look at the second generator and calculate, using (
5.12
),

\begin{split}&\quad\;(\mathrm{id}\otimes\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}(\gamma_{\otimes}))\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}(\gamma_{\otimes})_{|\mathbb{L}}\bigl(\Upsilon(\pi_{\boxtimes,2}(a_{2}))\bigr)=(\Delta_{\mathbb{L},\mathbb{H}}\otimes\mathrm{id}^{\otimes 4})\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}(\gamma_{\otimes})\bigl(\Upsilon(\pi_{\boxtimes,2}(a_{2}))\bigr)\\
&=(\Delta_{\mathbb{L},\mathbb{H}}\otimes\mathrm{id}^{\otimes 4})\bigl(\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{*}_{[34]}u_{\mathbb{L}[1]}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{*}_{[14]}u_{\mathbb{L}[1]}\mathrm{W}^{*}_{\mathbb{L}[24]}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{*}_{[14]}\gamma_{2}(a_{2})_{[46]}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{[14]}\mathrm{W}_{\mathbb{L}[24]}u_{\mathbb{L}[1]}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{[14]}u_{\mathbb{L}[1]}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{[34]}\bigr)\\
&=\mathrm{W}_{\mathbb{L}[12]}^{*}\mathrm{W}_{\mathbb{L}[13]}^{*}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{*}_{[45]}u_{\mathbb{L}[2]}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{*}_{[25]}u_{\mathbb{L}[2]}\mathrm{W}^{*}_{\mathbb{L}[35]}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{*}_{[25]}\gamma_{2}(a_{2})_{[57]}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{[25]}\mathrm{W}_{\mathbb{L}[35]}u_{\mathbb{L}[2]}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{[25]}u_{\mathbb{L}[2]}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{[45]}\mathrm{W}_{\mathbb{L}[13]}\mathrm{W}_{\mathbb{L}[12]}\\
&=\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{*}_{[45]}u_{\mathbb{L}[2]}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{*}_{[25]}u_{\mathbb{L}[2]}\mathrm{W}_{\mathbb{L}[12]}^{*}\mathrm{W}^{*}_{\mathbb{L}[35]}\mathrm{W}^{*}_{\mathbb{L}[15]}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{*}_{[25]}\gamma_{2}(a_{2})_{[57]}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{[25]}\mathrm{W}_{\mathbb{L}[15]}\mathrm{W}_{\mathbb{L}[35]}\mathrm{W}_{\mathbb{L}[12]}u_{\mathbb{L}[2]}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{[25]}u_{\mathbb{L}[2]}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{[45]}.\end{split}

Using the assumption that
\widehat{\mathcal{X}}
is an
\operatorname{R}
-matrix, we have
\mathrm{W}^{*}_{\mathbb{L}[12]}\mathrm{W}^{*}_{\mathbb{L}[15]}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{*}_{[25]}=\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{[25]}^{*}\mathrm{W}_{\mathbb{L}[15]}^{*}\mathrm{W}_{\mathbb{L}[12]}^{*}
, hence

\begin{split}&\quad\;(\mathrm{id}\otimes\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}(\gamma_{\otimes}))\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}(\gamma_{\otimes})_{|\mathbb{L}}\bigl(\Upsilon(\pi_{\boxtimes,2}(a_{2}))\bigr)\\
&=\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{*}_{[45]}u_{\mathbb{L}[2]}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{*}_{[25]}u_{\mathbb{L}[2]}\mathrm{W}^{*}_{\mathbb{L}[35]}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{*}_{[25]}\mathrm{W}^{*}_{\mathbb{L}[15]}\mathrm{W}_{\mathbb{L}[12]}^{*}\gamma_{2}(a_{2})_{[57]}\mathrm{W}_{\mathbb{L}[12]}\mathrm{W}_{\mathbb{L}[15]}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{[25]}\mathrm{W}_{\mathbb{L}[35]}u_{\mathbb{L}[2]}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{[25]}u_{\mathbb{L}[2]}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{[45]}.\end{split}

On the other hand

\begin{split}&\quad\;(\mathrm{id}\otimes\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}(\gamma_{\otimes})\Upsilon)(\gamma_{1}\bowtie\gamma_{2})\bigl(\pi_{\boxtimes,2}(a_{2})\bigr)=(\mathrm{id}\otimes\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}(\gamma_{\otimes})\Upsilon)(\mathrm{id}\otimes\pi_{\boxtimes,2})\gamma_{2}(a_{2})\\
&=\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{*}_{[45]}u_{\mathbb{L}[2]}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{*}_{[25]}u_{\mathbb{L}[2]}\mathrm{W}^{*}_{\mathbb{L}[35]}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{*}_{[25]}(\mathrm{id}\otimes\gamma_{2})\gamma_{2}(a_{2})_{[157]}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{[25]}\mathrm{W}_{\mathbb{L}[35]}u_{\mathbb{L}[2]}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{[25]}u_{\mathbb{L}[2]}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{[45]}\\
&=\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{*}_{[45]}u_{\mathbb{L}[2]}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{*}_{[25]}u_{\mathbb{L}[2]}\mathrm{W}^{*}_{\mathbb{L}[35]}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}^{*}_{[25]}\mathrm{W}^{*}_{\mathbb{L}[15]}\gamma_{2}(a_{2})_{[57]}\mathrm{W}_{\mathbb{L}[15]}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{[25]}\mathrm{W}_{\mathbb{L}[35]}u_{\mathbb{L}[2]}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{[25]}u_{\mathbb{L}[2]}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{[45]}.\end{split}

Since
\mathrm{id}\otimes\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}(\gamma_{\otimes})
is injective, this proves (
5.23
).

Finally, we need to show (
5.21
). Since the
\mathbb{H}
-action
\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}(\gamma_{\otimes})
restricts to the
\mathbb{L}
-action
\gamma_{1}\bowtie\gamma_{2}
(up to the isomorphism
\Upsilon
), we can conclude that
(U_{\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}(\gamma_{\otimes})})_{|\mathbb{L}}\cong U_{\gamma_{1}\bowtie\gamma_{2}}
. The restricted representation is characterised by

(\mathrm{id}\otimes\iota_{\mathbb{L}})(\mathrm{W}^{*}_{\mathbb{L}})_{[123]}U_{\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}(\gamma_{\otimes})[2345]}(\mathrm{id}\otimes\iota_{\mathbb{L}})(\mathrm{W}_{\mathbb{L}})_{[123]}=(U_{\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}(\gamma_{\otimes})})_{|\mathbb{L}[145]}U_{\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}(\gamma_{\otimes})[2345]}.

(5.25)

Using the formula for
U_{\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}(\gamma_{\otimes})}
from Theorem
5.11
, transported to
L^{2}(A_{1})\otimes L^{2}(A_{2})
, we calculate

\begin{split}&\quad\;(\mathrm{id}\otimes\iota_{\mathbb{L}})(\mathrm{W}^{*}_{\mathbb{L}})_{[123]}I_{\boxtimes[45]}^{*}U_{\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}(\gamma_{\otimes})[2345]}I_{\boxtimes[45]}(\mathrm{id}\otimes\iota_{\mathbb{L}})(\mathrm{W}_{\mathbb{L}})_{[123]}\\
&=\mathrm{W}_{\mathbb{L}[12]}^{*}\mathrm{W}_{\mathbb{L}[13]}^{*}(\hat{\pi}_{U_{\gamma_{1}}}\otimes\hat{\pi}_{U_{\gamma_{2}}})(\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{u})_{[45]}(\hat{\pi}_{1,\mathrm{red}}\otimes\hat{\pi}_{U_{\gamma_{2}}})(\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{u})_{[25]}U_{\gamma_{1}[24]}U_{\gamma_{2}[35]}\\
&\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad u_{\mathbb{L}[2]}(\hat{\pi}_{1,\mathrm{red}}\otimes\hat{\pi}_{U_{\gamma_{2}}})(\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{u})_{[25]}u_{\mathbb{L}[2]}\mathrm{W}_{\mathbb{L}[13]}\mathrm{W}_{\mathbb{L}[12]}\\
&=\mathrm{W}_{\mathbb{L}[12]}^{*}(\hat{\pi}_{U_{\gamma_{1}}}\otimes\hat{\pi}_{U_{\gamma_{2}}})(\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{u})_{[45]}(\hat{\pi}_{1,\mathrm{red}}\otimes\hat{\pi}_{U_{\gamma_{2}}})(\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{u})_{[25]}U_{\gamma_{2}[15]}\mathrm{W}_{\mathbb{L}[12]}U_{\gamma_{1}[14]}U_{\gamma_{1}[24]}U_{\gamma_{2}[35]}\\
&\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad u_{\mathbb{L}[2]}(\hat{\pi}_{1,\mathrm{red}}\otimes\hat{\pi}_{U_{\gamma_{2}}})(\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{u})_{[25]}u_{\mathbb{L}[2]}.\end{split}

Observe that (see
[
DCKr24
]
*Remark 2.2)

\begin{split}&\quad\;\mathrm{W}_{\mathbb{L}[12]}^{*}(\hat{\pi}_{U_{\gamma_{1}}}\otimes\hat{\pi}_{U_{\gamma_{2}}})(\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{u})_{[45]}(\hat{\pi}_{1,\mathrm{red}}\otimes\hat{\pi}_{U_{\gamma_{2}}})(\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{u})_{[25]}U_{\gamma_{2}[15]}\mathrm{W}_{\mathbb{L}[12]}U_{\gamma_{1}[14]}\\
&=(\mathrm{id}\otimes\hat{\pi}_{1,\mathrm{red}}\otimes\hat{\pi}_{U_{\gamma_{1}}}\otimes\hat{\pi}_{U_{\gamma_{2}}})\bigl(\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{W}$}}\>\!^{*}_{\mathbb{L}[12]}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{u[34]}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{u[24]}\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{W}$}}\>\!_{\mathbb{L}[14]}\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{W}$}}\>\!_{\mathbb{L}[12]}\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{W}$}}\>\!_{\mathbb{L}[13]}\bigr)_{[1245]}\\
&=(\mathrm{id}\otimes\hat{\pi}_{1,\mathrm{red}}\otimes\hat{\pi}_{U_{\gamma_{1}}}\otimes\hat{\pi}_{U_{\gamma_{2}}})\bigl(\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{W}$}}\>\!^{*}_{\mathbb{L}[12]}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{u[34]}\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{W}$}}\>\!_{\mathbb{L}[12]}\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{W}$}}\>\!_{\mathbb{L}[14]}\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{W}$}}\>\!_{\mathbb{L}[13]}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{u[24]}\bigr)_{[1245]}\\
&=(\mathrm{id}\otimes\hat{\pi}_{1,\mathrm{red}}\otimes\hat{\pi}_{U_{\gamma_{1}}}\otimes\hat{\pi}_{U_{\gamma_{2}}})\bigl(\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{W}$}}\>\!_{\mathbb{L}[13]}\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{W}$}}\>\!_{\mathbb{L}[14]}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{u[34]}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{u[24]}\bigr)_{[1245]}\\
&=U_{\gamma_{1}[14]}U_{\gamma_{2}[15]}(\hat{\pi}_{U_{\gamma_{1}}}\otimes\hat{\pi}_{U_{\gamma_{2}}})(\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{u})_{[45]}(\hat{\pi}_{1,\mathrm{red}}\otimes\hat{\pi}_{U_{\gamma_{2}}})(\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{u})_{[25]}.\end{split}

Using this, we continue

\begin{split}&\quad\;(\mathrm{id}\otimes\iota_{\mathbb{L}})(\mathrm{W}^{*}_{\mathbb{L}})_{[123]}I_{\boxtimes[45]}^{*}U_{\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}(\gamma_{\otimes})[2345]}I_{\boxtimes[45]}(\mathrm{id}\otimes\iota_{\mathbb{L}})(\mathrm{W}_{\mathbb{L}})_{[123]}\\
&=U_{\gamma_{1}[14]}U_{\gamma_{2}[15]}(\hat{\pi}_{U_{\gamma_{1}}}\otimes\hat{\pi}_{U_{\gamma_{2}}})(\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{u})_{[45]}(\hat{\pi}_{1,\mathrm{red}}\otimes\hat{\pi}_{U_{\gamma_{2}}})(\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{u})_{[25]}U_{\gamma_{1}[24]}U_{\gamma_{2}[35]}u_{\mathbb{L}[2]}(\hat{\pi}_{1,\mathrm{red}}\otimes\hat{\pi}_{U_{\gamma_{2}}})(\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{u})_{[25]}u_{\mathbb{L}[2]}\\
&=(U_{\gamma_{1}}\otop U_{\gamma_{2}})_{[145]}I_{\boxtimes[45]}^{*}U_{\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{X}}(\gamma_{\otimes})[2345]}I_{\boxtimes[45]}.\end{split}

Equation (
5.25
) ends the proof.
∎

5.3.
Application to generalized Drinfeld double

In this subsection we apply our results to obtain (to our knowledge – new) information concerning the topological structure of (generalized) Drinfeld doubles. Let us stress that we do not make any regularity assumptions.

We keep the “dual” notation, to stay in agreement with the rest of the article. As in Section
5.1
, let
\mathbb{G}_{1}=(M_{1},\Delta_{1}),\mathbb{G}_{2}=(M_{2},\Delta_{2})
be LCQGs with a unitary skew bicharacter
\widehat{\mathcal{X}}\in\hat{M}_{1}\bar{\otimes}\hat{M}_{2}
and unitary
2
-cocycle
\hat{\Omega}=\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{[32]}
. Then we can consider (see Section
1.5
) the generalized Drinfeld double

D_{\widehat{\mathcal{X}}}(\hat{\mathbb{G}}_{1},\hat{\mathbb{G}}_{2})=(\hat{M}_{1}\bar{\otimes}\hat{M}_{2},\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{[32]}\hat{\Delta}_{\otimes}(-)\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{[32]}^{*}).

In what follows, we will use the comultiplication
\hat{\Delta}_{\otimes,u}
on
C^{*}(\mathbb{G}_{1}\times\mathbb{G}_{2})=C_{0}^{u}(\hat{\mathbb{G}}_{1})\otimes_{\max}C_{0}^{u}(\hat{\mathbb{G}}_{2})
, given by
\hat{\Delta}_{\otimes,u}(x\otimes y)=\hat{\Delta}_{1,u}(x)_{[13]}\hat{\Delta}_{2,u}(y)_{[24]}
, see Lemma
5.1
.

Proposition 5.16
.

There is an isomorphism
C_{0}^{u}(D_{\widehat{\mathcal{X}}}(\hat{\mathbb{G}}_{1},\hat{\mathbb{G}}_{2}))\cong C^{u}_{0}(\hat{\mathbb{G}}_{1})\otimes_{\max}C^{u}_{0}(\hat{\mathbb{G}}_{2})
, under which:

(1)

the comultiplication on
C_{0}^{u}(D_{\widehat{\mathcal{X}}}(\hat{\mathbb{G}}_{1},\hat{\mathbb{G}}_{2}))
corresponds to
\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{u[32]}\hat{\Delta}_{\otimes,u}(-)\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{u[32]}^{*}
,

(2)

the reducing map of
D_{\widehat{\mathcal{X}}}(\hat{\mathbb{G}}_{1},\hat{\mathbb{G}}_{2})
corresponds to
(\hat{\pi}_{1,\mathrm{red}}\otimes\hat{\pi}_{2,\mathrm{red}})\hat{\theta}
, where
\hat{\theta}
is the tensor product reducing map
C^{u}_{0}(\hat{\mathbb{G}}_{1})\otimes_{\max}C^{u}_{0}(\hat{\mathbb{G}}_{2})\rightarrow C^{u}_{0}(\hat{\mathbb{G}}_{1})\otimes C^{u}_{0}(\hat{\mathbb{G}}_{2})
,

(3)

the counit of
D_{\widehat{\mathcal{X}}}(\hat{\mathbb{G}}_{1},\hat{\mathbb{G}}_{2})
corresponds to
\varepsilon_{\hat{\mathbb{G}}_{1}}\times\varepsilon_{\hat{\mathbb{G}}_{2}}
.

Furthermore,
C_{0}(D_{\widehat{\mathcal{X}}}(\hat{\mathbb{G}}_{1},\hat{\mathbb{G}}_{2}))=C_{0}(\hat{\mathbb{G}}_{1})\otimes C_{0}(\hat{\mathbb{G}}_{2})
.

Remark 5.17
.

•

The equality
C_{0}(D_{\widehat{\mathcal{X}}}(\hat{\mathbb{G}}_{1},\hat{\mathbb{G}}_{2}))=C_{0}(\hat{\mathbb{G}}_{1})\otimes C_{0}(\hat{\mathbb{G}}_{2})
at the reduced level is known from
[
MNW03
]
, see in particular Section
8
and Theorem
8.7
therein. See also
[
Roy15
]
*Theorem 5.3 for a more general result in the setting of multiplicative unitaries.

•

One can express the unitary antipode on
C_{0}^{u}(D_{\widehat{\mathcal{X}}}(\hat{\mathbb{G}}_{1},\hat{\mathbb{G}}_{2}))
using the universal analog of (
4.6
) and the element
X_{\hat{\Omega},u}
(see Lemma
4.9
and Lemma
5.4
).

Proof of Proposition
5.16
.

Theorem
5.2
states that
\hat{\Omega}_{u}=\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{u[32]}
is a universal lift of
\hat{\Omega}=\hat{\mathcal{X}}_{[32]}
(in the sense of Definition
4.3
). This immediately gives us an isomorphism
C_{0}^{u}(D_{\widehat{\mathcal{X}}}(\hat{\mathbb{G}}_{1},\hat{\mathbb{G}}_{2}))\cong C^{u}_{0}(\hat{\mathbb{G}}_{1})\otimes_{\max}C^{u}_{0}(\hat{\mathbb{G}}_{2})
for which points
(2),(3)
hold, and it follows that
C_{0}(D_{\widehat{\mathcal{X}}}(\hat{\mathbb{G}}_{1},\hat{\mathbb{G}}_{2}))=C_{0}(\hat{\mathbb{G}}_{1})\otimes C_{0}(\hat{\mathbb{G}}_{2})
. Next, denote by
\mathscr{G}
the linking quantum groupoid constructed from
\hat{\Omega}
as in Section
4
and let
\hat{\Delta}_{u,ij}
be the ingredients of the comultiplication on
C^{*}(\mathscr{G})
. In particular, we have
D_{\widehat{\mathcal{X}}}(\hat{\mathbb{G}}_{1},\hat{\mathbb{G}}_{2})
and
\hat{\mathbb{G}}_{1}\times\hat{\mathbb{G}}_{2}
as two diagonal corners. Point
(1)
follows from the calculation

\Delta_{D_{\widehat{\mathcal{X}}}(\hat{\mathbb{G}}_{1},\hat{\mathbb{G}}_{2}),u}(z)=\hat{\Delta}_{11,u}(z)=\hat{\Delta}_{12,u}(1)\hat{\Delta}_{22,u}(z)\hat{\Delta}_{12,u}(1)^{*}=\hat{\Omega}_{u}\hat{\Delta}_{\otimes,u}(z)\hat{\Omega}_{u}^{*}=\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{[32]}\hat{\Delta}_{\otimes,u}(z)\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{[32]}^{*},

where
z\in C_{0}^{u}(\hat{\mathbb{G}}_{1})\otimes_{\max}C_{0}^{u}(\hat{\mathbb{G}}_{2})
and where
\Delta_{D_{\widehat{\mathcal{X}}}(\hat{\mathbb{G}}_{1},\hat{\mathbb{G}}_{2}),u}
is the universal comultiplication on
D_{\widehat{\mathcal{X}}}(\hat{\mathbb{G}}_{1},\hat{\mathbb{G}}_{2})
, transported to
C_{0}^{u}(\hat{\mathbb{G}}_{1})\otimes_{\max}C_{0}^{u}(\hat{\mathbb{G}}_{2})
.
∎

5.4.
Proof of Theorem
5.2

To prove Theorem
5.2
, we start with some general preparations.

Lemma 5.18
.

Let
\mathbb{H}
be a locally compact quantum group, with right half-lifted multiplicative unitary
\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{W}$}}\>\!\in\operatorname{M}(C_{0}(\mathbb{H})\otimes C^{*}(\mathbb{H}))
. Then the dual coproduct lifts uniquely to a non-degenerate
*
-homomorphism

\hat{\Delta}_{u}^{\max}\colon C^{*}(\mathbb{H})\rightarrow\operatorname{M}(C^{*}(\mathbb{H})\otimes_{\max}C^{*}(\mathbb{H}))

such that

\hat{\Delta}_{u}^{\max}((\omega\otimes\mathrm{id})\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{W}$}}\>\!)=(\omega\otimes\mathrm{id}\otimes\mathrm{id})(\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{W}$}}\>\!_{[13]}\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{W}$}}\>\!_{[12]}),\qquad\forall\omega\in L^{1}(\mathbb{H}).

(5.26)

Here we consider the product of
\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{W}$}}\>\!_{[13]}
and
\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{W}$}}\>\!_{[12]}
in
\operatorname{M}(C_{0}(\mathbb{H})\otimes(C^{*}(\mathbb{H})\otimes_{\max}C^{*}(\mathbb{H})))
.

Proof.

We recall that by its universal property, non-degenerate
*
-representations
\hat{\pi}
of
C^{*}(\mathbb{H})\otimes_{\max}C^{*}(\mathbb{H})
on a Hilbert space
\mathcal{H}
are in one-to-one correspondence with non-degenerate
*
-representations
\hat{\pi}_{1},\hat{\pi}_{2}
of
C^{*}(\mathbb{H})
on
\mathcal{H}
which pointwise commute. This correspondence is given by

\hat{\pi}=\hat{\pi}_{1}\times\hat{\pi}_{2},\qquad(\hat{\pi}_{1}\times\hat{\pi}_{2})(x\otimes y)=\hat{\pi}_{1}(x)\hat{\pi}_{2}(y),\qquad\forall x,y\in C^{*}(\mathbb{H}).

Such
*
-representations
\hat{\pi}_{1},\hat{\pi}_{2}
are implemented by commuting unitary representations
U_{\hat{\pi}_{1}},U_{\hat{\pi}_{2}}
of
\mathbb{H}
on
\mathcal{H}_{\hat{\pi}}
,

U_{\hat{\pi}_{i}}\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{H})\bar{\otimes}\mathcal{B}(\mathcal{H}_{\hat{\pi}}),\qquad U_{\hat{\pi}_{1}[13]}U_{\hat{\pi}_{2}[23]}=U_{\hat{\pi}_{2}[23]}U_{\hat{\pi}_{1}[13]}.

By the latter commutation relation, we have that

U_{\hat{\pi}_{2}\hat{\pi}_{1}}:=U_{\hat{\pi}_{2}}U_{\hat{\pi}_{1}}\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{H})\bar{\otimes}\mathcal{B}(\mathcal{H}_{\hat{\pi}})

is a unitary representation of
\mathbb{H}
too, and is hence associated to a non-degenerate
*
-representation
[
Kus01
]
*Proposition 6.5

\hat{\pi}_{2}\hat{\pi}_{1}\colon C^{*}(\mathbb{H})\rightarrow\mathcal{B}(\mathcal{H}_{\hat{\pi}}).

As

U_{\hat{\pi}_{i}}\in\operatorname{M}(C_{0}(\mathbb{H})\otimes\hat{\pi}(C^{*}(\mathbb{H})\otimes_{\max}C^{*}(\mathbb{H}))),

we deduce that
\hat{\pi}_{2}\hat{\pi}_{1}
is a non-degenerate
*
-homomorphism into
\operatorname{M}(\hat{\pi}(C^{*}(\mathbb{H})\otimes_{\max}C^{*}(\mathbb{H})))
. Taking for
\hat{\pi}
a universal non-degenerate
*
-representation of
C^{*}(\mathbb{H})\otimes_{\max}C^{*}(\mathbb{H})
with the associated
\hat{\pi}_{1},\hat{\pi}_{2}
, we can then define
\hat{\Delta}_{u}^{\max}
as the unique non-degenerate
*
-homomorphism
C^{*}(\mathbb{H})\rightarrow\operatorname{M}(C^{*}(\mathbb{H})\otimes_{\max}C^{*}(\mathbb{H}))
such that

\hat{\pi}_{2}\hat{\pi}_{1}=\hat{\pi}\circ\hat{\Delta}_{u}^{\max}.

We then have by construction that

\begin{split}&\quad\;(\mathrm{id}\otimes\hat{\pi}\hat{\Delta}_{u}^{\max})(\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{W}$}}\>\!)=(\mathrm{id}\otimes\hat{\pi}_{2}\hat{\pi}_{1})\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{W}$}}\>\!=U_{\hat{\pi}_{2}}U_{\hat{\pi}_{1}}=(\mathrm{id}\otimes\hat{\pi}_{2})(\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{W}$}}\>\!)(\mathrm{id}\otimes\hat{\pi}_{1})(\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{W}$}}\>\!)\\
&=(\mathrm{id}\otimes(\hat{\pi}_{1}\times\hat{\pi}_{2}))(\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{W}$}}\>\!_{[13]}\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{W}$}}\>\!_{[12]})=(\mathrm{id}\otimes\hat{\pi})(\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{W}$}}\>\!_{[13]}\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{W}$}}\>\!_{[12]}),\end{split}

(5.27)

so that (
5.26
) is satisfied. In particular,
\hat{\Delta}_{u}^{\max}
does not depend on the choice of
\hat{\pi}
. As

C^{*}(\mathbb{H})=[(\omega\otimes\mathrm{id})\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{W}$}}\>\!\mid\omega\in L^{1}(\mathbb{H})],

it is clear that this latter formula completely determines
\hat{\Delta}_{u}^{\max}
.
∎

It is easy to see from the above construction, or directly from (
5.26
), that
\hat{\Delta}_{u}^{\max}
is still coassociative in the appropriate way:

(\mathrm{id}\otimes_{\max}\hat{\Delta}_{u}^{\max})\hat{\Delta}_{u}^{\max}=(\hat{\Delta}_{u}^{\max}\otimes_{\max}\mathrm{id})\hat{\Delta}_{u}^{\max}.

In the following, we will also use this result for C
∗
-algebras associated to linking quantum groupoids (the proof is completely the same).

The following lemma is just an easy observation on linking C
∗
-algebras.

Lemma 5.19
.

Let
A=\begin{pmatrix}A_{11}&A_{12}\\
A_{21}&A_{22}\end{pmatrix}
be a linking C
∗
-algebra, i.e.
[A_{ij}A_{jk}]=A_{ik}
for all indices
i,j,k\in\{1,2\}
. Assume that
B
is another linking C
∗
-algebra, and let

\pi\colon A\rightarrow\operatorname{M}(B)

be a non-degenerate
*
-homomorphism preserving the projection
e_{1}=\begin{pmatrix}1&0\\
0&0\end{pmatrix}\in\operatorname{M}(A)
and hence also
e_{2}=1-e_{1}
. Write

\pi_{ij}=\pi_{\mid A_{ij}}\colon A_{ij}\rightarrow\operatorname{M}(B_{ij}):=e_{i}\operatorname{M}(B)e_{j}.

Then if

A_{22}\cong_{\pi_{22}}B_{22},

we have that
\pi
is an isomorphism
A\cong_{\pi}B
.

Proof.

We have

\pi_{12}(A_{12})B_{22}=\pi_{12}(A_{12})\pi_{22}(A_{22})=\pi_{12}(A_{12}A_{22}),

so we see that in fact
\pi_{12}(A_{12})\subseteq B_{12}
. It follows easily from this that
\pi(A)\subseteq B
. Moreover, since

\pi_{12}(x)^{*}\pi_{12}(x)=\pi_{22}(x^{*}x),\qquad\forall x\in A_{12},

we see that
\pi_{12}
is injective, and then is
\pi_{21}
. To see that also
\pi_{11}
is injective, take
x\in\mathrm{Ker}(\pi_{11})
and notice that then
\pi_{12}(xA_{12})=\pi_{11}(x)\pi_{12}(A_{12})=\{0\}
, hence
xA_{12}=\{0\}
. Consequently
xA_{11}=\{0\}
and
x=0
. In particular,
\pi
is injective.

Finally, to see that
\pi
is surjective, it is sufficient to show that
\pi_{12}
is surjective. Take a bounded net
(x_{i})_{i\in I}
in
A_{11}
that strictly converges to
1
. We may assume each
x_{i}
is a finite sum
\sum_{j=1}^{N_{i}}x_{ij}x_{ij}^{*}
with
x_{ij}\in A_{12}
. Then as
\pi
is non-degenerate, also
\bigl(\sum_{j=1}^{N_{i}}\pi_{12}(x_{ij})\pi_{12}(x_{ij})^{*}\bigr)_{i\in I}
converges strictly to
1_{11}\in\operatorname{M}(B_{11})
, and hence

y=\lim_{i\in I}\sum_{j=1}^{N_{i}}\pi_{12}(x_{ij})(\pi_{12}(x_{ij})^{*}y)\in[\pi_{12}(A_{12})B_{22}]\subseteq\pi_{12}(A_{12})

for any
y\in B_{12}
. This shows
\pi_{12}
is surjective. Finally,

B_{11}=[B_{12}B_{21}]=[\pi_{12}(A_{12})\pi_{21}(A_{21})]=\pi_{11}(A_{11}).

Note that we have used that
\pi_{11}
and
\pi_{12}
have closed image.
∎

Let us now return to the setting of Theorem
5.2
.

We denote by
\mathscr{G}
the linking quantum groupoid associated to
\mathbb{G}=\mathbb{G}_{1}\times\mathbb{G}_{2}
with respect to its dual unitary
2
-cocycle
\hat{\Omega}=\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{[32]}
(see Section
1.5
). Recall that the universal linking W
∗
-algebra associated to
\mathscr{G}
is

W^{*}(\mathscr{G})=\begin{pmatrix}W^{*}(\mathbb{G}_{11})&W^{*}(\mathbb{G}_{12})\\
W^{*}(\mathbb{G}_{21})&W^{*}(\mathbb{G}_{22})\end{pmatrix}.

We can
*
-represent all the components of
L^{\infty}(\mathscr{G})
on the same Hilbert space,

L^{\infty}(\mathbb{G}_{ij})\subseteq\mathcal{B}(L^{2}(\mathbb{G}_{1})\otimes L^{2}(\mathbb{G}_{2})).

Now for
\mathbb{G}_{i}
, we consider the trivial linking quantum groupoid
\mathscr{G}_{i}=\mathbb{G}_{i}^{(2)}
, which is just the trivial linking W
∗
-algebra
\begin{pmatrix}\hat{M}_{i}&\hat{M}_{i}\\
\hat{M}_{i}&\hat{M}_{i}\end{pmatrix}
with
\hat{\Delta}_{i}
at all components. Its dual is the direct sum von Neumann algebra

L^{\infty}(\mathbb{G}_{i}^{(2)})=\bigoplus_{k,l=1}^{2}L^{\infty}(\mathbb{G}_{i})

with
\Delta_{ab}^{c}=\Delta_{i}
at all components. We call
\mathbb{G}_{i}^{(2)}
the
amplification
of
\mathbb{G}_{i}
.

The following lemma will show that
\hat{\mathbb{G}}_{i}^{(2)}
is a closed linking quantum subgroupoid of
\hat{\mathscr{G}}
. Note that we already used this observation in the proof of Lemma
5.4
, but this relied on the fact that we knew the cocycle twist of
C^{*}(\mathbb{G})
provided a linking quantum groupoid, making use of Theorem
5.2
.

Lemma 5.20
.

For
i\in\{1,2\}
we have a normal, unital embedding

\pi^{(i)}\colon L^{\infty}(\mathbb{G}_{i}^{(2)})\rightarrow L^{\infty}(\mathscr{G})

which splits into
\pi^{(i)}_{kl}\colon L^{\infty}(\mathbb{G}_{i})\rightarrow L^{\infty}(\mathbb{G}_{kl})
, given by

\pi^{(1)}_{kl}(x_{1})=x_{1}\otimes 1,\qquad\pi^{(2)}_{kl}(x_{2})=\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{kl}^{*}(1\otimes x_{2})\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{kl},\qquad\forall x_{i}\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{G}_{i}),

(5.28)

where

\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{22}=1\otimes 1,\quad\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{12}=\widehat{\mathcal{X}},\quad\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{21}=(\hat{J}_{1}\otimes\hat{J}_{2})\widehat{\mathcal{X}}(\hat{J}_{1}\otimes\hat{J}_{2}),\quad\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{11}=\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{12}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{21}.

The maps
\pi^{(i)},\pi^{(i)}_{kl}
respect coproducts, in the sense that

\Delta^{a}_{kl}\pi^{(i)}_{kl}=(\pi^{(i)}_{ka}\otimes\pi^{(i)}_{al})\Delta_{i},\quad\Delta_{L^{\infty}(\mathscr{G})}\pi^{(i)}=(\pi^{(i)}\otimes\pi^{(i)})\Delta_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{G}_{i}^{(2)})}

for
i,a,k,l\in\{1,2\}
. Furthermore

L^{\infty}(\mathbb{G}_{kl})=\overline{\operatorname{span}}^{\,\sigma\textrm{-weak}}\,\{\pi^{(1)}_{kl}(x_{1})\pi^{(2)}_{kl}(x_{2})\mid x_{i}\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{G}_{i})\}.

Proof.

Recall that
J_{N}=(J_{1}\otimes J_{2})\widehat{\mathcal{X}}
(Lemma
5.5
). Simplifying the expressions (
4.4
) in this specific case, one can compute that the components of
\hat{\mathrm{W}}
, the left regular multiplicative partial isometry for
W^{*}(\mathscr{G})
, are given by

\hat{\mathrm{W}}_{kl}^{a}=\hat{\mathrm{W}}_{1[13]}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{kl[34]}^{*}\hat{\mathrm{W}}_{2[24]}\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{kl[34]}=(\mathrm{id}\otimes\pi^{(1)}_{kl})(\hat{\mathrm{W}}_{1})_{[134]}(\mathrm{id}\otimes\pi^{(2)}_{kl})(\hat{\mathrm{W}}_{2})_{[234]}

(5.29)

for
a,k,l\in\{1,2\}
(note that the formula for
\hat{\mathrm{W}}^{a}_{11}
gives the form of the multiplicative unitary of the generalized quantum double as given in
[
BV05
]
*Theorem 5.3). As the
L^{\infty}(\mathbb{G}_{kl})
are obtained by slicing the first leg of
\hat{\mathrm{W}}_{kl}
, the last statement of the lemma follows immediately from (
5.29
). Applying
\Delta_{kl}^{a}
to legs
3,4
of (
5.29
) and using (
1.7
) gives us

\begin{split}&\quad\;(\mathrm{id}\otimes\Delta^{a}_{kl}\pi^{(1)}_{kl})(\hat{\mathrm{W}}_{1})_{[13456]}(\mathrm{id}\otimes\Delta^{a}_{kl}\pi^{(2)}_{kl})(\hat{\mathrm{W}}_{2})_{[23456]}=(\mathrm{id}\otimes\mathrm{id}\otimes\Delta^{a}_{kl})(\hat{\mathrm{W}}^{a}_{kl})=\hat{\mathrm{W}}^{a}_{al[1256]}\hat{\mathrm{W}}^{a}_{ka[1234]}\\
&=(\mathrm{id}\otimes\pi^{(1)}_{al})(\hat{\mathrm{W}}_{1})_{[156]}(\mathrm{id}\otimes\pi^{(2)}_{al})(\hat{\mathrm{W}}_{2})_{[256]}(\mathrm{id}\otimes\pi^{(1)}_{ka})(\hat{\mathrm{W}}_{1})_{[134]}(\mathrm{id}\otimes\pi^{(2)}_{ka})(\hat{\mathrm{W}}_{2})_{[234]}\\
&=(\mathrm{id}\otimes\pi^{(1)}_{al})(\hat{\mathrm{W}}_{1})_{[156]}(\mathrm{id}\otimes\pi^{(1)}_{ka})(\hat{\mathrm{W}}_{1})_{[134]}(\mathrm{id}\otimes\pi^{(2)}_{al})(\hat{\mathrm{W}}_{2})_{[256]}(\mathrm{id}\otimes\pi^{(2)}_{ka})(\hat{\mathrm{W}}_{2})_{[234]}\\
&=(\mathrm{id}\otimes\pi^{(1)}_{ka}\otimes\pi^{(1)}_{al})(\mathrm{id}\otimes\Delta_{1})(\hat{\mathrm{W}}_{1})_{[13456]}(\mathrm{id}\otimes\pi^{(2)}_{ka}\otimes\pi^{(2)}_{al})(\mathrm{id}\otimes\Delta_{2})(\hat{\mathrm{W}}_{2})_{[23456]},\end{split}

(5.30)

where
\Delta_{1},\Delta_{2}
are the comultiplications of respectively
\mathbb{G}_{1}
and
\mathbb{G}_{2}
. Since there is a net of normal functionals
\omega_{\lambda}\in L^{1}(\hat{\mathbb{G}}_{2})
such that
(\omega_{\lambda}\otimes\mathrm{id})(\hat{\mathrm{W}}_{2})\to 1
weak
∗
in
L^{\infty}(\mathbb{G}_{2})
, equation (
5.30
) gives us
\Delta^{a}_{kl}\pi^{(i)}_{kl}=(\pi^{(i)}_{ka}\otimes\pi^{(i)}_{al})\Delta_{i}
for
i=1
. The case
i=2
follows in a similar way. Then the identity

\Delta_{L^{\infty}(\mathscr{G})}\pi^{(i)}=(\pi^{(i)}\otimes\pi^{(i)})\Delta_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{G}_{i}^{(2)})}

is immediate.
∎

Lemma
5.18
in the case of
\mathbb{H}=\mathbb{G}_{1}\times\mathbb{G}_{2}
gives us a lift of the comultiplication
\hat{\Delta}_{\otimes,u}
on
C^{*}(\mathbb{G}_{1}\times\mathbb{G}_{2})
to a non-denegerate
*
-homomorphism

\hat{\Delta}_{\otimes,u}^{\max}\colon C^{*}(\mathbb{G}_{1}\times\mathbb{G}_{2})\rightarrow\operatorname{M}(C^{*}(\mathbb{G}_{1}\times\mathbb{G}_{2})\otimes_{\max}C^{*}(\mathbb{G}_{1}\times\mathbb{G}_{2})).

On the other hand, we can also consider the composition

\begin{split}&\quad\;\hat{\Delta}^{\max}_{u,\otimes}\colon C^{*}(\mathbb{G}_{1}\times\mathbb{G}_{2})=C^{*}(\mathbb{G}_{1})\otimes_{\max}C^{*}(\mathbb{G}_{2})\\
&\xrightarrow{\hat{\Delta}^{\max}_{1,u}\otimes_{\max}\hat{\Delta}^{\max}_{2,u}}\operatorname{M}(C^{*}(\mathbb{G}_{1})\otimes_{\max}C^{*}(\mathbb{G}_{1}))\otimes_{\max}\operatorname{M}(C^{*}(\mathbb{G}_{2})\otimes_{\max}C^{*}(\mathbb{G}_{2}))\\
&\longrightarrow\operatorname{M}(C^{*}(\mathbb{G}_{1})\otimes_{\max}C^{*}(\mathbb{G}_{1})\otimes_{\max}C^{*}(\mathbb{G}_{2})\otimes_{\max}C^{*}(\mathbb{G}_{2}))\\
&\cong\operatorname{M}(C^{*}(\mathbb{G}_{1})\otimes_{\max}C^{*}(\mathbb{G}_{2})\otimes_{\max}C^{*}(\mathbb{G}_{1})\otimes_{\max}C^{*}(\mathbb{G}_{2})),\end{split}

where the above arrow without label indicates the canonical non-degenerate
*
-homomorphism

\operatorname{M}(A)\otimes_{\max}\operatorname{M}(B)\rightarrow\operatorname{M}(A\otimes_{\max}B),

and where the isomorphism in the last line is the extension of the flip map

a\otimes b\otimes c\otimes d\mapsto a\otimes c\otimes b\otimes d.

Recalling that
\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{W}$}}\>\!_{\mathbb{G}_{1}\times\mathbb{G}_{2}}=\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{W}$}}\>\!_{\mathbb{G}_{1}[13]}\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{W}$}}\>\!_{\mathbb{G}_{2}[24]}
(Lemma
5.1
), we immediately conclude:

Lemma 5.21
.

The non-degenerate
*
-homomorphisms
\hat{\Delta}^{\max}_{\otimes,u}
and
\hat{\Delta}^{\max}_{u,\otimes}
are equal.

Lemma 5.22
.

The linking quantum groupoid
\mathscr{G}
, built using the unitary
2
-cocycle
\hat{\Omega}
for
(\hat{M}_{1}\bar{\otimes}\hat{M}_{2},\hat{\Delta}_{\otimes})
, has trivialisable universal linking C
∗
-algebra.

Proof.

Let us write the universal linking C
∗
-algebra associated to
\mathscr{G}
as

C^{*}(\mathscr{G})=\begin{pmatrix}C^{*}(\mathbb{H})&C^{*}(\mathbb{X})\\
C^{*}(\mathbb{Y})&C^{*}(\mathbb{G})\end{pmatrix}

(that
C^{*}(\mathscr{G})
has this form follows directly from the definition of the universal quantum groupoid C
∗
-algebra associated to a linking quantum groupoid, see e.g.
[
DC09
]
*Theorem 7.6.4). A linking quantum groupoid version of Lemma
5.18
gives a lift of
\Delta_{C^{*}(\mathscr{G})}
to a
*
-homomorphism

\Delta_{C^{*}(\mathscr{G})}^{\max}\colon C^{*}(\mathscr{G})\rightarrow\operatorname{M}(C^{*}(\mathscr{G})\otimes_{\max}C^{*}(\mathscr{G}))

which respects matrix decompositions. We can identify

C^{*}(\mathbb{G})=C^{*}(\mathbb{G}_{1})\otimes_{\max}C^{*}(\mathbb{G}_{2})

as C
∗
-algebras (Lemma
5.1
). But since

L^{\infty}(\mathbb{G}_{i}^{(2)})\subseteq L^{\infty}(\mathscr{G})

via an inclusion map which respects coproducts (Lemma
5.20
), we also have surjective, coproduct-preserving C
∗
-algebra morphisms

\hat{\pi}_{i}\colon C^{*}(\mathscr{G})\twoheadrightarrow\begin{pmatrix}C^{*}(\mathbb{G}_{i})&C^{*}(\mathbb{G}_{i})\\
C^{*}(\mathbb{G}_{i})&C^{*}(\mathbb{G}_{i})\end{pmatrix}

which respect the matrix structure. Using that the
22
-parts of the half-lifted Kac-Takesaki operators for
\hat{\mathscr{G}}
and
\widehat{\mathbb{G}_{i}^{(2)}}
agree with the corresponding operators for
\hat{\mathbb{G}}
and
\hat{\mathbb{G}}_{i}
, we see that
\hat{\pi}_{1,22}=\mathrm{id}\times\hat{\varepsilon}_{2}
and
\hat{\pi}_{2,22}=\hat{\varepsilon}_{1}\times\mathrm{id}
. We obtain the non-degenerate C
∗
-algebra morphism

\hat{\pi}=(\hat{\pi}_{1}\otimes_{\max}\hat{\pi}_{2})\Delta_{C^{*}(\mathscr{G})}^{\max}\colon C^{*}(\mathscr{G})\rightarrow\operatorname{M}\begin{pmatrix}C^{*}(\mathbb{G}_{1})\otimes_{\max}C^{*}(\mathbb{G}_{2})&C^{*}(\mathbb{G}_{1})\otimes_{\max}C^{*}(\mathbb{G}_{2})\\
C^{*}(\mathbb{G}_{1})\otimes_{\max}C^{*}(\mathbb{G}_{2})&C^{*}(\mathbb{G}_{1})\otimes_{\max}C^{*}(\mathbb{G}_{2})\end{pmatrix}.

(5.31)

Note that the codomain of
\hat{\pi}
is correct, because
\hat{\pi}_{i}
and
\Delta^{\max}_{C^{*}(\mathscr{G})}
preserve the matrix decomposition. Using Lemma
5.21
we conclude that
\hat{\pi}
restricts to the identity map on the lower right hand corner. Then it follows from Lemma
5.19
that
\hat{\pi}
must be an isomorphism

C^{*}(\mathscr{G})\cong_{\hat{\pi}}\begin{pmatrix}C^{*}(\mathbb{G}_{1})\otimes_{\max}C^{*}(\mathbb{G}_{2})&C^{*}(\mathbb{G}_{1})\otimes_{\max}C^{*}(\mathbb{G}_{2})\\
C^{*}(\mathbb{G}_{1})\otimes_{\max}C^{*}(\mathbb{G}_{2})&C^{*}(\mathbb{G}_{1})\otimes_{\max}C^{*}(\mathbb{G}_{2})\end{pmatrix}.

(5.32)

∎

To prove Theorem
5.2
, it remains to show that the particular element
\hat{\Omega}_{u}
is a universal lift of
\hat{\Omega}
. This will again require some preparations.

Note that in the proof of Theorem
5.22
, we obtained in particular an isomorphism of right Hilbert C
∗
-modules over
C^{*}(\mathbb{G}_{1})\otimes_{\max}C^{*}(\mathbb{G}_{2})
:

\hat{\pi}_{12}\colon C^{*}(\mathbb{G}_{12})\cong C^{*}(\mathbb{G}_{1})\otimes_{\max}C^{*}(\mathbb{G}_{2}).

(5.33)

Remark 5.23
.

For
k\in\{1,2\}
, let

\varphi^{(k)}=(\varphi_{ij}^{(k)})_{i,j=1}^{2}\colon A^{(k)}=\begin{pmatrix}A_{11}^{(k)}&A_{12}^{(k)}\\
A_{21}^{(k)}&A_{22}^{(k)}\end{pmatrix}\rightarrow\operatorname{M}(B^{(k)})=\operatorname{M}\biggl(\!\begin{pmatrix}B_{11}^{(k)}&B_{12}^{(k)}\\
B_{21}^{(k)}&B_{22}^{(k)}\end{pmatrix}\!\biggr)

be a non-degenerate
*
-homomorphism between C
∗
-algebras which preserves the matrix structure. Then the tensor product

\varphi^{(1)}\otimes_{\max}\varphi^{(2)}\colon A^{(1)}\otimes_{\max}A^{(2)}\rightarrow\operatorname{M}(B^{(1)}\otimes_{\max}B^{(2)})

can be (co-)restricted to a map

\varphi^{(1)}_{12}\otimes_{\max}\varphi^{(2)}_{12}\colon A^{(1)}_{12}\otimes_{\max}A^{(2)}_{12}\rightarrow\operatorname{M}(B^{(1)}_{12}\otimes_{\max}B^{(2)}_{12}),

where
A^{(1)}_{12}\otimes_{\max}A^{(2)}_{12}
is a corner in
A^{(1)}\otimes_{\max}A^{(2)}
(and similarly for
B
). In particular, we will consider the isomorphism

\hat{\pi}_{12}=(\hat{\pi}_{1,12}\otimes_{\max}\hat{\pi}_{2,12})\hat{\Delta}_{u,12}^{\max}.

Note that if
A^{(k)}_{ij}=C^{(k)}
for some C
∗
-algebra
C^{(k)}
and all
i,j\in\{1,2\}
(so that
A^{(k)}=C^{(k)}\otimes M_{2}
), then because the matrix algebras are nuclear,
A^{(1)}_{12}\otimes_{\max}A^{(2)}_{12}
agrees with the usual maximal tensor product of C
∗
-algebras
C^{(1)}\otimes_{\max}C^{(2)}
.

We can use the map
\hat{\pi}_{12}
from (
5.33
) to transport
\hat{\Delta}_{u,12}
to a map

\widetilde{\Delta}_{u,12}:=(\hat{\pi}_{12}\otimes\hat{\pi}_{12})\hat{\Delta}_{u,12}\hat{\pi}_{12}^{-1}\colon C^{*}(\mathbb{G}_{1})\otimes_{\max}C^{*}(\mathbb{G}_{2})\rightarrow\operatorname{M}((C^{*}(\mathbb{G}_{1})\otimes_{\max}C^{*}(\mathbb{G}_{2}))\otimes(C^{*}(\mathbb{G}_{1})\otimes_{\max}C^{*}(\mathbb{G}_{2})))

and similarly
\hat{\Delta}^{\max}_{u,12}
to

\begin{split}\widetilde{\Delta}_{u,12}^{\max}:=(\hat{\pi}_{12}\otimes_{\max}\hat{\pi}_{12})\hat{\Delta}_{u,12}^{\max}\hat{\pi}_{12}^{-1}&\colon C^{*}(\mathbb{G}_{1})\otimes_{\max}C^{*}(\mathbb{G}_{2})\\
&\rightarrow\operatorname{M}((C^{*}(\mathbb{G}_{1})\otimes_{\max}C^{*}(\mathbb{G}_{2}))\otimes_{\max}(C^{*}(\mathbb{G}_{1})\otimes_{\max}C^{*}(\mathbb{G}_{2}))).\end{split}

On the other hand, we can also consider the quotient maps

\widetilde{\pi}_{i}=\hat{\pi}_{i,12}\circ\hat{\pi}^{-1}_{12}\colon C^{*}(\mathbb{G}_{1})\otimes_{\max}C^{*}(\mathbb{G}_{2})\rightarrow C^{*}(\mathbb{G}_{i}).

Lemma 5.24
.

We have

\widetilde{\pi}_{1}=\mathrm{id}_{C^{*}(\mathbb{G}_{1})}\times\hat{\varepsilon}_{2},\quad\widetilde{\pi}_{2}=\hat{\varepsilon}_{1}\times\mathrm{id}_{C^{*}(\mathbb{G}_{2})},\quad(\widetilde{\pi}_{1}\otimes_{\max}\widetilde{\pi}_{2})\widetilde{\Delta}_{u,12}^{\max}=\mathrm{id},\quad(\widetilde{\pi}_{1}\otimes\widetilde{\pi}_{2})\widetilde{\Delta}_{u,12}=\hat{\theta},

where
\hat{\theta}\colon C^{*}(\mathbb{G}_{1})\otimes_{\max}C^{*}(\mathbb{G}_{2})\rightarrow C^{*}(\mathbb{G}_{1})\otimes C^{*}(\mathbb{G}_{2})
is the canonical quotient map.

Proof.

We have

\widetilde{\pi}_{1}\hat{\pi}_{12}=\hat{\pi}_{1,12}=(\hat{\pi}_{1,12}\otimes_{\max}\hat{\varepsilon}_{12})\hat{\Delta}_{u,12}^{\max}=(\hat{\pi}_{1,12}\otimes_{\max}\hat{\varepsilon}_{2}\hat{\pi}_{2,12})\hat{\Delta}_{u,12}^{\max}=(\mathrm{id}\times\hat{\varepsilon}_{2})\hat{\pi}_{12}

where we used that universal linking quantum groupoids admit counits (
2.10
), which factorize through the counit of any quotient linking quantum groupoid. A similar computation works for
\widetilde{\pi}_{2}
. The next identity is just a formal computation:

(\widetilde{\pi}_{1}\otimes_{\max}\widetilde{\pi}_{2})\widetilde{\Delta}_{u,12}^{\max}=(\hat{\pi}_{1,12}\hat{\pi}_{12}^{-1}\otimes_{\max}\hat{\pi}_{2,12}\hat{\pi}_{12}^{-1})(\hat{\pi}_{12}\otimes_{\max}\hat{\pi}_{12})\hat{\Delta}_{u,12}^{\max}\hat{\pi}_{12}^{-1}=(\hat{\pi}_{1,12}\otimes_{\max}\hat{\pi}_{2,12})\hat{\Delta}_{u,12}^{\max}\hat{\pi}_{12}^{-1}=\mathrm{id}.

The last equality follows by composing both sides with
\hat{\theta}
.
∎

Lemma 5.25
.

For
i,j\in\{1,2\}
we have
(\hat{\varepsilon}_{1}\times\hat{\varepsilon}_{2})\hat{\pi}_{ij}=\hat{\varepsilon}_{ij}
.

Proof.

The claim is trivial for
i=j=2
. Consider now
i=1,j=2
. Denote by
\hat{\varepsilon}_{\mathbb{G}_{1}^{(2)},ij}
the counit of
\hat{\mathbb{G}}_{1}^{(2)}
. Using Lemma
5.24
we find

\hat{\varepsilon}_{1}\times\hat{\varepsilon}_{2}=\hat{\varepsilon}_{1}\widetilde{\pi}_{1}=\hat{\varepsilon}_{1}\hat{\pi}_{1,12}\hat{\pi}^{-1}_{12}=\hat{\varepsilon}_{\mathbb{G}_{1}^{(2)},12}\hat{\pi}_{1,12}\hat{\pi}^{-1}_{12}=\hat{\varepsilon}_{12}\hat{\pi}^{-1}_{12}

which proves
(\hat{\varepsilon}_{1}\times\hat{\varepsilon}_{2})\hat{\pi}_{12}=\hat{\varepsilon}_{12}
. The case
i=2,j=1
follows by taking adjoints at the matrix level. Finally, for
x\in C^{*}(\mathbb{G}_{11})
of the form
x=yz
with
y\in C^{*}(\mathbb{G}_{12}),z\in C^{*}(\mathbb{G}_{21})
we have

\hat{\varepsilon}_{11}(x)=\hat{\varepsilon}_{12}(y)\hat{\varepsilon}_{21}(z)=(\hat{\varepsilon}_{1}\times\hat{\varepsilon}_{2})(\hat{\pi}_{12}(y)\hat{\pi}_{21}(z))=(\hat{\varepsilon}_{1}\times\hat{\varepsilon})\hat{\pi}_{11}(x),

and the claim follows as
C^{*}(\mathbb{G}_{12})C^{*}(\mathbb{G}_{21})
is linearly dense in
C^{*}(\mathbb{G}_{11})
.
∎

Write now

\widetilde{\Omega}_{u}=\widetilde{\Delta}_{u,12}(1\otimes 1)\in\operatorname{M}((C^{*}(\mathbb{G}_{1})\otimes_{\max}C^{*}(\mathbb{G}_{2}))\otimes(C^{*}(\mathbb{G}_{1})\otimes_{\max}C^{*}(\mathbb{G}_{2}))).

Lemma 5.26
.

There exists a unitary skew bicharacter
\widetilde{\mathcal{X}}_{u}\in\operatorname{M}(C^{*}(\mathbb{G}_{1})\otimes C^{*}(\mathbb{G}_{2}))
such that

\widetilde{\Omega}_{u}=\widetilde{\mathcal{X}}_{u[32]}.

Proof.

First note that we can also write

\widetilde{\Delta}^{\max}_{u,12}(z)=(\hat{\pi}_{12}\otimes_{\max}\hat{\pi}_{12})\hat{\Delta}_{u,12}^{\max}(\hat{\pi}_{12}^{-1}(z))=\widetilde{\Omega}_{u}^{\max}\hat{\Delta}_{u,\otimes}^{\max}(z),\qquad\forall z\in C^{*}(\mathbb{G}),

for the unitary
2
-cocycle

\widetilde{\Omega}^{\max}_{u}=\widetilde{\Delta}_{u,12}^{\max}(1)\in\operatorname{M}((C^{*}(\mathbb{G}_{1})\otimes_{\max}C^{*}(\mathbb{G}_{2}))\otimes_{\max}(C^{*}(\mathbb{G}_{1})\otimes_{\max}C^{*}(\mathbb{G}_{2})))

lifting
\widetilde{\Omega}_{u}
. Below, we will do computations with
\widetilde{\Omega}_{u}^{\max}
, where we still will use the ‘placement’ indices, even though we are now interpreting them inside maximal tensor products. The
2
-cocycle condition of
\widetilde{\Omega}_{u}^{\max}
is with respect to
\hat{\Delta}_{\otimes,u}^{\max}
:

\widetilde{\Omega}_{u[1234]}^{\max}(\hat{\Delta}_{\otimes,u}^{\max}\otimes_{\max}\mathrm{id}\otimes_{\max}\mathrm{id})(\widetilde{\Omega}_{u}^{\max})=\widetilde{\Omega}_{u[3456]}^{\max}(\mathrm{id}\otimes_{\max}\mathrm{id}\otimes_{\max}\hat{\Delta}_{\otimes,u}^{\max})(\widetilde{\Omega}_{u}^{\max}).

(5.34)

Put now

A=(\mathrm{id}\otimes_{\max}\hat{\varepsilon}_{2}\otimes_{\max}\mathrm{id}\otimes_{\max}\mathrm{id})\widetilde{\Omega}_{u}^{\max},\qquad B=(\hat{\varepsilon}_{1}\otimes_{\max}\mathrm{id}\otimes_{\max}\mathrm{id}\otimes_{\max}\mathrm{id})\widetilde{\Omega}_{u}^{\max}.

Applying
\hat{\varepsilon}_{2}
on the second component of (
5.34
) leads to

A_{[123]}(\hat{\Delta}_{1,u}^{\max}\otimes_{\max}\mathrm{id}\otimes_{\max}\mathrm{id}\otimes_{\max}\mathrm{id})(\widetilde{\Omega}_{u}^{\max})=\widetilde{\Omega}_{u[2345]}^{\max}(\mathrm{id}\otimes_{\max}\hat{\Delta}_{\otimes,u}^{\max})(A).

(5.35)

But since

\begin{split}(\mathrm{id}\otimes_{\max}\hat{\varepsilon}_{1}\otimes_{\max}\mathrm{id})A=(\mathrm{id}\otimes_{\max}\hat{\varepsilon}_{2}\otimes_{\max}\hat{\varepsilon}_{1}\otimes_{\max}\mathrm{id})(\widetilde{\Omega}_{u}^{\max})=(\widetilde{\pi}_{1}\otimes_{\max}\widetilde{\pi}_{2})\widetilde{\Delta}_{u,12}^{\max}(1)=1\end{split}

by Lemma
5.24
, applying
(\mathrm{id}\otimes_{\max}\mathrm{id}\otimes_{\max}\hat{\varepsilon}_{2}\otimes_{\max}\hat{\varepsilon}_{1}\otimes_{\max}\mathrm{id})
to (
5.35
) leads to

(\mathrm{id}\otimes_{\max}\mathrm{id}\otimes_{\max}\hat{\varepsilon}_{2})(A)_{[12]}=A.

(5.36)

However, using Lemma
5.24
and the property that
\hat{\pi}_{1}
commutes with comultiplications

\begin{split}&\quad\;(\mathrm{id}\otimes_{\max}\mathrm{id}\otimes_{\max}\hat{\varepsilon}_{2})(A)=(\mathrm{id}\otimes_{\max}\hat{\varepsilon}_{2}\otimes_{\max}\mathrm{id}\otimes_{\max}\hat{\varepsilon}_{2})\widetilde{\Omega}^{\max}_{u}\\
&=(\mathrm{id}\otimes_{\max}\hat{\varepsilon}_{2}\otimes_{\max}\mathrm{id}\otimes_{\max}\hat{\varepsilon}_{2})(\hat{\pi}_{12}\otimes_{\max}\hat{\pi}_{12})\hat{\Delta}^{\max}_{u,12}(\hat{\pi}^{-1}_{12}(1))=(\tilde{\pi}_{1}\hat{\pi}_{12}\otimes_{\max}\tilde{\pi}_{1}\hat{\pi}_{12})\hat{\Delta}^{\max}_{u,12}(\hat{\pi}^{-1}_{12}(1))\\
&=(\hat{\pi}_{1,12}\otimes_{\max}\hat{\pi}_{1,12})\hat{\Delta}^{\max}_{u,12}(\hat{\pi}^{-1}_{12}(1))=\hat{\Delta}^{\max}_{1,u}(\hat{\pi}_{1,12}\hat{\pi}^{-1}_{12}(1))=\hat{\Delta}^{\max}_{1,u}(\tilde{\pi}_{1}(1))=\hat{\Delta}^{\max}_{1,u}((\mathrm{id}\times\hat{\varepsilon}_{2})(1))=1,\end{split}

(5.37)

which together with (
5.36
) gives
A=1
.
Applying
\mathrm{id}\otimes_{\max}\hat{\varepsilon}_{1}\otimes_{\max}\mathrm{id}^{\otimes_{\max}3}
on (
5.35
), we find

\widetilde{\Omega}_{u}^{\max}=B_{[234]}.

Put now

Z=(\mathrm{id}\otimes_{\max}\mathrm{id}\otimes_{\max}\hat{\varepsilon}_{2})(B)=(\hat{\varepsilon}_{1}\otimes_{\max}\mathrm{id}\otimes_{\max}\mathrm{id}\otimes_{\max}\hat{\varepsilon}_{2})(\widetilde{\Omega}_{u}^{\max})\in\operatorname{M}(C^{*}(\mathbb{G}_{2})\otimes_{\max}C^{*}(\mathbb{G}_{1})).

Then applying
\hat{\varepsilon}_{1}\otimes_{\max}\mathrm{id}\otimes_{\max}\mathrm{id}\otimes_{\max}\hat{\varepsilon}_{2}\otimes_{\max}\hat{\varepsilon}_{1}\otimes_{\max}\mathrm{id}
to (
5.34
), we find that

(\mathrm{id}\otimes_{\max}\mathrm{id}\otimes_{\max}\hat{\varepsilon}_{2})(B)_{[12]}(\mathrm{id}\otimes_{\max}\hat{\varepsilon}_{1}\otimes_{\max}\mathrm{id})(B)_{[23]}=B.

As in (
5.37
) we find that
(\mathrm{id}\otimes_{\max}\hat{\varepsilon}_{1}\otimes_{\max}\mathrm{id})(B)=1
and consequently

\widetilde{\Omega}_{u}^{\max}=B_{[234]}=Z_{[23]}.

Considering then once again the
2
-cocycle identity (
5.34
), applying resp.
\mathrm{id}^{\otimes_{\max}2}\otimes_{\max}\hat{\varepsilon}_{1}\otimes_{\max}\mathrm{id}^{\otimes_{\max}3}
or
\mathrm{id}^{\otimes_{\max}3}\otimes_{\max}\hat{\varepsilon}_{2}\otimes_{\max}\mathrm{id}^{\otimes_{\max}2}
leads to

(\hat{\Delta}_{2,u}^{\max}\otimes_{\max}\mathrm{id})Z=Z_{[23]}Z_{[13]},\qquad(\mathrm{id}\otimes_{\max}\hat{\Delta}_{1,u}^{\max})Z=Z_{[12]}Z_{[13]}.

If we now write
\widetilde{\mathcal{X}}_{u}
for the image of
Z_{[21]}
under the quotient map

C^{*}(\mathbb{G}_{1})\otimes_{\max}C^{*}(\mathbb{G}_{2})\rightarrow C^{*}(\mathbb{G}_{1})\otimes C^{*}(\mathbb{G}_{2}),

we find the statement of the lemma.
∎

To proceed, we will need to modify the isomorphism
\hat{\pi}
. In order to do this, first we establish a general lemma concerning cocycles.

Lemma 5.27
.

Let
\mathbb{H}_{1},\mathbb{H}_{2},\mathbb{H}=\mathbb{H}_{1}\times\mathbb{H}_{2}
be locally compact quantum groups and
\mathfrak{X},\widetilde{\mathfrak{X}}\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{H}_{1})\bar{\otimes}L^{\infty}(\mathbb{H}_{2})
skew bicharacters. Denote the coproduct of
\mathbb{H}
by
\Delta_{\otimes}
. Assume that cocyles
\mathfrak{X}_{[32]},\widetilde{\mathfrak{X}}_{[32]}\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{H})\bar{\otimes}L^{\infty}(\mathbb{H})
are coboundary equivalent, i.e.

\mathfrak{X}_{[32]}\Delta_{\otimes}(Z)=(Z\otimes Z)\widetilde{\mathfrak{X}}_{[32]}.

(5.38)

for a unitary
Z\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{H})
. Then, there exist group-like unitaries
v_{1}\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{H}_{1}),v_{2}\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{H}_{2})
such that

Z=v_{1}\otimes v_{2},\qquad\mathfrak{X}=(v_{1}\otimes v_{2})\widetilde{\mathfrak{X}}(v_{1}^{*}\otimes v_{2}^{*}).

Proof.

We will write
\Delta_{i}
for the coproduct of
\mathbb{H}_{i}\,(i\in\{1,2\})
. Applying the flip at legs
2,3
of (
5.38
) gives

\mathfrak{X}_{[23]}(\Delta_{1}\otimes\Delta_{2})(Z)=Z_{[13]}Z_{[24]}\widetilde{\mathfrak{X}}_{[23]}.

(5.39)

If we apply
\Delta_{1}
to the first leg of (
5.39
), we get

\mathfrak{X}_{[34]}(\Delta_{1}^{(2)}\otimes\Delta_{2})(Z)=(\Delta_{1}\otimes\mathrm{id})(Z)_{[124]}Z_{[35]}\widetilde{\mathfrak{X}}_{[34]},

(5.40)

while if we apply
\Delta_{1}
to the second leg of (
5.39
), we obtain

\mathfrak{X}_{[24]}\mathfrak{X}_{[34]}(\Delta_{1}^{(2)}\otimes\Delta_{2})(Z)=Z_{[14]}(\Delta_{1}\otimes\mathrm{id})(Z)_{[235]}\widetilde{\mathfrak{X}}_{[24]}\widetilde{\mathfrak{X}}_{[34]}.

(5.41)

Combining (
5.40
) and (
5.41
) and cancelling
\widetilde{\mathfrak{X}}_{[34]}
gives

\mathfrak{X}_{[24]}(\Delta_{1}\otimes\mathrm{id})(Z)_{[124]}Z_{[35]}=Z_{[14]}(\Delta_{1}\otimes\mathrm{id})(Z)_{[235]}\widetilde{\mathfrak{X}}_{[24]}.

(5.42)

Similarly, applying
\Delta_{2}
to the third and fourth leg of (
5.39
) leads to

\mathfrak{X}_{[24]}Z_{[13]}(\mathrm{id}\otimes\Delta_{2})(Z)_{[245]}=(\mathrm{id}\otimes\Delta_{2})(Z)_{[134]}Z_{[25]}\widetilde{\mathfrak{X}}_{[24]}.

(5.43)

From equation (
5.43
) we deduce

(\mathrm{id}\otimes\Delta_{2})(Z)_{[134]}=Z_{[13]}\mathfrak{X}_{[24]}(\mathrm{id}\otimes\Delta_{2})(Z)_{[245]}\widetilde{\mathfrak{X}}_{[24]}^{*}Z_{[25]}^{*}.

Apply the flip
a\otimes b\otimes c\otimes d\otimes e\mapsto a\otimes c\otimes d\otimes b\otimes e
to get

(\mathrm{id}\otimes\Delta_{2})(Z)_{[123]}=Z_{[12]}\mathfrak{X}_{[43]}(\mathrm{id}\otimes\Delta_{2})(Z)_{[435]}\widetilde{\mathfrak{X}}_{[43]}^{*}Z_{[45]}^{*}.

It follows that

(\mathrm{id}\otimes\Delta_{2})(Z)=Z\otimes v_{2},

(5.44)

where
v_{2}\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{H}_{2})
is a unitary defined via
v_{2[123]}=\mathfrak{X}_{[21]}(\mathrm{id}\otimes\Delta_{2})(Z)_{[213]}\widetilde{\mathfrak{X}}^{*}_{[21]}Z^{*}_{[23]}
. Applying
\Delta_{2}
to the third leg of (
5.44
) gives

Z\otimes\Delta_{2}(v_{2})=(\mathrm{id}\otimes\Delta_{2}^{(2)})(Z)=(\mathrm{id}\otimes\Delta_{2})(Z)\otimes v_{2}=Z\otimes v_{2}\otimes v_{2},

hence
v_{2}
is a group-like unitary.

Observe now that

(\mathrm{id}\otimes\Delta_{2})(Z(1\otimes v_{2}^{*}))=(Z\otimes v_{2})(1\otimes v_{2}^{*}\otimes v_{2}^{*})=Z(1\otimes v_{2}^{*})\otimes 1.

Hence it follows from the “invariants are constant” technique
[
Daw12
]
*Theorem 2.1 that
Z(1\otimes v_{2}^{*})=v_{1}\otimes 1
for a unitary
v_{1}\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{H}_{1})
. Consequently

Z=v_{1}\otimes v_{2}.

(5.45)

We can rewrite (
5.42
) as

\mathfrak{X}_{[24]}\Delta_{1}(v_{1})_{[12]}v_{2[4]}v_{1[3]}v_{2[5]}=v_{1[1]}v_{2[4]}\Delta_{1}(v_{1})_{[23]}v_{2[5]}\widetilde{\mathfrak{X}}_{[24]}.

Consequently, similarly as above we can introduce a unitary
\tilde{v}_{1}\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{H}_{1})
such that

\Delta_{1}(v_{1})=v_{1}\otimes\tilde{v}_{1}.

(5.46)

As with
v_{2}
and (
5.44
), we deduce from here that
\tilde{v}_{1}
is a group-like unitary. Using again equation (
5.39
) and equation (
5.46
) gives

\mathfrak{X}_{[23]}v_{1[1]}\tilde{v}_{1[2]}v_{2[3]}v_{2[4]}=v_{1[1]}v_{2[3]}v_{1[2]}v_{2[4]}\widetilde{\mathfrak{X}}_{[23]}

which simplifies to

\mathfrak{X}(\tilde{v}_{1}\otimes v_{2})=(v_{1}\otimes v_{2})\widetilde{\mathfrak{X}}.

(5.47)

Using that
\tilde{v}_{1}
is a group-like, applying
\Delta_{1}
to the first leg of (
5.47
) gives

(\Delta_{1}(v_{1})\otimes v_{2})\widetilde{\mathfrak{X}}_{[13]}\widetilde{\mathfrak{X}}_{[23]}=\mathfrak{X}_{[13]}\mathfrak{X}_{[23]}(\tilde{v}_{1}\otimes\tilde{v}_{1}\otimes v_{2})=\mathfrak{X}_{[13]}(\tilde{v}_{1}\otimes v_{1}\otimes v_{2})\widetilde{\mathfrak{X}}_{[23]}=(v_{1}\otimes v_{1}\otimes v_{2})\widetilde{\mathfrak{X}}_{[13]}\widetilde{\mathfrak{X}}_{[23]},

and we conclude that
v_{1}
is group-like as well. Equation (
5.46
) implies that
\tilde{v}_{1}=v_{1}
and (
5.47
) ends the proof.
∎

Let us go back to our situation. Let
\widetilde{\mathcal{X}}_{u}\in\operatorname{M}(C^{*}(\mathbb{G}_{1})\otimes C^{*}(\mathbb{G}_{2}))
be the skew bicharacter from Lemma
5.26
, and let
\widetilde{\mathcal{X}}
be its image in
\operatorname{M}(C^{*}_{\mathrm{red}}(\mathbb{G}_{1})\otimes C^{*}_{\mathrm{red}}(\mathbb{G}_{2}))
.

Lemma 5.28
.

The cocycles
\hat{\Omega}=\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{[32]}
and
\widetilde{\Omega}=\widetilde{\mathcal{X}}_{[32]}
are coboundary equivalent. More precisely, we have

\hat{\Omega}\hat{\Delta}_{\otimes}(Z)=(Z\otimes Z)\widetilde{\Omega}

for
Z=\hat{\pi}_{\mathrm{red},12}\hat{\pi}^{-1}_{12}(1\otimes 1)\in\hat{M}_{1}\bar{\otimes}\hat{M}_{2}
.

Proof.

Observe that
Z^{*}\hat{\pi}_{\mathrm{red},12}(\hat{\pi}_{12}^{-1}(x))=\hat{\pi}_{\mathrm{red}}(x)
for
x\in C^{*}(\mathbb{G})
. Using this, we have

\begin{split}&\quad\;\widetilde{\Omega}=(\hat{\pi}_{\mathrm{red}}\otimes\hat{\pi}_{\mathrm{red}})(\widetilde{\mathcal{X}}_{u[32]})=(\hat{\pi}_{\mathrm{red}}\otimes\hat{\pi}_{\mathrm{red}})\widetilde{\Delta}_{u,12}(1\otimes 1)=(Z^{*}\otimes Z^{*})(\hat{\pi}_{\mathrm{red},12}\hat{\pi}^{-1}_{12}\otimes\hat{\pi}_{\mathrm{red},12}\hat{\pi}^{-1}_{12})\widetilde{\Delta}_{u,12}(1\otimes 1)\\
&=(Z^{*}\otimes Z^{*})(\hat{\pi}_{\mathrm{red},12}\otimes\hat{\pi}_{\mathrm{red},12})\hat{\Delta}_{u,12}\hat{\pi}^{-1}_{12}(1\otimes 1)=(Z^{*}\otimes Z^{*})\hat{\Delta}_{12}(\hat{\pi}_{\mathrm{red},12}\hat{\pi}^{-1}_{12}(1\otimes 1))\\
&=(Z^{*}\otimes Z^{*})\hat{\Omega}\hat{\Delta}_{\otimes}(\hat{\pi}_{\mathrm{red},12}\hat{\pi}^{-1}_{12}(1\otimes 1))=(Z^{*}\otimes Z^{*})\hat{\Omega}\hat{\Delta}_{\otimes}(Z).\end{split}

∎

If we apply Lemma
5.27
and Lemma
5.28
to the bicharacters
\widehat{\mathcal{X}},\widetilde{\mathcal{X}}
, we obtain group-like unitaries
v_{1}\in\hat{M}_{1},v_{2}\in\hat{M}_{2}
such that
Z=v_{1}\otimes v_{2}
and
\widehat{\mathcal{X}}=(v_{1}\otimes v_{2})\widetilde{\mathcal{X}}(v_{1}^{*}\otimes v_{2}^{*})
. These unitaries
v_{1},v_{2}
can be seen as one-dimensional unitary representations, hence they lift uniquely to group-like unitaries
v_{1}^{u}\in\operatorname{M}(C^{*}(\mathbb{G}_{1})),v_{2}^{u}\in\operatorname{M}(C^{*}(\mathbb{G}_{2}))
. It then easily follows that

\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{u}=(v_{1}^{u}\otimes v_{2}^{u})\widetilde{\mathcal{X}}_{u}(v_{1}^{u*}\otimes v_{2}^{u*}).

Next, we correct the isomorphism
\hat{\pi}\colon C^{*}(\mathscr{G})\rightarrow\begin{pmatrix}C^{*}(\mathbb{G})&C^{*}(\mathbb{G})\\
C^{*}(\mathbb{G})&C^{*}(\mathbb{G})\end{pmatrix}
by twisting it with an inner automorphism:

\underline{\hat{\pi}}=\operatorname{Ad}\Bigl(\begin{pmatrix}v_{1}^{u}\otimes v_{2}^{u}&0\\
0&1\otimes 1\end{pmatrix}\Bigr)\hat{\pi}\colon C^{*}(\mathscr{G})\rightarrow\begin{pmatrix}C^{*}(\mathbb{G})&C^{*}(\mathbb{G})\\
C^{*}(\mathbb{G})&C^{*}(\mathbb{G})\end{pmatrix}.

(5.48)

More explicitely,

\underline{\hat{\pi}}\Bigl(\,\begin{pmatrix}a&b\\
c&d\end{pmatrix}\,\Bigr)=\begin{pmatrix}\underline{\hat{\pi}}_{11}(a)&\underline{\hat{\pi}}_{12}(b)\\
\underline{\hat{\pi}}_{21}(c)&\underline{\hat{\pi}}_{22}(d)\end{pmatrix}=\begin{pmatrix}(v^{u}_{1}\otimes v_{2}^{u})\hat{\pi}_{11}(a)(v_{1}^{u}\otimes v_{2}^{u})^{*}&(v^{u}_{1}\otimes v_{2}^{u})\hat{\pi}_{12}(b)\\
\hat{\pi}_{21}(c)(v_{1}^{u}\otimes v_{2}^{u})^{*}&\hat{\pi}_{22}(d)\end{pmatrix}

(5.49)

for
\begin{pmatrix}a&b\\
c&d\end{pmatrix}\in C^{*}(\mathscr{G})
. The following lemma now finishes the proof of Theorem
5.2
.

Lemma 5.29
.

Via the isomorphism
\underline{\hat{\pi}}
, the element
\hat{\Omega}_{u}
is a universal lift of
\hat{\Omega}
in the sense of Definition
4.3
.

Proof.

We first claim that
\mathscr{G}
has trivial universal linking C
∗
-algebra with isomorphism

\underline{\hat{\pi}}\colon C^{*}(\mathscr{G})\rightarrow\begin{pmatrix}\hat{M}_{u}&\hat{M}_{u}\\
\hat{M}_{u}&\hat{M}_{u}\end{pmatrix}

(see Definition
4.2
). It is clear that
\underline{\hat{\pi}}
preserves the matrix structure, and is the identity on the lower right hand corner. Next, we have for
x\in C^{*}(\mathbb{G}_{ij})

\varepsilon_{\hat{M}_{u}}\underline{\hat{\pi}}_{ij}(x)=\varepsilon_{\hat{M}_{u}}\hat{\pi}_{ij}(x)=\hat{\varepsilon}_{ij}(x)

using that
\varepsilon_{\hat{M}_{u}}(v_{1}^{u}\otimes v_{2}^{u})=1
in the first equality (because
v_{i}^{u}
is a group-like unitary) and Lemma
5.25
. Finally, we need to check that
\underline{\hat{\pi}}
respects reducing maps. Take
y\in C^{*}(\mathbb{G}_{12})
. We have

\begin{split}&\quad\;\hat{\pi}_{\mathrm{red},12}(y)=\hat{\pi}_{\mathrm{red},12}\hat{\pi}^{-1}_{12}\hat{\pi}_{12}(y)=\hat{\pi}_{\mathrm{red},12}(\hat{\pi}^{-1}_{12}(1\otimes 1)\hat{\pi}^{-1}_{22}\hat{\pi}_{12}(y))=\hat{\pi}_{\mathrm{red},12}(\hat{\pi}^{-1}_{12}(1\otimes 1)\hat{\pi}_{12}(y))\\
&=\hat{\pi}_{\mathrm{red},12}\hat{\pi}^{-1}_{12}(1\otimes 1)\hat{\pi}_{\mathrm{red},22}\hat{\pi}_{12}(y)=(v_{1}\otimes v_{2})\hat{\pi}_{\mathrm{red}}\hat{\pi}_{12}(y)=\hat{\pi}_{\mathrm{red}}((v_{1}^{u}\otimes v_{2}^{u})\hat{\pi}_{12}(y))=\hat{\pi}_{\mathrm{red}}\underline{\hat{\pi}}_{12}(y)\end{split}

using Lemma
5.28
and the explicit formula (
5.49
). The claim now follows by a reasoning similar to the one presented in the proof of Lemma
5.25
.

To end the proof, we show that

(\underline{\hat{\pi}}_{12}\otimes\underline{\hat{\pi}}_{12})\hat{\Delta}^{u}_{12}\underline{\hat{\pi}}_{12}^{-1}(1\otimes 1)=\hat{\Omega}_{u}.

(5.50)

Indeed, we have

\begin{split}&\quad\;(\underline{\hat{\pi}}_{12}\otimes\underline{\hat{\pi}}_{12})\hat{\Delta}^{u}_{12}\underline{\hat{\pi}}_{12}^{-1}(1\otimes 1)=(v_{1}^{u}\otimes v_{2}^{u}\otimes v_{1}^{u}\otimes v_{2}^{u})(\hat{\pi}_{12}\otimes\hat{\pi}_{12})\hat{\Delta}^{u}_{12}\hat{\pi}^{-1}_{12}(v_{1}^{u*}\otimes v_{2}^{u*})\\
&=(v_{1}^{u}\otimes v_{2}^{u}\otimes v_{1}^{u}\otimes v_{2}^{u})\widetilde{\Delta}_{u,12}(v_{1}^{u*}\otimes v_{2}^{u*})=(v_{1}^{u}\otimes v_{2}^{u}\otimes v_{1}^{u}\otimes v_{2}^{u})\widetilde{\Delta}_{u,12}(1\otimes 1)\widetilde{\Delta}_{u,22}(v_{1}^{u*}\otimes v_{2}^{u*})\\
&=(v_{1}^{u}\otimes v_{2}^{u}\otimes v_{1}^{u}\otimes v_{2}^{u})\widetilde{\Omega}_{u}\hat{\Delta}_{u,22}(v_{1}^{u*}\otimes v_{2}^{u*})=(v_{1}^{u}\otimes v_{2}^{u}\otimes v_{1}^{u}\otimes v_{2}^{u})\widetilde{\mathcal{X}}_{u[32]}(v_{1}^{u*}\otimes v_{2}^{u*}\otimes v_{1}^{u*}\otimes v_{2}^{u*})\\
&=\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{u[32]}=\hat{\Omega}_{u}.\end{split}

∎

6.
